 3.3.  Lowest weight modules 

 
 Let  ( M , ( − , − ) ) (M,(-,-))  be a monoid with bilinear form. We assume that the natural map  M → M ^ M\rightarrow\widehat{M}  is an embedding into a lattice. We let  P : Φ M , ( − , − ) + → 𝐍 [ t ± 1 / 2 ] P\colon\Phi^{+}_{M,(-,-)}\rightarrow\mathbf{N}[t^{\pm 1/2}]  be a weight function. We let  𝔤 ≔ 𝔤 M , ( − , − ) , P \mathfrak{g}\coloneqq\mathfrak{g}_{M,(-,-),P}  and  𝔟 − ≔ 𝔫 M , ( − , − ) , P − ⊕ 𝔥 M \mathfrak{b}^{-}\coloneqq\mathfrak{n}^{-}_{M,(-,-),P}\oplus\mathfrak{h}_{M} . For  𝐟 ∈ Hom ⁡ ( M ^ , 𝐙 ) \mathbf{f}\in\operatorname{Hom}(\widehat{M},\mathbf{Z}) , we define the  Verma module   V 𝔤 , 𝐟 V_{\mathfrak{g},\mathbf{f}}  as follows: 
 

 
 
 V 𝔤 , 𝐟 ≔ 𝐔 ⁡ ( 𝔤 ) ⊗ 𝐔 ⁡ ( 𝔟 − ) 𝐂 , V_{\mathfrak{g},\mathbf{f}}\coloneqq\UEA(\mathfrak{g})\otimes_{\UEA(\mathfrak{b}^{-})}\mathbf{C}, 
 
 
 where  𝐂 \mathbf{C}  is a  𝐔 ⁡ ( 𝔟 − ) \UEA(\mathfrak{b}^{-}) -module via the quotient  𝐔 ⁡ ( 𝔟 − ) → 𝐔 ⁡ ( 𝔥 ) \UEA(\mathfrak{b}^{-})\rightarrow\UEA(\mathfrak{h})  and the action of  𝔥 \mathfrak{h}  on  𝐂 \mathbf{C}  is given by  h m ⋅ 1 ≔ 𝐟 ⁡ ( m ) h_{m}\cdot 1\coloneqq\mathbf{f}(m)  for  m ∈ M m\in M . We let  v ≔ 1 ⊗ 1 ∈ V 𝔤 , 𝐟 v\coloneqq 1\otimes 1\in V_{\mathfrak{g},\mathbf{f}} . There is a unique maximal submodule of  V 𝔤 , 𝐟 V_{\mathfrak{g},\mathbf{f}}  not containing  v v . We denote by  L 𝔤 , 𝐟 L_{\mathfrak{g},\mathbf{f}}  (or  L 𝐟 L_{\mathbf{f}}  for short) the quotient of  V 𝔤 , 𝐟 V_{\mathfrak{g},\mathbf{f}}  by this submodule. It is a simple  𝔤 \mathfrak{g} -module. It is called the  simple lowest weight   𝔤 \mathfrak{g} -module of weight  𝐟 \mathbf{f} . 
 
 
 
 3.4.  Cartan involution 

 
 The  Cartan involution  of  𝔤 M , ( − , − ) , P \mathfrak{g}_{M,(-,-),P}  is the unique automorphism of Lie algebras  w : 𝔤 M , ( − , − ) , P → 𝔤 M , ( − , − ) , P w\colon\mathfrak{g}_{M,(-,-),P}\rightarrow\mathfrak{g}_{M,(-,-),P}  such that for any  m ∈ Φ M , ( − , − ) + m\in\Phi^{+}_{M,(-,-)} ,  j ∈ 𝐙 j\in\mathbf{Z} ,  1 ⩽ l ⩽ p m , j 1\leqslant l\leqslant p_{m,j} , 
 

 
 
 w ⁡ ( e m , j , l ) = − f m , j , l \displaystyle w(e_{m,j,l})=-f_{m,j,l} 
 
 
 
 w ⁡ ( f m , j , l ) = − e m , j , l \displaystyle w(f_{m,j,l})=-e_{m,j,l} 
 
 
 
 w ⁡ ( h m ) = − h m . \displaystyle w(h_{m})=-h_{m}. 
 
 
 It is described for Kac–Moody algebras in  [ Kac90 , §1.3] . For generalised Kac–Moody algebras, its definition appears in  [ Bor88 , §1] . 
 
 
 
 3.5.  Generalised half Kac–Moody (Lie) algebras in tensor categories of mixed Hodge modules or perverse sheaves 

 
 We let  ( ℳ , ⊕ ) (\mathcal{M},\oplus)  be a commutative monoid in the category of complex schemes. We allow  ℳ \mathcal{M}  to have infinitely many connected components. We assume that each connected component of  ℳ \mathcal{M}  is a finite type separated scheme and that the sum  ⊕ : ℳ × ℳ → ℳ \oplus\colon\mathcal{M}\times\mathcal{M}\rightarrow\mathcal{M}  is a finite morphism. The formula  ℱ ⊡ 𝒢 ≔ ⊕ ∗ ( ℱ ⊠ 𝒢 ) \mathscr{F}\boxdot\mathscr{G}\coloneqq\oplus_{*}(\mathscr{F}\boxtimes\mathscr{G})  defines symmetric monoidal structures on  𝒟 c + ​ ( ℳ ) \mathcal{D}^{+}_{\mathrm{c}}(\mathcal{M})  and  𝒟 + ​ ( MHM ​ ( ℳ ) ) \mathcal{D}^{+}(\mathrm{MHM}(\mathcal{M})) , and by  t t -exactness of  ⊕ ∗ \oplus_{*} , also on  Perv ⁡ ( ℳ ) \operatorname{Perv}(\mathcal{M})  and  MHM ⁡ ( ℳ ) \mathrm{MHM}(\mathcal{M}) . We let  T ℱ , 𝒢 : ℱ ⊡ 𝒢 → ≅ 𝒢 ⊡ ℱ T_{\mathscr{F},\mathscr{G}}\colon\mathscr{F}\boxdot\mathscr{G}\xrightarrow{\cong}\mathscr{G}\boxdot\mathscr{F}  be the natural isomorphisms for objects  ℱ , 𝒢 \mathscr{F},\mathscr{G}  of  𝒟 c b ​ ( ℳ ) \mathcal{D}^{\mathrm{b}}_{\mathrm{c}}(\mathcal{M})  or  𝒟 + ​ ( MHM ​ ( ℳ ) ) \mathcal{D}^{+}(\mathrm{MHM}(\mathcal{M})) . See  [ MSS11 ]  for details. In this section, we define (positive halves of) generalised Kac–Moody Lie algebras in the tensor category  MHM ⁡ ( ℳ ) \mathrm{MHM}(\mathcal{M}) . The same formulas provide a definition for generalised half Kac–Moody Lie algebras in  Perv ⁡ ( ℳ ) \operatorname{Perv}(\mathcal{M}) ; we do not make them explicit (just apply the functor  𝐫𝐚𝐭 : MHM ⁡ ( ℳ ) → Perv ⁡ ( ℳ ) \mathbf{rat}\colon\mathrm{MHM}(\mathcal{M})\rightarrow\operatorname{Perv}(\mathcal{M}) ). 
 
 
 3.5.1.  Free algebras and free Lie algebras 

 
 Let  π 0 ​ ( ℳ ) \pi_{0}(\mathcal{M})  be the monoid of connected components of  ℳ \mathcal{M} . We fix a morphism of monoids  cl : π 0 ​ ( ℳ ) → M \mathrm{cl}\colon\pi_{0}(\mathcal{M})\rightarrow M  with finite fibres, where  M M  satisfies the condition that the natural map  M → M ^ M\rightarrow\widehat{M}  is an injection into a lattice. For  m ∈ M m\in M  we write  ℳ m ⊆ ℳ \mathcal{M}_{m}\subseteq\mathcal{M}  for the union of connected components in  cl − 1 ​ ( m ) \mathrm{cl}^{-1}(m) . Let  ( − , − ) : M × M → 𝐙 (-,-)\colon M\times M\rightarrow\mathbf{Z}  be a bilinear form. The sets of primitive positive roots  Σ M , ( − , − ) \Sigma_{M,(-,-)}  and simple positive roots  Φ M , ( − , − ) + \Phi_{M,(-,-)}^{+}  are defined in § 3.1 . We let  A M , ( − , − ) A_{M,(-,-)}  be the Cartan matrix, as defined in § 3.2 . We assume that it satisfies the assumptions of § 3.2 :  A M , ( − , − ) A_{M,(-,-)}  is symmetric,  a m , m ∈ 2 ​ 𝐙 ⩽ 1 a_{m,m}\in 2\mathbf{Z}_{\leqslant 1}  for all  m m , and  a m , n ⩽ 0 a_{m,n}\leqslant 0  if  a m , m = 2 a_{m,m}=2 . We also assume that the fibres of the sum map  + : M × M → M +\colon M\times M\rightarrow M  are finite. This condition will ensure that the restriction to various connected components of  ℳ \mathcal{M}  of the constructions are actual complexes of mixed Hodge modules and not infinite direct sums of such (which would require some completion of the derived category of mixed Hodge modules). 
 
 
 We assume that  ℳ m ≅ pt \mathcal{M}_{m}\cong\mathrm{pt}  for  m ∈ Φ M , ( − , − ) + , m\in\Phi_{M,(-,-)}^{+,\real} . Let  𝒢 ¯ m ∈ MHM ⁡ ( ℳ m ) \underline{\mathscr{G}}_{m}\in\mathrm{MHM}(\mathcal{M}_{m})  for  m ∈ Φ M , ( − , − ) + m\in\Phi_{M,(-,-)}^{+} . We assume that  𝒢 ¯ m = 𝐐 ¯ ℳ m \underline{\mathscr{G}}_{m}=\underline{\mathbf{Q}}_{\mathcal{M}_{m}}  for  m ∈ Φ M , ( − , − ) + , m\in\Phi_{M,(-,-)}^{+,\real} . We let  𝒢 ¯ = ⨁ m ∈ Φ M , ( − , − ) + 𝒢 ¯ m \underline{\mathscr{G}}=\bigoplus_{m\in\Phi^{+}_{M,(-,-)}}\underline{\mathscr{G}}_{m}  and consider 
 

 
 
 Free ⊡ -Alg ⁡ ( 𝒢 ¯ ) \displaystyle\operatorname{Free}_{\boxdot\sAlg}(\underline{\mathscr{G}}) 
 ≔ Free ⊡ -Alg ⁡ ( ⨁ m ∈ Φ M , ( − , − ) + 𝒢 ¯ m ) \displaystyle\coloneqq\operatorname{Free}_{\boxdot\sAlg}\left(\bigoplus_{m\in\Phi_{M,(-,-)}^{+}}\underline{\mathscr{G}}_{m}\right) 
 
 
 
 
 = ⨁ r ⩾ 0 ⨁ m 1 , … , m r ∈ Φ M , ( − , − ) + ⊕ ∗ ( 𝒢 ¯ m 1 ⊠ … ⊠ 𝒢 ¯ m r ) \displaystyle=\bigoplus_{r\geqslant 0}\bigoplus_{m_{1},\ldots,m_{r}\in\Phi_{M,(-,-)}^{+}}\oplus_{*}(\underline{\mathscr{G}}_{m_{1}}\boxtimes\ldots\boxtimes\underline{\mathscr{G}}_{m_{r}}) 
 
 
 
 
 = ⨁ r ⩾ 0 ⊕ ∗ ( 𝒢 ¯ ⊠ r ) . \displaystyle=\bigoplus_{r\geqslant 0}\oplus_{*}(\underline{\mathscr{G}}^{\boxtimes r}). 
 
 
 The finiteness of  ⊕ \oplus  ensures that  Free ⊡ -Alg ⁡ ( 𝒢 ¯ ) \operatorname{Free}_{\boxdot\sAlg}(\underline{\mathscr{G}})  is a mixed Hodge module on  ℳ \mathcal{M} , i.e. the restriction to each connected component of  ℳ \mathcal{M}  is a genuine mixed Hodge module. The mixed Hodge module  Free ⊡ -Alg ⁡ ( 𝒢 ¯ ) \operatorname{Free}_{\boxdot\sAlg}(\underline{\mathscr{G}})  is an algebra object, with the multiplication  𝔪 : Free ⊡ -Alg ⁡ ( 𝒢 ¯ ) ⊡ Free ⊡ -Alg ⁡ ( 𝒢 ¯ ) → Free ⊡ -Alg ⁡ ( 𝒢 ¯ ) \mathfrak{m}\colon\operatorname{Free}_{\boxdot\sAlg}(\underline{\mathscr{G}})\boxdot\operatorname{Free}_{\boxdot\sAlg}(\underline{\mathscr{G}})\rightarrow\operatorname{Free}_{\boxdot\sAlg}(\underline{\mathscr{G}})  obtained via the canonical identifications 
 

 
 
 ⊕ ∗ ( ( ⊕ ∗ 𝒢 ¯ ⊠ r ) ⊠ ( ⊕ ∗ 𝒢 ¯ ⊠ s ) ) ≃ ⊕ ∗ 𝒢 ¯ ⊠ ( r + s ) . \oplus_{*}\!\left((\oplus_{*}\underline{\mathscr{G}}^{\boxtimes r})\boxtimes(\oplus_{*}\underline{\mathscr{G}}^{\boxtimes s})\right)\simeq\oplus_{*}\underline{\mathscr{G}}^{\boxtimes(r+s)}. 
 
 
 For  m ∈ Φ M , ( − , − ) + m\in\Phi_{M,(-,-)}^{+} , we have by definition a natural monomorphism  𝒢 ¯ m → Free ⊡ -Alg ⁡ ( 𝒢 ¯ ) \underline{\mathscr{G}}_{m}\rightarrow\operatorname{Free}_{\boxdot\sAlg}(\underline{\mathscr{G}}) . Moreover, the commutator  𝔪 − 𝔪 ∘ T Free ⊡ -Alg ⁡ ( 𝒢 ¯ ) , Free ⊡ -Alg ⁡ ( 𝒢 ¯ ) \mathfrak{m}-\mathfrak{m}\circ T_{\operatorname{Free}_{\boxdot\sAlg}(\underline{\mathscr{G}}),\operatorname{Free}_{\boxdot\sAlg}(\underline{\mathscr{G}})}  gives  Free ⊡ -Alg ⁡ ( 𝒢 ¯ ) \operatorname{Free}_{\boxdot\sAlg}(\underline{\mathscr{G}})  a Lie algebra structure. We let  Free ⊡ -Lie ⁡ ( 𝒢 ¯ ) \operatorname{Free}_{\boxdot\sLie}(\underline{\mathscr{G}})  be the Lie subalgebra of  Free ⊡ -Alg ⁡ ( 𝒢 ¯ ) \operatorname{Free}_{\boxdot\sAlg}(\underline{\mathscr{G}})  generated by  𝒢 ¯ \underline{\mathscr{G}} . We denote by  [ − , − ] [-,-]  its Lie bracket. It is referred to as  the free Lie algebra generated by  𝒢 ¯ \underline{\mathscr{G}} . 
 
 
 For morphisms of mixed Hodge modules  ℱ ¯ → Free ⊡ -Lie ⁡ ( 𝒢 ¯ ) \underline{\mathscr{F}}\rightarrow\operatorname{Free}_{\boxdot\sLie}(\underline{\mathscr{G}})  and  ℋ ¯ → Free ⊡ -Lie ⁡ ( 𝒢 ¯ ) \underline{\mathscr{H}}\rightarrow\operatorname{Free}_{\boxdot\sLie}(\underline{\mathscr{G}}) , we have a morphism  ℱ ¯ ⊠ ℋ ¯ → Free ⊡ -Lie ⁡ ( 𝒢 ¯ ) ⊠ Free ⊡ -Lie ⁡ ( 𝒢 ¯ ) \underline{\mathscr{F}}\boxtimes\underline{\mathscr{H}}\rightarrow\operatorname{Free}_{\boxdot\sLie}(\underline{\mathscr{G}})\boxtimes\operatorname{Free}_{\boxdot\sLie}(\underline{\mathscr{G}})  and hence, by applying  ⊕ ∗ \oplus_{*} , a morphism  ℱ ¯ ⊡ ℋ ¯ → Free ⊡ -Lie ⁡ ( 𝒢 ¯ ) ⊡ Free ⊡ -Lie ⁡ ( 𝒢 ¯ ) \underline{\mathscr{F}}\boxdot\underline{\mathscr{H}}\rightarrow\operatorname{Free}_{\boxdot\sLie}(\underline{\mathscr{G}})\boxdot\operatorname{Free}_{\boxdot\sLie}(\underline{\mathscr{G}}) . We denote by  ad ⁡ ( ℱ ¯ ) ​ ( ℋ ¯ ) ⊆ Free ⊡ -Lie ⁡ ( 𝒢 ¯ ) \operatorname{ad}(\underline{\mathscr{F}})(\underline{\mathscr{H}})\subseteq\operatorname{Free}_{\boxdot\sLie}(\underline{\mathscr{G}})  the image of the composition of this morphism with the Lie bracket  [ − , − ] [-,-] . We can also see  ad ⁡ ( ℱ ¯ ) ​ ( ℋ ¯ ) \operatorname{ad}(\underline{\mathscr{F}})(\underline{\mathscr{H}})  as a subobject of  Free ⊡ -Alg ⁡ ( 𝒢 ¯ ) \operatorname{Free}_{\boxdot\sAlg}(\underline{\mathscr{G}})  thanks to the inclusion  Free ⊡ -Lie ⁡ ( 𝒢 ¯ ) → Free ⊡ -Alg ⁡ ( 𝒢 ¯ ) \operatorname{Free}_{\boxdot\sLie}(\underline{\mathscr{G}})\rightarrow\operatorname{Free}_{\boxdot\sAlg}(\underline{\mathscr{G}}) . We can iterate this construction, therefore defining for any  k ⩾ 0 k\geqslant 0  the subobject  ad ⁡ ( ℱ ¯ ) k ​ ( ℋ ¯ ) ⊆ Free ⊡ -Lie ⁡ ( 𝒢 ¯ ) ⊆ Free ⊡ -Alg ⁡ ( 𝒢 ¯ ) \operatorname{ad}(\underline{\mathscr{F}})^{k}(\underline{\mathscr{H}})\subseteq\operatorname{Free}_{\boxdot\sLie}(\underline{\mathscr{G}})\subseteq\operatorname{Free}_{\boxdot\sAlg}(\underline{\mathscr{G}}) . 
 
 
 
 3.5.2.  Serre relations and Serre ideal in the category of mixed Hodge modules 

 
 
 (1)  
 
 For  m , n ∈ Φ M , ( − , − ) + m,n\in\Phi_{M,(-,-)}^{+}  such that  a m , n = 0 a_{m,n}=0 , we set  ℛ ¯ m , n = ad ⁡ ( 𝒢 ¯ m ) ​ ( 𝒢 ¯ n ) ⊆ Free ⊡ -Lie ⁡ ( 𝒢 ¯ ) \underline{\mathscr{R}}_{m,n}=\operatorname{ad}(\underline{\mathscr{G}}_{m})(\underline{\mathscr{G}}_{n})\subseteq\operatorname{Free}_{\boxdot\sLie}(\underline{\mathscr{G}}) , 
 
 (2)  
 
 For  m , n ∈ Φ M , ( − , − ) + m,n\in\Phi_{M,(-,-)}^{+}  such that  a m , m = 2 a_{m,m}=2 , we set  ℛ ¯ m , n = ad ⁡ ( 𝒢 ¯ m ) 1 − a m , n ​ ( 𝒢 ¯ n ) ⊆ Free ⊡ -Lie ⁡ ( 𝒢 ¯ ) \underline{\mathscr{R}}_{m,n}=\operatorname{ad}(\underline{\mathscr{G}}_{m})^{1-a_{m,n}}(\underline{\mathscr{G}}_{n})\subseteq\operatorname{Free}_{\boxdot\sLie}(\underline{\mathscr{G}}) , 
 
 (3)  
 
 For all other  m , n ∈ Φ M , ( − , − ) + m,n\in\Phi_{M,(-,-)}^{+} , we set  ℛ ¯ m , n = 0 \underline{\mathscr{R}}_{m,n}=0 . 
 
 
 We note that if  a m , n = 0 a_{m,n}=0  and  a m , m = 2 a_{m,m}=2 , both ( 1 ) and ( 2 ) give the same definition of  ℛ ¯ m , n \underline{\mathscr{R}}_{m,n} . 
 
 
 We let  ℐ ℳ , 𝒢 ¯ , Alg \mathcal{I}_{\mathcal{M},\underline{\mathscr{G}},\mathrm{Alg}}  be the two-sided ideal of  Free ⊡ -Alg ⁡ ( 𝒢 ¯ ) \operatorname{Free}_{\boxdot\sAlg}(\underline{\mathscr{G}})  generated by  ℛ ¯ m , n \underline{\mathscr{R}}_{m,n} ,  m , n ∈ Φ M , ( − , − ) + m,n\in\Phi_{M,(-,-)}^{+} . This is the two-sided ideal of  Free ⊡ -Alg ⁡ ( 𝒢 ¯ ) \operatorname{Free}_{\boxdot\sAlg}(\underline{\mathscr{G}})  defined as the image of the morphism 
 

 
 
 Free ⊡ -Alg ⁡ ( 𝒢 ¯ ) ⊡ ( ( ⨁ m , n ∈ Φ M , ( − , − ) + ℛ ¯ m , n ) ⊡ Free ⊡ -Alg ⁡ ( 𝒢 ¯ ) ) → Free ⊡ -Alg ⁡ ( 𝒢 ¯ ) \operatorname{Free}_{\boxdot\sAlg}(\underline{\mathscr{G}})\boxdot\left(\left(\bigoplus_{m,n\in\Phi_{M,(-,-)}^{+}}\underline{\mathscr{R}}_{m,n}\right)\boxdot\operatorname{Free}_{\boxdot\sAlg}(\underline{\mathscr{G}})\right)\rightarrow\operatorname{Free}_{\boxdot\sAlg}(\underline{\mathscr{G}}) 
 
 
 induced by  𝔪 ∘ ( id ⊡ 𝔪 ) \mathfrak{m}\circ(\operatorname{id}\boxdot\mathfrak{m}) . It is called the  Serre ideal . 
 
 
 We let  ℐ ℳ , 𝒢 , Lie \mathcal{I}_{\mathcal{M},\mathscr{G},\mathrm{Lie}}  be the Lie ideal of  Free ⊡ -Lie ⁡ ( 𝒢 ¯ ) \operatorname{Free}_{\boxdot\sLie}(\underline{\mathscr{G}})  generated by  ℛ ¯ m , n \underline{\mathscr{R}}_{m,n} ,  m , n ∈ Φ M , ( − , − ) + m,n\in\Phi_{M,(-,-)}^{+} . It is defined as the sum of the images of the morphisms, for  t ⩾ 0 t\geqslant 0 : 
 

 
 
 ( ( ⨁ m , n ∈ Φ M , ( − , − ) + ℛ ¯ m , n ) ⊡ ( Free ⊡ -Lie ⁡ ( 𝒢 ¯ ) ) ⊡ t ) → Free ⊡ -Lie ⁡ ( 𝒢 ¯ ) . \left(\left(\bigoplus_{m,n\in\Phi_{M,(-,-)}^{+}}\underline{\mathscr{R}}_{m,n}\right)\boxdot(\operatorname{Free}_{\boxdot\sLie}(\underline{\mathscr{G}}))^{\boxdot t}\right)\rightarrow\operatorname{Free}_{\boxdot\sLie}(\underline{\mathscr{G}}). 
 
 
 induced by the iterated Lie bracket  OPEN [ … ​ [ − , − ] , − ] , … ] [\hbox to8.00003pt{.\hss.\hss.}[-,-],-],\hbox to8.00003pt{.\hss.\hss.}] . It is also called the Serre (Lie) ideal, and the context will determine which of  ℐ ℳ , 𝒢 , Alg \mathcal{I}_{\mathcal{M},\mathscr{G},\mathrm{Alg}}  and  ℐ ℳ , 𝒢 , Lie \mathcal{I}_{\mathcal{M},\mathscr{G},\mathrm{Lie}}  is considered. 
 
 
 
 3.5.3.  Enveloping algebras 

 
 If  ℒ ¯ \underline{\mathscr{L}}  is a Lie algebra object in  MHM ⁡ ( ℳ ) \mathrm{MHM}(\mathcal{M}) , we let  Free ⊡ -Alg ⁡ ( ℒ ¯ ) \operatorname{Free}_{\boxdot\sAlg}(\underline{\mathscr{L}})  be the free associative algebra generated by the underlying mixed Hodge module of  ℒ ¯ \underline{\mathscr{L}}  defined in § 3.5.1 . We let  𝐔 ⁡ ( ℒ ¯ ) \UEA(\underline{\mathscr{L}})  be the quotient of  Free ⊡ -Alg ⁡ ( ℒ ¯ ) \operatorname{Free}_{\boxdot\sAlg}(\underline{\mathscr{L}})  by the two-sided ideal generated by the image of the morphism 
 

 
 (3.4) 
 
 C ≔ [ − , − ] ⊕ ( T ℒ ¯ , ℒ ¯ − id ℒ ¯ ⊡ ℒ ¯ ) : ℒ ¯ ⊡ ℒ ¯ → ℒ ¯ ⊕ ( ℒ ¯ ⊡ ℒ ¯ ) ⊆ Free ⊡ -Alg ⁡ ( ℒ ¯ ) . C\coloneqq[-,-]\oplus(T_{\underline{\mathscr{L}},\underline{\mathscr{L}}}-\operatorname{id}_{\underline{\mathscr{L}}\boxdot\underline{\mathscr{L}}})\colon\underline{\mathscr{L}}\boxdot\underline{\mathscr{L}}\rightarrow\underline{\mathscr{L}}\oplus(\underline{\mathscr{L}}\boxdot\underline{\mathscr{L}})\subseteq\operatorname{Free}_{\boxdot\sAlg}(\underline{\mathscr{L}}). 
 
 
 We have a canonical monomorphism of Lie algebra objects  ℒ ¯ → 𝐔 ⁡ ( ℒ ¯ ) \underline{\mathscr{L}}\rightarrow\UEA(\underline{\mathscr{L}})  induced by the canonical inclusion  ℒ ¯ → Free ⊡ -Alg ⁡ ( ℒ ¯ ) \underline{\mathscr{L}}\rightarrow\operatorname{Free}_{\boxdot\sAlg}(\underline{\mathscr{L}}) , considering the target as a Lie algebra via the commutator bracket. 
 
 
 Proposition 3.3   (Universal property) . 
 
 For any associative algebra  𝒜 ¯ \underline{\mathscr{A}}  in  ( MHM ⁡ ( ℳ ) , ⊡ ) (\mathrm{MHM}(\mathcal{M}),\boxdot)  and any morphism of Lie algebras  ℒ ¯ → 𝒜 ¯ \underline{\mathscr{L}}\rightarrow\underline{\mathscr{A}} , there exists a unique morphism of algebras  𝐔 ⁡ ( ℒ ¯ ) → 𝒜 ¯ \UEA(\underline{\mathscr{L}})\rightarrow\underline{\mathscr{A}}  such that the following diagram commutes: 
 

 
 
 ℒ ¯ {\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\underline{\mathscr{L}}}} 𝐔 ⁡ ( ℒ ¯ ) {\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\UEA(\underline{\mathscr{L}})}} 𝒜 ¯ {\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\underline{\mathscr{A}}} 
 
 
 
 
 
 Proof. 
 
 The proof follows exactly the same steps as the proof of the analogous statement for Lie algebras and their enveloping algebras in the category of vector spaces. In brief, the morphism  ℒ ¯ → 𝒜 ¯ \underline{\mathscr{L}}\rightarrow\underline{\mathscr{A}}  induces a morphism  Free ⊡ -Alg ⁡ ( ℒ ¯ ) → 𝒜 ¯ \operatorname{Free}_{\boxdot\sAlg}(\underline{\mathscr{L}})\rightarrow\underline{\mathscr{A}} . The image of  C C  ( 3.4 ) is clearly in the kernel of this morphism. We then obtain the factorisation  𝐔 ⁡ ( ℒ ¯ ) → 𝒜 ¯ \UEA(\underline{\mathscr{L}})\rightarrow\underline{\mathscr{A}}  as in the proposition. The uniqueness is immediate, as  ℒ ¯ \underline{\mathscr{L}}  generates the algebra object  𝐔 ⁡ ( ℒ ¯ ) \UEA(\underline{\mathscr{L}}) .
∎ 
 
 
 
 Proposition 3.4   (PBW isomorphism) . 
 
 Let  ℒ ¯ ∈ MHM ⁡ ( ℳ ) \underline{\mathscr{L}}\in\mathrm{MHM}(\mathcal{M})  be a Lie algebra object. Then the morphism 
 

 
 
 Sym ⊡ ⁡ ( ℒ ¯ ) → 𝐔 ⁡ ( ℒ ¯ ) \Sym_{\boxdot}(\underline{\mathscr{L}})\rightarrow\UEA(\underline{\mathscr{L}}) 
 
 
 induced by the multiplication is an isomorphism. 
 
 
 
 Proof. 
 
 The proof is identical to that for ordinary Lie algebras. Namely, we can define a filtration of  𝐔 ⁡ ( ℒ ¯ ) \UEA(\underline{\mathscr{L}})  by letting  𝐔 ⁡ ( ℒ ¯ ) n \UEA(\underline{\mathscr{L}})_{n}  be the image under the canonical surjection  Free ⊡ -Alg ⁡ ( ℒ ¯ ) → 𝐔 ⁡ ( ℒ ¯ ) \operatorname{Free}_{\boxdot\sAlg}(\underline{\mathscr{L}})\rightarrow\UEA(\underline{\mathscr{L}})  of the sub-mixed Hodge module  ⨁ m ⩽ n ℒ ¯ ⊡ m \bigoplus_{m\leqslant n}\underline{\mathscr{L}}^{\boxdot m} . The corresponding associated graded algebra object is isomorphic to  Sym ⊡ ⁡ ( ℒ ¯ ) \Sym_{\boxdot}(\underline{\mathscr{L}}) .
∎ 
 
 
 
 We now give a formal proposition whose proof follows from the universal property of the universal enveloping algebra. 
 
 
 Proposition 3.5 . 
 
 Let  𝒢 ¯ \underline{\mathscr{G}}  be an object of the symmetric monoidal category  MHM ⁡ ( ℳ ) \mathrm{MHM}(\mathcal{M}) .
Let  Free ⊡ -Lie ⁡ ( 𝒢 ¯ ) \operatorname{Free}_{\boxdot\sLie}(\underline{\mathscr{G}})  be the free Lie algebra generated by  𝒢 ¯ \underline{\mathscr{G}}  and  ℛ ¯ ⊆ Free ⊡ -Lie ⁡ ( 𝒢 ¯ ) \underline{\mathscr{R}}\subseteq\operatorname{Free}_{\boxdot\sLie}(\underline{\mathscr{G}})  be a suboject. Consider the Lie ideal  ℐ ℛ ¯ , Lie ⊆ Free ⊡ -Lie ⁡ ( 𝒢 ¯ ) \mathcal{I}_{\underline{\mathscr{R}},\mathrm{Lie}}\subseteq\operatorname{Free}_{\boxdot\sLie}(\underline{\mathscr{G}})  generated by  ℛ ¯ \underline{\mathscr{R}} . Consider also the two-sided ideal  ℐ ℛ ¯ , Alg ⊆ Free ⊡ -Alg ⁡ ( 𝒢 ¯ ) \mathcal{I}_{\underline{\mathscr{R}},\mathrm{Alg}}\subseteq\operatorname{Free}_{\boxdot\sAlg}(\underline{\mathscr{G}})  generated by  ℛ ¯ \underline{\mathscr{R}} .
Then the universal enveloping algebra  𝐔 ⁡ ( Free ⊡ -Lie ⁡ ( 𝒢 ¯ ) / ℐ ℛ ¯ , Lie ) \UEA(\operatorname{Free}_{\boxdot\sLie}(\underline{\mathscr{G}})/\mathcal{I}_{\underline{\mathscr{R}},\mathrm{Lie}})  is naturally isomorphic to the algebra  Free ⊡ -Alg ⁡ ( 𝒢 ¯ ) / ℐ ℛ ¯ , Alg \operatorname{Free}_{\boxdot\sAlg}(\underline{\mathscr{G}})/\mathcal{I}_{\underline{\mathscr{R}},\mathrm{Alg}} .
Moreover, the Lie subalgebra  ℒ ¯ ⊆ 𝐔 ⁡ ( Free ⊡ -Lie ⁡ ( 𝒢 ¯ ) / ℐ ℛ ¯ , Lie ) \underline{\mathscr{L}}\subseteq\UEA(\operatorname{Free}_{\boxdot\sLie}(\underline{\mathscr{G}})/\mathcal{I}_{\underline{\mathscr{R}},\mathrm{Lie}})  generated by  𝒢 ¯ ⊆ 𝐔 ⁡ ( Free ⊡ -Lie ⁡ ( 𝒢 ¯ ) / ℐ ℛ ¯ , Lie ) \underline{\mathscr{G}}\subseteq\UEA(\operatorname{Free}_{\boxdot\sLie}(\underline{\mathscr{G}})/\mathcal{I}_{\underline{\mathscr{R}},\mathrm{Lie}})  coincides with  Free ⊡ -Lie ⁡ ( 𝒢 ¯ ) / ℐ ℛ ¯ , Lie \operatorname{Free}_{\boxdot\sLie}(\underline{\mathscr{G}})/\mathcal{I}_{\underline{\mathscr{R}},\mathrm{Lie}} . 
 
 
 
 Proof. 
 
 Let  𝒜 ¯ = Free ⊡ -Alg ⁡ ( 𝒢 ¯ ) / ℐ ℛ ¯ , Alg \underline{\mathscr{A}}=\operatorname{Free}_{\boxdot\sAlg}(\underline{\mathscr{G}})/\mathcal{I}_{\underline{\mathscr{R}},\mathrm{Alg}} ,  𝔫 ¯ = Free ⊡ -Lie ⁡ ( 𝒢 ¯ ) / ℐ ℛ ¯ , Lie \underline{\mathfrak{n}}=\operatorname{Free}_{\boxdot\sLie}(\underline{\mathscr{G}})/\mathcal{I}_{\underline{\mathscr{R}},\mathrm{Lie}}  and  ℬ ¯ = 𝐔 ⁡ ( 𝔫 ¯ ) \underline{\mathscr{B}}=\UEA(\underline{\mathfrak{n}}) . Since  ℐ ℛ ¯ , Lie ⊆ ℐ ℛ ¯ , Alg \mathcal{I}_{\underline{\mathscr{R}},\mathrm{Lie}}\subseteq\mathcal{I}_{\underline{\mathscr{R}},\mathrm{Alg}}  we have a morphism of Lie algebras  𝔫 ¯ → 𝒜 ¯ \underline{\mathfrak{n}}\to\underline{\mathscr{A}} . By Proposition  3.3 , it extends uniquely to a morphism of algebras  ℬ ¯ → 𝒜 ¯ \underline{\mathscr{B}}\rightarrow\underline{\mathscr{A}} . Conversely, we have a morphism of algebras  Free ⊡ -Alg ⁡ ( 𝒢 ) → ℬ ¯ \operatorname{Free}_{\boxdot\sAlg}(\mathscr{G})\rightarrow\underline{\mathscr{B}}  induced by the natural map  𝒢 ¯ → ℬ ¯ \underline{\mathscr{G}}\rightarrow\underline{\mathscr{B}} . The generating subobject  ℛ ¯ \underline{\mathscr{R}}  of the ideal  ℐ ℛ ¯ , Alg \mathcal{I}_{\underline{\mathscr{R}},\mathrm{Alg}}  is sent to zero by this morphism since  ℛ ¯ ⊆ ℐ ℛ ¯ , Lie \underline{\mathscr{R}}\subseteq\mathcal{I}_{\underline{\mathscr{R}},\mathrm{Lie}} . We then obtain the morphism  𝒜 ¯ → ℬ ¯ \underline{\mathscr{A}}\rightarrow\underline{\mathscr{B}}  by quotienting the source by  ℐ ℛ ¯ , Alg \mathcal{I}_{\underline{\mathscr{R}},\mathrm{Alg}} . The morphisms  𝒜 ¯ → ℬ ¯ \underline{\mathscr{A}}\rightarrow\underline{\mathscr{B}}  and  ℬ ¯ → 𝒜 ¯ \underline{\mathscr{B}}\rightarrow\underline{\mathscr{A}}  are inverse of each other (as their composition is the identity on the generating subobject  𝒢 ¯ \underline{\mathscr{G}} ). 
 
 
 The second part of the statement follows from the first since both Lie algebras are generated by  𝒢 ¯ \underline{\mathscr{G}} .
∎ 
 
 
 
 Definition 3.6   (Positive part of the enveloping algebra and generalised Kac–Moody Lie algebra) . 
 
 We define  𝔫 ℳ , ( − , − ) , 𝒢 ¯ + ≔ Free ⊡ -Lie ⁡ ( 𝒢 ¯ ) / ℐ ℳ , 𝒢 ¯ , Lie \mathfrak{n}^{+}_{\mathcal{M},(-,-),\underline{\mathscr{G}}}\coloneqq\operatorname{Free}_{\boxdot\sLie}(\underline{\mathscr{G}})/\mathcal{I}_{\mathcal{M},\underline{\mathscr{G}},\mathrm{Lie}} . It has an induced Lie algebra structure in  MHM ⁡ ( ℳ ) \mathrm{MHM}(\mathcal{M}) . We let  𝐔 ⁡ ( 𝔫 ℳ , ( − , − ) , 𝒢 ¯ + ) \UEA(\mathfrak{n}^{+}_{\mathcal{M},(-,-),\underline{\mathscr{G}}})  be its universal enveloping algebra in  MHM ⁡ ( ℳ ) \mathrm{MHM}(\mathcal{M}) . 
 
 
 
 The MHM  𝐔 ⁡ ( 𝔫 ℳ , ( − , − ) , 𝒢 ¯ + ) \UEA(\mathfrak{n}^{+}_{\mathcal{M},(-,-),\underline{\mathscr{G}}})  is an algebra object in  MHM ⁡ ( ℳ ) \mathrm{MHM}(\mathcal{M})  and it is isomorphic to  Free ⊡ -Alg ⁡ ( 𝒢 ¯ ) / ℐ ℳ , 𝒢 ¯ , Alg \operatorname{Free}_{\boxdot\sAlg}(\underline{\mathscr{G}})/\mathcal{I}_{\mathcal{M},\underline{\mathscr{G}},\mathrm{Alg}}  by Proposition  3.5 . By the same proposition,  𝔫 ℳ , ( − , − ) , 𝒢 ¯ + \mathfrak{n}^{+}_{\mathcal{M},(-,-),\underline{\mathscr{G}}}  is the Lie subalgebra object in  𝐔 ⁡ ( 𝔫 ℳ , ( − , − ) , 𝒢 ¯ + ) \UEA(\mathfrak{n}^{+}_{\mathcal{M},(-,-),\underline{\mathscr{G}}})  generated by  𝒢 ¯ \underline{\mathscr{G}} . 
 
 
 
 
 3.6.  Global sections and restriction of generalised Kac–Moody algebras 

 
 We first give an elementary lemma. It is the analogue of Proposition  3.5  for genuine Lie algebras (as opposed to generalised Kac–Moody Lie algebras in tensor categories of perverse sheaves/mixed Hodge modules), which we repeat for convenience. 
 
 
 Lemma 3.7 . 
 
 Let  A ≔ F / I A\coloneqq F/I  be an associative algebra described as the quotient of the free associative algebra  F F  on a set of generators  E E  modulo a two-sided ideal  I ⊆ F I\subseteq F  generated by a subspace  V ⊆ F V\subseteq F . We let  E ¯ \overline{E}  be the image of  E E  in  A A . We let  𝔤 \mathfrak{g}  be the Lie subalgebra of  A A  generated by  E ¯ \overline{E} . We assume that  V ⊆ Free Lie ⁡ ( E ) ⊆ F V\subseteq\operatorname{Free}_{\mathrm{Lie}}(E)\subseteq F . Then, there is a canonical isomorphism  A ≅ 𝐔 ⁡ ( 𝔤 ) A\cong\UEA(\mathfrak{g}) . 
 
 
 
 Proposition 3.8 . 
 
 We let  cl : π 0 ​ ( ℳ ) → M \mathrm{cl}\colon\pi_{0}(\mathcal{M})\rightarrow M  be a morphism of monoids, where  M M  is a finitely generated, commutative and cancellative and set 
 

 
 
 P : Φ M , ( − , − ) + → 𝐍 [ t ± 1 / 2 ] m ↦ ch 𝐙 ​ ( H ∗ ⁡ ( 𝒢 m ) ) ≔ ∑ j ∈ 𝐙 dim H j ⁡ ( 𝒢 m ) ​ t j / 2 . \begin{matrix}P\colon&\Phi_{M,(-,-)}^{+}&\rightarrow&\mathbf{N}[t^{\pm 1/2}]&\\
&m&\mapsto&\mathrm{ch}^{\mathbf{Z}}(\HO^{*}(\mathscr{G}_{m}))&\coloneqq\sum_{j\in\mathbf{Z}}\dim\HO^{j}(\mathscr{G}_{m})t^{j/2}.\end{matrix} 
 
 
 
 (1)  
 
 The derived global sections  H ∗ ⁡ ( 𝐔 ⁡ ( 𝔫 ℳ , ( − , − ) , 𝒢 + ) ) \HO^{*}(\UEA(\mathfrak{n}^{+}_{\mathcal{M},(-,-),\mathscr{G}}))  is an algebra, isomorphic to the positive part  𝐔 ⁡ ( 𝔫 M , ( − , − ) , P + ) \UEA(\mathfrak{n}^{+}_{M,(-,-),P})  of the enveloping algebra  𝐔 ⁡ ( 𝔤 M , ( − , − ) , P ) \UEA(\mathfrak{g}_{M,(-,-),P}) . 
 
 (2)  
 
 The derived global sections  H ∗ ⁡ ( 𝔫 ℳ , ( − , − ) , 𝒢 + ) \HO^{*}(\mathfrak{n}^{+}_{\mathcal{M},(-,-),\mathscr{G}})  is a Lie algebra, isomorphic to the positive part  𝔫 M , ( − , − ) , P + \mathfrak{n}^{+}_{M,(-,-),P}  of the generalised Kac–Moody Lie algebra  𝔤 M , ( − , − ) , P \mathfrak{g}_{M,(-,-),P} . 
 
 
 
 
 
 Proof. 
 
 By the Künneth formula,  H ∗ \HO^{*}  is a monoidal functor. Therefore,  H ∗ ⁡ ( 𝔫 ℳ , ( − , − ) , 𝒢 + ) \HO^{*}(\mathfrak{n}^{+}_{\mathcal{M},(-,-),\mathscr{G}})  is the Lie subalgebra of  H ∗ ⁡ ( 𝐔 ⁡ ( 𝔫 ℳ , ( − , − ) , 𝒢 + ) ) \HO^{*}(\UEA(\mathfrak{n}^{+}_{\mathcal{M},(-,-),\mathscr{G}}))  generated by  H ∗ ⁡ ( 𝒢 m ) \HO^{*}(\mathscr{G}_{m}) ,  m ∈ Φ M , ( − , − ) + m\in\Phi_{M,(-,-)}^{+} . Moreover, 
 

 
 
 H ∗ ⁡ ( 𝐔 ⁡ ( 𝔫 ℳ , ( − , − ) , 𝒢 + ) ) = Free ⁡ ( ⨁ m ∈ Φ M , ( − , − ) + H ∗ ⁡ ( 𝒢 m ) ) / H ∗ ⁡ ( ℐ ℳ , 𝒢 , Alg ) \HO^{*}(\UEA(\mathfrak{n}^{+}_{\mathcal{M},(-,-),\mathscr{G}}))=\operatorname{Free}\left(\bigoplus_{m\in\Phi_{M,(-,-)}^{+}}\HO^{*}(\mathscr{G}_{m})\right)/\HO^{*}(\mathcal{I}_{\mathcal{M},\mathscr{G},\mathrm{Alg}}) 
 
 
 and  H ∗ ⁡ ( ℐ ℳ , 𝒢 , Alg ) \HO^{*}(\mathcal{I}_{\mathcal{M},\mathscr{G},\mathrm{Alg}})  is the two-sided ideal of  Free ⁡ ( ⨁ m ∈ Φ M , ( − , − ) + H ∗ ⁡ ( 𝒢 m ) ) \operatorname{Free}\left(\bigoplus_{m\in\Phi_{M,(-,-)}^{+}}\HO^{*}(\mathscr{G}_{m})\right)  generated by  H ∗ ⁡ ( ℛ m , n ) \HO^{*}(\mathscr{R}_{m,n})  for  m , n ∈ Φ M , ( − , − ) + m,n\in\Phi_{M,(-,-)}^{+} . By the Künneth formula again, using  ℳ m = pt \mathcal{M}_{m}=\mathrm{pt}  and  𝒢 m = 𝐐 ¯ pt \mathscr{G}_{m}=\underline{\mathbf{Q}}_{\mathrm{pt}}  if  m ∈ Φ M , ( − , − ) + , m\in\Phi_{M,(-,-)}^{+,\real} ,  H ∗ ⁡ ( ℛ m , n ) \HO^{*}(\mathscr{R}_{m,n})  is exactly the set of relations  R m , n R_{m,n}  associated to the  𝐙 \mathbf{Z} -graded vector spaces  H ∗ ⁡ ( 𝒢 m ) \HO^{*}(\mathscr{G}_{m}) ,  m ∈ Φ M , ( − , − ) + m\in\Phi^{+}_{M,(-,-)}  (see the end of § 3.2 ). This proves (1). Since  R m , n ⊆ Free Lie ⁡ ( ⨁ m ∈ Φ M , ( − , − ) + H ∗ ⁡ ( 𝒢 m ) ) R_{m,n}\subseteq\operatorname{Free}_{\mathrm{Lie}}(\bigoplus_{m\in\Phi_{M,(-,-)}^{+}}\HO^{*}(\mathscr{G}_{m})) , (2) follows thanks to Lemma  3.7 .
∎ 
 
 
 
 Proposition 3.9 . 
 
 Assume that  M = π 0 ​ ( ℳ ) M=\pi_{0}(\mathcal{M})  (so that  π 0 ​ ( ℳ ) \pi_{0}(\mathcal{M})  is a finitely generated and cancellative monoid). We assume also that there is a saturated inclusion of monoids  ı : π 0 ​ ( ℳ ) → ℳ \imath\colon\pi_{0}(\mathcal{M})\rightarrow\mathcal{M} , right inverse to the projection  p : ℳ → π 0 ​ ( ℳ ) p\colon\mathcal{M}\rightarrow\pi_{0}(\mathcal{M})  (sending all points of  ℳ m \mathcal{M}_{m}  to  m m  for  m ∈ π 0 ​ ( ℳ ) m\in\pi_{0}(\mathcal{M}) ), and such that for any  m ∈ π 0 ​ ( ℳ ) m\in\pi_{0}(\mathcal{M}) , there is an action of  𝐂 ∗ \mathbf{C}^{*}  on  ℳ m \mathcal{M}_{m}  such that  ι ⁡ ( m ) \iota(m)  is the unique fixed point and the action contracts  ℳ m \mathcal{M}_{m}  onto  ι ⁡ ( m ) \iota(m) .
We set 
 

 
 
 P : Φ M , ( − , − ) + → 𝐍 [ t ± 1 / 2 ] m ↦ dim H ∗ ⁡ ( 𝔻 ​ 𝒢 m ) ∗ = ∑ j ∈ 𝐙 dim H j ( 𝔻 𝒢 m ) t − j / 2 . \begin{matrix}P\colon&\Phi_{M,(-,-)}^{+}&\rightarrow&\mathbf{N}[t^{\pm 1/2}]&\\
&m&\mapsto&\dim\HO^{*}(\mathbb{D}\mathscr{G}_{m})^{*}&=\sum_{j\in\mathbf{Z}}\dim\HO^{j}(\mathbb{D}\mathscr{G}_{m})t^{-j/2}.\end{matrix} 
 
 
 
 (1)  
 
 The algebra  ı ! 𝐔 ( 𝔫 ℳ , ( − , − ) , 𝒢 + ) \imath^{!}\UEA(\mathfrak{n}^{+}_{\mathcal{M},(-,-),\mathscr{G}})  is isomorphic to the universal enveloping algebra  𝐔 ⁡ ( 𝔫 π 0 ​ ( ℳ ) , ( − , − ) , P + ) \UEA(\mathfrak{n}^{+}_{\pi_{0}(\mathcal{M}),(-,-),P}) , 
 
 (2)  
 
 The Lie algebra  ı ! 𝔫 + ℳ , ( − , − ) , 𝒢 \imath^{!}\mathfrak{n}^{+}_{\mathcal{M},(-,-),\mathscr{G}}  is isomorphic to the positive part  𝔫 π 0 ​ ( ℳ ) , ( − , − ) , P + \mathfrak{n}^{+}_{\pi_{0}(\mathcal{M}),(-,-),P}  of the generalised Kac–Moody Lie algebra  𝔤 π 0 ​ ( ℳ ) , ( − , − ) , P \mathfrak{g}_{\pi_{0}(\mathcal{M}),(-,-),P} . 
 
 
 
 
 
 Proof. 
 
 By base change in the diagram 
 

 
 
 π 0 ​ ( ℳ ) × π 0 ​ ( ℳ ) {\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\pi_{0}(\mathcal{M})\times\pi_{0}(\mathcal{M})}} π 0 ​ ( ℳ ) {\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\pi_{0}(\mathcal{M})}} ℳ × ℳ {\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{M}\times\mathcal{M}} ℳ {\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{M}} ⊕ \scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\oplus} ⊕ \scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\oplus} ı × ı \scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\imath\times\imath} ı \scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\imath} ⌟ \scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\lrcorner} 
 
 
 the functor  ı ! : 𝒟 + ( MHM ( ℳ ) ) → 𝒟 + ( MHM ( π 0 ( ℳ ) ) ) \imath^{!}\colon\mathcal{D}^{+}(\mathrm{MHM}(\mathcal{M}))\rightarrow\mathcal{D}^{+}(\mathrm{MHM}(\pi_{0}(\mathcal{M})))  is strictly monoidal. 
 
 
 By the assumptions on  ℳ \mathcal{M}  and  ι \iota , if  p : ℳ → π 0 ​ ( ℳ ) p\colon\mathcal{M}\rightarrow\pi_{0}(\mathcal{M})  is the natural projection mapping a point  x ∈ ℳ x\in\mathcal{M}  to the connected component of  ℳ \mathcal{M}  it belongs to,  ι ! ≅ p ! \iota^{!}\cong p_{!}  (see  [ Dav20 , Proof of Lemma 6.10] ). By strict monoidality of  ι ! \iota^{!} ,  ι ! 𝐔 ( 𝔫 ℳ , ( − , − ) , 𝒢 + ) ≅ ι ! Free Alg ( 𝒢 ) / ι ! ℐ ℳ , 𝒢 , Alg ≅ Free ( ⨁ m ∈ Φ M , ( − , − ) + ι ! 𝒢 m ) / ι ! ℐ ℳ , 𝒢 , Alg \iota^{!}\UEA(\mathfrak{n}^{+}_{\mathcal{M},(-,-),\mathscr{G}})\cong\iota^{!}\operatorname{Free}_{\mathrm{Alg}}(\mathscr{G})/\iota^{!}\mathcal{I}_{\mathcal{M},\mathscr{G},\mathrm{Alg}}\cong\operatorname{Free}(\bigoplus_{m\in\Phi_{M,(-,-)}^{+}}\iota^{!}\mathscr{G}_{m})/\iota^{!}\mathcal{I}_{\mathcal{M},\mathscr{G},\mathrm{Alg}} . Now,  ι ! 𝒢 m ≅ p ! 𝒢 m ≅ H − ⁣ ∗ ( 𝔻 𝒢 m ) ∗ \iota^{!}\mathscr{G}_{m}\cong p_{!}\mathscr{G}_{m}\cong\HO^{-*}(\mathbb{D}\mathscr{G}_{m})^{*}  as  𝐙 \mathbf{Z} -graded vector spaces and since for  m ∈ Φ M , ( − , − ) + , m\in\Phi_{M,(-,-)}^{+,\real} ,  ℳ m = pt \mathcal{M}_{m}=\mathrm{pt}  and  𝒢 m = 𝐐 ¯ pt \mathscr{G}_{m}=\underline{\mathbf{Q}}_{\mathrm{pt}} ,  ι ! ( 𝒢 m ) = 𝐐 e m \iota^{!}(\mathscr{G}_{m})=\mathbf{Q}e_{m}  and  ι ! ℛ m , n \iota^{!}\mathscr{R}_{m,n}  is the Serre relation  R m , n R_{m,n}  associated to  H ∗ ⁡ ( 𝔻 ​ 𝒢 m ) ∗ \HO^{*}(\mathbb{D}\mathscr{G}_{m})^{*} ,  m ∈ Φ M , ( − , − ) + m\in\Phi_{M,(-,-)}^{+}  (see the end of § 3.2 ). This proves part (1). Part (2) follows using Lemma  3.7 .
∎ 
 
 
 
 
 
 4.  Moduli stacks of objects in  2 2 -dimensional categories 

 
 We recall the categorical and geometric framework of  [ DHS22 ] . 
 
 
 4.1.  Moduli stacks of objects 

 
 We let  𝒞 \mathscr{C}  be a  𝐂 \mathbf{C} -linear dg-category, and let  𝕸 ⊆ 𝕸 𝒞 \bm{\mathfrak{M}}\subseteq\bm{\mathfrak{M}}_{\mathscr{C}}  be a  1 1 -Artin open substack of the stack of objects of  𝒞 \mathscr{C}  (in the sense of  [ TV07 ] ). We denote by  𝔐 = t 0 ​ ( 𝕸 ) \mathfrak{M}=t_{0}(\bm{\mathfrak{M}})  its classical truncation. We assume that  𝔐 \mathfrak{M}  parametrises objects of an admissible (in the sense of  [ Bei+18 , §1.2] ) finite length Abelian subcategory  𝒜 \mathcal{A}  of the homotopy category  H 0 ⁡ ( 𝒞 ) \HO^{0}(\mathscr{C}) . For  X = Spec ⁡ ( A ) X=\operatorname{Spec}(A) ,  X X -points of  𝕸 𝒞 \bm{\mathfrak{M}}_{\mathscr{C}}  correspond to pseudo-perfect  𝒞 ⊗ 𝐂 A \mathscr{C}\otimes_{\mathbf{C}}A -modules  N N , i.e. bimodules  N N  that are perfect as  A A -modules. Given a pair of such modules we obtain the dg  A A -module  RHom 𝒞 ⊗ 𝐂 A ⁡ ( N , N ′ ) \operatorname{RHom}_{\mathscr{C}\otimes_{\mathbf{C}}A}(N,N^{\prime}) . This defines the RHom complex on  𝕸 𝒞 × 2 \bm{\mathfrak{M}}_{\mathscr{C}}^{\times 2} . We define the  RHom \operatorname{RHom}  complex on  𝔐 × 2 \mathfrak{M}^{\times 2}  by restriction. 
 
 
 
 4.2.  Globally presented quasi-smooth morphisms 

 
 In this section we recall some terminology from  [ DHS22 , §4]  that we use in § 4.3 . We let  𝒞 ∙ \mathcal{C}^{\bullet}  be a graded perfect complex on a 1-Artin derived stack  𝕸 \bm{\mathfrak{M}}  with  𝒞 i = 0 \mathcal{C}^{i}=0  for  i ⩾ 2 i\geqslant 2 . Let  Q ∈ Der 𝕸 ⁡ ( Tot 𝕸 ⁡ ( 𝒞 ∙ ) ) Q\in\operatorname{Der}_{\bm{\mathfrak{M}}}(\operatorname{Tot}_{\bm{\mathfrak{M}}}(\mathcal{C}^{\bullet}))  be a degree one derivation that vanishes on the zero section, and satisfies  [ Q , Q ] = 0 [Q,Q]=0 . 
 
 
 Associated to this data is the derived  𝕸 \bm{\mathfrak{M}} -stack  Tot 𝕸 Q ⁡ ( 𝒞 ∙ ) \operatorname{Tot}_{\bm{\mathfrak{M}}}^{Q}(\mathcal{C}^{\bullet}) , which is the total space construction  Tot 𝕸 ⁡ ( 𝒞 ∙ ) \operatorname{Tot}_{\bm{\mathfrak{M}}}(\mathcal{C}^{\bullet}) , with the differential twisted by  Q Q . For a precise definition of  Tot 𝕸 Q ⁡ ( 𝒞 ∙ ) \operatorname{Tot}_{\bm{\mathfrak{M}}}^{Q}(\mathcal{C}^{\bullet})  see  [ DHS22 , §4.3] .
We denote the projection 
 

 
 (4.1) 
 
 𝝅 : Tot 𝕸 Q ⁡ ( 𝒞 ∙ ) ⟶ 𝕸 . \bm{\pi}\colon\operatorname{Tot}_{\bm{\mathfrak{M}}}^{Q}(\mathcal{C}^{\bullet})\longrightarrow\bm{\mathfrak{M}}. 
 
 
 We call a morphism of the form ( 4.1 ) a  globally presented quasi-smooth morphism .
A  global presentation  of a quasi-smooth morphism of derived stacks  𝒇 : 𝖃 → 𝖄 \bm{f}\colon\bm{\mathfrak{X}}\to\bm{\mathfrak{Y}}  is a commutative diagram of 1-Artin stacks 
 

 
 
 𝖃 {\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\bm{\mathfrak{X}}} 𝖄 {\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\bm{\mathfrak{Y}}} Tot 𝕸 Q ⁡ ( 𝒞 ∙ ) {\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\operatorname{Tot}^{Q}_{\bm{\mathfrak{M}}}(\mathcal{C}^{\bullet})} 𝕸 {\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\bm{\mathfrak{M}}} 𝒇 \scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\bm{f}} 𝝅 \scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\bm{\pi}} 
 
 
 where the vertical morphisms are equivalences and the bottom morphism is a globally presented quasi-smooth morphism. 
 
 
 Let  ( 𝒞 ∙ , Q 𝒞 ) (\mathcal{C}^{\bullet},Q_{\mathcal{C}})  and  ( 𝒞 ′ ⁣ ∙ , Q 𝒞 ′ ) (\mathcal{C}^{\prime\bullet},Q_{\mathcal{C}^{\prime}})  be global presentations of quasi-smooth morphisms, with both  𝒞 ∙ \mathcal{C}^{\bullet}  and  𝒞 ′ ⁣ ∙ \mathcal{C}^{\prime\bullet}  graded vector bundles over the same stack  𝕸 \bm{\mathfrak{M}} . Let
 𝒇 : Tot 𝕸 ⁡ ( 𝒞 ∙ ) → Tot 𝕸 ⁡ ( 𝒞 ′ ⁣ ∙ ) \bm{f}\colon\operatorname{Tot}_{\bm{\mathfrak{M}}}(\mathcal{C}^{\bullet})\rightarrow\operatorname{Tot}_{\bm{\mathfrak{M}}}(\mathcal{C}^{\prime\bullet}) 
be a morphism over  𝕸 \bm{\mathfrak{M}}  such that  Q 𝒞 = 𝒇 ∗ ​ Q 𝒞 ′ Q_{\mathcal{C}}=\bm{f}^{*}Q_{\mathcal{C}^{\prime}} , and such that the induced morphism of complexes  ( ( 𝒞 ∙ ) ∨ , d 𝒞 ) → ( ( 𝒞 ′ ⁣ ∙ ) ∨ , d 𝒞 ′ ) ((\mathcal{C}^{\bullet})^{\vee},d_{\mathcal{C}})\rightarrow((\mathcal{C}^{\prime\bullet})^{\vee},d_{\mathcal{C}^{\prime}})  is a quasi-isomorphism, inducing an equivalence
 Tot 𝕸 Q 𝒞 ⁡ ( 𝒞 ∙ ) → Tot 𝕸 Q 𝒞 ′ ⁡ ( 𝒞 ′ ⁣ ∙ ) . \operatorname{Tot}_{\bm{\mathfrak{M}}}^{Q_{\mathcal{C}}}(\mathcal{C}^{\bullet})\rightarrow\operatorname{Tot}_{\bm{\mathfrak{M}}}^{Q_{\mathcal{C}^{\prime}}}(\mathcal{C}^{\prime\bullet}). 
We call an equivalence defined via such an  𝒇 \bm{f}  a  global equivalence . 
 
 
 Globally presented morphisms over a fixed stack  𝕸 \bm{\mathfrak{M}}  are closed under composition: if  Tot 𝕹 Q ′ ⁡ ( 𝒞 ′ ) → 𝕹 \operatorname{Tot}_{\bm{\mathfrak{N}}}^{Q^{\prime}}(\mathcal{C}^{\prime})\to\bm{\mathfrak{N}}  and
 𝕹 ≃ Tot 𝕸 Q ⁡ ( 𝒞 ) → 𝕸 \bm{\mathfrak{N}}\simeq\operatorname{Tot}_{\bm{\mathfrak{M}}}^{Q}(\mathcal{C})\to\bm{\mathfrak{M}}  are globally presented quasi-smooth morphisms,
then the composition  𝝅 ∘ 𝝅 ′ \bm{\pi}\circ\bm{\pi}^{\prime}  is globally presented by  Tot 𝕸 Q + Q ′ ⁡ ( 𝒞 ⊕ 𝒞 ′ ~ ) → 𝕸 \operatorname{Tot}^{Q+Q^{\prime}}_{\bm{\mathfrak{M}}}(\mathcal{C}\oplus\widetilde{\mathcal{C}^{\prime}})\to\bm{\mathfrak{M}} , where  𝒞 ′ ~ \widetilde{\mathcal{C}^{\prime}}  is a graded perfect complex on  𝕸 \bm{\mathfrak{M}} .
We define the 2-category  𝙶𝙿𝚛𝚎𝚜 𝕸 \GPres_{\bm{\mathfrak{M}}}  to be the 2-category having as objects the global presentations  Tot 𝕸 Q ⁡ ( 𝒞 ∙ ) → 𝕸 \operatorname{Tot}_{\bm{\mathfrak{M}}}^{Q}(\mathcal{C}^{\bullet})\rightarrow\bm{\mathfrak{M}} , 1-morphisms given by projections of globally presented quasi-smooth  𝕸 \bm{\mathfrak{M}} -stacks, and 2-morphisms defined by global equivalences. 
 
 
 
 4.3.  Assumptions on the stack of objects 

 
 For the construction of relative CoHAs in  [ DHS22 ] , various assumptions are made on the stack of objects in  𝒜 \mathcal{A} , which we now recall. In concrete applications they are all known to hold; see  [ DHS22 ]  for a more thorough treatment of these, as well as the assumptions  4 – 6  that follow. The first three concern the stack of objects  𝔐 \mathfrak{M}  from § 4.1 . Assumptions  1 – 3  are necessary for the construction of the CoHA product. Assumptions  4  and  6  allow us to use the neighbourhood theorem of  [ Dav21 ] . Assumption  5  is used to defined the BPS algebra. 
 
 
 Assumption 1   (Assumption for the construction of the pushforward by  p p ) . 
 
 Let  𝔈 ​ 𝔵 ​ 𝔞 ​ 𝔠 ​ 𝔱 \mathfrak{Exact}  be the (classical truncation of the) stack of short exact sequences in  𝒜 \mathcal{A} . We let  p : 𝔈 ​ 𝔵 ​ 𝔞 ​ 𝔠 ​ 𝔱 → 𝔐 p\colon\mathfrak{Exact}\rightarrow\mathfrak{M}  be the morphism forgetting the extreme terms of the short exact sequence. We assume that  p p  is a proper and representable morphism of stacks. 
 
 
 
 Let  ℋ ∙ \mathcal{H}^{\bullet}  be the RHom complex on  𝔐 × 𝔐 \mathfrak{M}\times\mathfrak{M}  (§ 4.1 ), and set  𝒞 ∙ = ℋ ∙ ​ [ 1 ] \mathcal{C}^{\bullet}=\mathcal{H}^{\bullet}[1] . The stack  𝕰 = Tot 𝔐 × 𝔐 ⁡ ( 𝒞 ∙ ) \bm{\mathfrak{E}}=\operatorname{Tot}_{\mathfrak{M}\times\mathfrak{M}}(\mathcal{C}^{\bullet})  carries a universal bundle of (shifted) homomorphisms of objects in  𝒞 \mathscr{C} , so that we have a natural identification  t 0 ​ ( 𝕰 ) = 𝔈 ​ 𝔵 ​ 𝔞 ​ 𝔠 ​ 𝔱 t_{0}(\bm{\mathfrak{E}})=\mathfrak{Exact} , and a morphism  𝒑 : 𝕰 → 𝔐 \bm{p}\colon\bm{\mathfrak{E}}\rightarrow\mathfrak{M}  given by sending the morphism  f : ρ → ρ ′ f\colon\rho\rightarrow\rho^{\prime}  to  cone ​ ( f ) ​ [ 1 ] \mathrm{cone}(f)[1] . Then  p = t 0 ​ ( 𝒑 ) p=t_{0}(\bm{p})  is the morphism sending a short exact sequence to its middle term. 
 
 
 Assumption 2   (Assumption for the construction of the virtual pullback by  𝒒 \bm{q} ) . 
 
 We assume that  𝔐 \mathfrak{M}  has the resolution property, i.e. every coherent sheaf on  𝔐 \mathfrak{M}  is a quotient of a vector bundle. For all  ℳ a , ℳ b ∈ π 0 ​ ( ℳ ) \mathcal{M}_{a},\mathcal{M}_{b}\in\pi_{0}(\mathcal{M})  we assume that the morphism  𝐪 a , b : Tot 𝔐 a × 𝔐 b ⁡ ( 𝒞 a , b ∙ ) → 𝔐 a × 𝔐 b \bm{q}_{a,b}\colon\operatorname{Tot}_{\mathfrak{M}_{a}\times\mathfrak{M}_{b}}(\mathcal{C}^{\bullet}_{a,b})\rightarrow\mathfrak{M}_{a}\times\mathfrak{M}_{b}  is represented by a globally presented quasi-smooth morphism  𝐪 a , b ′ \bm{q}^{\prime}_{a,b} . 
 
 
 
 We note that, for the second part of Assumption  2  to be satisfied, it is enough for the RHom complex to be globally presented by a three-term complex of vector bundles, which is generally easy to check in examples. There are general criteria ensuring the resolution property  [ Tot04 ,  Gro17 ] . 
 
 
 Assumption 3   (Assumption for associativity) . 
 
 We assume that the stack  𝔉 ​ 𝔦 ​ 𝔩 ​ 𝔱 \mathfrak{Filt}  of three step filtrations has a derived enhancement  𝕱 ​ 𝐢 ​ 𝐥 ​ 𝐭 \bm{\mathfrak{F}ilt}  which is globally presented over  𝔐 × 𝔐 × 𝔐 \mathfrak{M}\times\mathfrak{M}\times\mathfrak{M}  and and compatibly globally presented over  𝔐 × 𝕰 \mathfrak{M}\times\bm{\mathfrak{E}}  and  𝕰 × 𝔐 \bm{\mathfrak{E}}\times\mathfrak{M} . More precisely, consider the diagram of derived stacks 
 

 
 (4.2) 
 
 𝕱 ​ 𝒊 ​ 𝒍 ​ 𝒕 {\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\bm{\mathfrak{F}ilt}} 𝔐 × 𝕰 {\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathfrak{M}\times\bm{\mathfrak{E}}} 𝕰 × 𝔐 {\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\bm{\mathfrak{E}}\times\mathfrak{M}} 𝔐 × 𝔐 × 𝔐 {\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathfrak{M}\times\mathfrak{M}\times\mathfrak{M}} 𝜶 \scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\bm{\alpha}} 𝜷 \scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\bm{\beta}} 𝜹 \scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\bm{\delta}} 𝜸 \scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\bm{\gamma}} 
 
 
 whose truncation is 
 

 
 
               𝔉 ​ 𝔦 ​ 𝔩 ​ 𝔱       𝔐 × 𝔈 ​ 𝔵 ​ 𝔞 ​ 𝔠 ​ 𝔱           𝔈 ​ 𝔵 ​ 𝔞 ​ 𝔠 ​ 𝔱 × 𝔐       𝔐 × 𝔐 × 𝔐                       α                   β                   δ                   γ           . \hbox to148.15pt{\vbox to51.11pt{\pgfpicture\makeatletter\hbox{\hskip 74.07489pt\lower-24.24161pt\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin=1} \lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke=#000000} \lxSVG@begingroup@{fill=#000000} \lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width=0.4pt} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\nullfont\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin=1} {}{}{}{{}}\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\hbox{\hbox{{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin=1} {{}{}{{{}}{{}}{{}}{{}}}{{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}}{{}{}{{
