We define the morphism  Υ Π Q : Free ⊡ -Alg ⁡ ( 𝒢 ¯ Π Q ) → ℬ ​ 𝒫 ​ 𝒮 ¯ Π Q , Alg ψ \Upsilon_{\Pi_{Q}}\colon\operatorname{Free}_{\boxdot\sAlg}(\underline{\mathscr{G}}_{\Pi_{Q}})\rightarrow\underline{\mathcal{BPS}}_{\Pi_{Q},\mathrm{Alg}}^{\psi}  in the same way (for the particular case  𝒜 = Rep ⁡ Π Q \mathcal{A}=\operatorname{Rep}\Pi_{Q} ). 
 
 
 Proposition 7.7 . 
 
 We have a commutative diagram 
 

 
 
 ι 𝒩 ! Free ⊡ -Alg ( 𝒢 ¯ Π Q ℱ ¯ ) {\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\iota_{\mathcal{N}}^{!}\operatorname{Free}_{\boxdot\sAlg}(\underline{\mathscr{G}}_{\Pi_{Q_{\underline{\mathcal{F}}}}})}} ι 𝒩 ! ℬ ​ 𝒫 ​ 𝒮 ¯ Π Q ℱ ¯ , Alg ψ {\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\iota_{\mathcal{N}}^{!}\underline{\mathcal{BPS}}_{\Pi_{Q_{\underline{\mathcal{F}}}},\mathrm{Alg}}^{\psi}}} ι ℱ ¯ ! Free ⊡ -Alg ( 𝒢 ¯ 𝒜 ) {\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\iota_{\underline{\mathcal{F}}}^{!}\operatorname{Free}_{\boxdot\sAlg}(\underline{\mathscr{G}}_{\mathcal{A}})}} ι ℱ ¯ ! ℬ ​ 𝒫 ​ 𝒮 ¯ 𝒜 ψ {\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\iota_{\underline{\mathcal{F}}}^{!}\underline{\mathcal{BPS}}_{\mathcal{A}}^{\psi}}} Υ 𝒜 \scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Upsilon_{\mathcal{A}}} Υ Π Q \scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Upsilon_{\Pi_{Q}}} 
 
 
 where vertical arrows are isomorphisms, given respectively by Proposition  7.4  and Lemma  7.1 . 
 
 
 
 Proof. 
 
 The left-most vertical arrow is an isomorphism by Proposition  7.4 . The right-most vertical arrow is an isomorphism by Lemma  7.1 . The commutativity comes from the compatibility with restriction to monoids of the multiplication proven in  [ DHS22 , Corollary 9.10] : the left-most vertical map is an  algebra  isomorphism (Proposition  7.4 ) and the right-most vertical arrow is also an algebra isomorphism (compatibility of CoHA multiplications).
∎ 
 
 
 
 
 7.5.  Loop equivariance 

 
 Let  L ¯ ⊆ Q ¯ 0 \overline{L}\subseteq\overline{Q}_{0}  be the set of loops of the quiver  Q ¯ \overline{Q} . The group  𝔾 a L ¯ \mathbb{G}_{a}^{\overline{L}}  acts on  𝔐 Π Q \mathfrak{M}_{\Pi_{Q}}  in the following way. If  x ¯ ≔ ( x α ) α ∈ Q ¯ 1 ∈ μ Q , 𝐝 − 1 ​ ( 0 ) \underline{x}\coloneqq(x_{\alpha})_{\alpha\in\overline{Q}_{1}}\in\mu^{-1}_{Q,\mathbf{d}}(0)  is a  𝐝 \mathbf{d} -dimensional representation of  Q Q  and  g ¯ ∈ 𝔾 a L ¯ \underline{g}\in\mathbb{G}_{a}^{\overline{L}} , then 
 

 
 
 ( g ¯ ⋅ x ¯ ) α ≔ { x α + g α ⋅ Id d s ⁡ ( α ) × d s ⁡ ( α ) ⁡  if  α ∈ L ¯ x α ​  otherwise . (\underline{g}\cdot\underline{x})_{\alpha}\coloneqq\left\{\begin{aligned} &x_{\alpha}+g_{\alpha}\!\cdot\!\Id_{d_{s(\alpha)}\times d_{s(\alpha)}}\text{ if $\alpha\in\overline{L}$}\\
&x_{\alpha}\text{ otherwise}.\end{aligned}\right. 
 
 
 This action commutes with the  GL 𝐝 \mathrm{GL}_{\mathbf{d}} -action on  μ 𝐝 − 1 ​ ( 0 ) \mu_{\mathbf{d}}^{-1}(0)  and so descends to  𝔾 a L ¯ \mathbb{G}_{a}^{\overline{L}} -actions on  𝔐 Π Q , 𝐝 \mathfrak{M}_{\Pi_{Q},\mathbf{d}}  and  ℳ Π Q , 𝐝 \mathcal{M}_{\Pi_{Q},\mathbf{d}}  and therefore on  𝔐 Π Q \mathfrak{M}_{\Pi_{Q}}  and  ℳ Π Q \mathcal{M}_{\Pi_{Q}} . 
 
 
 Lemma 7.8 . 
 
 The relative cohomological Hall algebra  𝒜 ¯ Π Q ( ψ ) \underline{\mathscr{A}}_{\Pi_{Q}}^{(\psi)} , the relative BPS algebra  ℬ ​ 𝒫 ​ 𝒮 ¯ Π Q , Alg ( ψ ) \underline{\mathcal{BPS}}_{\Pi_{Q},\mathrm{Alg}}^{(\psi)}  and the free algebra  Free ⊡ -Alg ⁡ ( 𝒢 ¯ Π Q ) \operatorname{Free}_{\boxdot\sAlg}(\underline{\mathscr{G}}_{\Pi_{Q}})  are  𝔾 a L ¯ \mathbb{G}_{a}^{\overline{L}} -equivariant. 
 
 
 
 Proof. 
 
 The equivariance of the relative cohomological Hall algebra  𝒜 ¯ Π Q ( ψ ) \underline{\mathscr{A}}_{\Pi_{Q}}^{(\psi)}  and of the relative BPS algebra  ℬ ​ 𝒫 ​ 𝒮 ¯ Π Q , Alg ( ψ ) \underline{\mathcal{BPS}}_{\Pi_{Q},\mathrm{Alg}}^{(\psi)}  are direct consequences of the  𝔾 a L ¯ \mathbb{G}_{a}^{\overline{L}} -equivariance of  𝙹𝙷 Π Q \JH_{\Pi_{Q}} . The equivariance of  Free ⊡ -Alg ⁡ ( 𝒢 ¯ Π Q ) \operatorname{Free}_{\boxdot\sAlg}(\underline{\mathscr{G}}_{\Pi_{Q}})  comes from the  𝔾 a L ¯ \mathbb{G}_{a}^{\overline{L}}  stability of the smooth locus of  ℳ Π Q , 𝐝 \mathcal{M}_{\Pi_{Q},\mathbf{d}}  (for  𝐝 ∈ Σ Π Q \mathbf{d}\in\Sigma_{\Pi_{Q}} ) and the  𝔾 a L ¯ \mathbb{G}_{a}^{\overline{L}} -stability of the image of  u 𝐝 : ℳ Π Q , 𝐝 → ℳ Π Q , l ​ 𝐝 u_{\mathbf{d}}\colon\mathcal{M}_{\Pi_{Q},\mathbf{d}}\rightarrow\mathcal{M}_{\Pi_{Q},l\mathbf{d}}  for  𝐝 ∈ Σ Π Q \mathbf{d}\in\Sigma_{\Pi_{Q}}  (for the case when  𝐝 \mathbf{d}  is isotropic).
∎ 
 
 
 
 Let  L ⊆ L ¯ L\subseteq\overline{L}  be the set of loops of the quiver  Q Q . The group  𝔾 a L \mathbb{G}_{a}^{L}  is naturally a subgroup of  𝔾 a L ¯ \mathbb{G}_{a}^{\overline{L}}  and hence acts on  𝔐 Π Q \mathfrak{M}_{\Pi_{Q}}  via the above action. 
 
 
 
 7.6.  The degree zero BPS algebra of the semi-nilpotent stack 

 
 7.6.1.  Borcherds–Bozec Lie algebra of a quiver 

 
 Let  Q = ( Q 0 , Q 1 ) Q=(Q_{0},Q_{1})  be a quiver. We consider the monoid  M = 𝐍 Q 0 M=\mathbf{N}^{Q_{0}} . It is endowed with the symmetrisation of the Euler form of  Q Q ,  ( 𝐝 , 𝐞 ) = 2 ​ ∑ i ∈ Q 0 𝐝 i ​ 𝐞 i − ∑ i → 𝛼 j ∈ Q 1 ( 𝐝 i ​ 𝐝 j + 𝐝 j ​ 𝐝 i ) (\mathbf{d},\mathbf{e})=2\sum_{i\in Q_{0}}\mathbf{d}_{i}\mathbf{e}_{i}-\sum_{i\xrightarrow{\alpha}j\in Q_{1}}(\mathbf{d}_{i}\mathbf{d}_{j}+\mathbf{d}_{j}\mathbf{d}_{i})  (which is the Euler form of the Ginzburg dg-algebra  𝒢 2 ​ ( 𝐂 ​ Q ) \mathscr{G}_{2}(\mathbf{C}Q) ). Recall the set of positive simple roots  Φ M , ( − , − ) + \Phi^{+}_{M,(-,-)}  as defined in § 3.1 . We have a decomposition  Q 0 = Q 0 ⊔ Q 0 im Q_{0}=Q_{0}\sqcup Q_{0}^{\mathrm{im}} , where  Q 0 Q_{0}  is the set of vertices of  Q Q  carrying no loops and  Q 0 im Q_{0}^{\mathrm{im}}  is the set of vertices of  Q Q  carrying at least one loop. We let  I ∞ = ( Q 0 × { 1 } ) ⊔ ( Q 0 im × 𝐙 ⩾ 1 ) I_{\infty}=(Q_{0}\times\{1\})\sqcup(Q_{0}^{\mathrm{im}}\times\mathbf{Z}_{\geqslant 1}) . We have an injection  I ∞ → Φ M , ( − , − ) + I_{\infty}\rightarrow\Phi_{M,(-,-)}^{+}  sending  ( i ′ , n ) ∈ I ∞ (i^{\prime},n)\in I_{\infty}  to  n ​ 1 i ′ n1_{i^{\prime}} . We consider the weight function 
 

 
 
 P : Φ M , ( − , − ) + → 𝐍 m ↦ { 1 ​  if  m ∈ I ∞ 0 ​  otherwise. \begin{matrix}P&\colon&\Phi_{M,(-,-)}^{+}&\rightarrow&\mathbf{N}\\
&&m&\mapsto&\left\{\begin{aligned} &1\text{ if $m\in I_{\infty}$}\\
&0\text{ otherwise.}\end{aligned}\right.\par\end{matrix} 
 
 
 The  Borcherds–Bozec Lie algebra  associated to  Q Q  is  𝔤 Q ≔ 𝔤 M , ( − , − ) , P \mathfrak{g}_{Q}\coloneqq\mathfrak{g}_{M,(-,-),P} , following § 3 . We extend the bilinear form  ( − , − ) (-,-)  to  𝐙 ( I ∞ ) \mathbf{Z}^{(I_{\infty})}  by pullback via the natural morphism  𝐙 ( I ∞ ) → 𝐙 Q 0 \mathbf{Z}^{(I_{\infty})}\rightarrow\mathbf{Z}^{Q_{0}} ,  ( i ′ , n ) ↦ n ​ 1 i ′ (i^{\prime},n)\mapsto n1_{i^{\prime}} . The positive part  𝔫 Q + \mathfrak{n}_{Q}^{+}  of  𝔤 Q \mathfrak{g}_{Q}  admits the following description by generators and relations. It is generated by  e ( i ′ , n ) e_{(i^{\prime},n)}  for  ( i ′ , n ) ∈ I ∞ (i^{\prime},n)\in I_{\infty}  with the relations 
 

 
 
 ad ⁡ ( e j ) 1 − ( j , i ) ​ ( e i ) \displaystyle\operatorname{ad}(e_{j})^{1-(j,i)}(e_{i}) 
 = 0 \displaystyle=0 
 for  j ∈ Q 0 × { 1 } ,  i ≠ j \displaystyle\text{ for $j\in Q_{0}\times\{1\}$, $i\neq j$} 
 
 
 
 [ e i , e j ] \displaystyle[e_{i},e_{j}] 
 = 0 \displaystyle=0 
 if  ( i , j ) = 0 . \displaystyle\text{ if $(i,j)=0$}. 
 
 
 
 
 
 7.6.2.  The cohomological Hall algebra of the strictly semi-nilpotent stack 

 
 Recall from § 6.1  the stack of strictly semi-nilpotent representations of  Π Q \Pi_{Q} ,  𝔐 Π Q 𝒮 ​ 𝒮 ​ 𝒩 \mathfrak{M}_{\Pi_{Q}}^{\mathcal{SSN}} . The absolute cohomological Hall algebra of the category of strictly semi-nilpotent representations of  Q Q  is 
 

 
 
 H ∗ ⁡ 𝒜 Π Q 𝒮 ​ 𝒮 ​ 𝒩 , ψ := ⨁ 𝐝 ∈ 𝐍 Q 0 H ∗ BM ⁡ ( 𝔐 Π Q , 𝐝 𝒮 ​ 𝒮 ​ 𝒩 , 𝐐 vir ) , \HO^{*}\!\!\!\mathscr{A}_{\Pi_{Q}}^{\mathcal{SSN},\psi}:=\bigoplus_{\mathbf{d}\in\mathbf{N}^{Q_{0}}}\HO_{*}^{\BoMo}(\mathfrak{M}_{\Pi_{Q},\mathbf{d}}^{\mathcal{SSN}},\mathbf{Q}^{\mathrm{vir}}), 
 
 
 and has been defined and studied in  [ SV20 ] . 
 
 
 We let  H 0 ⁡ 𝒜 Π Q 𝒮 ​ 𝒮 ​ 𝒩 , ψ \HO^{0}\!\mathscr{A}_{\Pi_{Q}}^{\mathcal{SSN},\psi}  be the  degree zero strictly semi-nilpotent cohomological Hall algebra . It has a combinatorial basis given by fundamental classes of irreducible components of  𝔐 Π Q 𝒮 ​ 𝒮 ​ 𝒩 \mathfrak{M}_{\Pi_{Q}}^{\mathcal{SSN}} ,  [ 𝔐 Π Q , 𝐝 , c 𝒮 ​ 𝒮 ​ 𝒩 ] [\mathfrak{M}^{\mathcal{SSN}}_{\Pi_{Q},\mathbf{d},c}] . This algebra is identified with the enveloping algebra of the Borcherds–Bozec Lie algebra. This is a result proven in detail in  [ Hen22 , Theorem 1.3] . 
 
 
 Theorem 7.9 . 
 
 There is an isomorphism of algebras 
 

 
 
 𝐔 ⁡ ( 𝔫 Q + ) → H 0 ⁡ 𝒜 Π Q 𝒮 ​ 𝒮 ​ 𝒩 , ψ \UEA(\mathfrak{n}_{Q}^{+})\rightarrow\HO^{0}\!\mathcal{A}_{\Pi_{Q}}^{\mathcal{SSN},\psi} 
 
 
 sending the generator  e ( i ′ , n ) e_{(i^{\prime},n)}  to  [ 𝔐 n ​ 1 i ′ , ( n ) 𝒮 ​ 𝒮 ​ 𝒩 ] [\mathfrak{M}^{\mathcal{SSN}}_{n1_{i^{\prime}},(n)}] , where  𝔐 n ​ 1 i ′ , ( n ) 𝒮 ​ 𝒮 ​ 𝒩 \mathfrak{M}_{n1_{i^{\prime}},(n)}^{\mathcal{SSN}}  is the closed substack of  𝔐 Π Q \mathfrak{M}_{\Pi_{Q}}  of  n ​ 1 i ′ n1_{i^{\prime}} -dimensional representations for which the arrows of  Q 1 ∗ Q_{1}^{*}  act by  0 0 . 
 
 
 
 Recall the definition of  ℳ Π Q 𝒮 ​ 𝒮 ​ 𝒩 \mathcal{M}_{\Pi_{Q}}^{\mathcal{SSN}}  in § 6.1 : this is the subscheme of  ℳ Π Q \mathcal{M}_{\Pi_{Q}}  of semisimple  Π Q \Pi_{Q} -representations such that the only arrows of  Q ¯ \overline{Q}  acting nontrivially are loops in  Q 1 ⊆ Q ¯ 1 Q_{1}\subseteq\overline{Q}_{1} . Let  ı 𝒮 ​ 𝒮 ​ 𝒩 : ℳ Π Q 𝒮 ​ 𝒮 ​ 𝒩 → ℳ Π Q \imath_{\mathcal{SSN}}\colon\mathcal{M}_{\Pi_{Q}}^{\mathcal{SSN}}\rightarrow\mathcal{M}_{\Pi_{Q}}  be the natural inclusion. 
 
 
 Lemma 7.10   ( [ Dav20 ]  §6.4) . 
 
 The natural map  τ ⩽ 0 𝔭 ​ 𝒜 Π Q ( ψ ) → 𝒜 Π Q ( ψ ) {{}^{\mathfrak{p}}\tau}_{\leqslant 0}\mathscr{A}_{\Pi_{Q}}^{(\psi)}\rightarrow\mathscr{A}_{\Pi_{Q}}^{(\psi)}  induces a map  ı 𝒮 ​ 𝒮 ​ 𝒩 ! 𝔭 τ ⩽ 0 𝒜 Π Q ( ψ ) → ı 𝒮 ​ 𝒮 ​ 𝒩 ! 𝒜 Π Q ( ψ ) = : 𝒜 Π Q 𝒮 ​ 𝒮 ​ 𝒩 , ( ψ ) \imath_{\mathcal{SSN}}^{!}{{}^{\mathfrak{p}}\tau}_{\leqslant 0}\mathscr{A}_{\Pi_{Q}}^{(\psi)}\rightarrow\imath_{\mathcal{SSN}}^{!}\mathscr{A}_{\Pi_{Q}}^{(\psi)}=:\mathscr{A}_{\Pi_{Q}}^{\mathcal{SSN},(\psi)}  which in turn induces an isomorphism 
 

 
 
 H 0 ( ı 𝒮 ​ 𝒮 ​ 𝒩 ! τ ⩽ 0 𝔭 𝒜 Π Q ) → H 0 ( ı 𝒮 ​ 𝒮 ​ 𝒩 ! 𝒜 Π Q ) . \HO^{0}(\imath_{\mathcal{SSN}}^{!}{{}^{\mathfrak{p}}\tau}_{\leqslant 0}\mathscr{A}_{\Pi_{Q}})\rightarrow\HO^{0}(\imath_{\mathcal{SSN}}^{!}\mathscr{A}_{\Pi_{Q}}). 
 
 
 
 
 
 Corollary 7.11 . 
 
 Let  Q Q  be a quiver. Then the morphism 
 

 
 (7.2) 
 
 H 0 ( ı 𝒮 ​ 𝒮 ​ 𝒩 ! Υ Π Q ) : H 0 ( ı 𝒮 ​ 𝒮 ​ 𝒩 ! ( Free ⊡ -Alg ( 𝒢 Π Q ) ) ) → H 0 ( 𝒜 Π Q 𝒮 ​ 𝒮 ​ 𝒩 , ψ ) \HO^{0}(\imath_{\mathcal{SSN}}^{!}\Upsilon_{\Pi_{Q}})\colon\HO^{0}\!(\imath_{\mathcal{SSN}}^{!}(\operatorname{Free}_{\boxdot\sAlg}(\mathscr{G}_{\Pi_{Q}})))\rightarrow\HO^{0}(\mathscr{A}_{\Pi_{Q}}^{\mathcal{SSN},\psi}) 
 
 
 obtained by applying the functor  H 0 ( ı 𝒮 ​ 𝒮 ​ 𝒩 ! ( − ) ) \HO^{0}(\imath_{\mathcal{SSN}}^{!}(-))  to the natural morphism  Υ Π Q : Free ⊡ -Alg ⁡ ( 𝒢 Π Q ) → 𝒜 Π Q ψ \Upsilon_{\Pi_{Q}}\colon\operatorname{Free}_{\boxdot\sAlg}(\mathscr{G}_{\Pi_{Q}})\rightarrow\mathscr{A}_{\Pi_{Q}}^{\psi}  (see § 7.4 ) vanishes on  H 0 ( ı 𝒮 ​ 𝒮 ​ 𝒩 ! ℐ Π Q , Alg ) \HO^{0}(\imath_{\mathcal{SSN}}^{!}\mathcal{I}_{\Pi_{Q},\mathrm{Alg}})  and induces an isomorphism 
 

 
 
 H 0 ( ı 𝒮 ​ 𝒮 ​ 𝒩 ! ( Free ⊡ -Alg ( 𝒢 Π Q ) ) ) / H 0 ( ı 𝒮 ​ 𝒮 ​ 𝒩 ! ℐ Π Q , Alg ) → H 0 ( 𝒜 Π Q 𝒮 ​ 𝒮 ​ 𝒩 , ψ ) . \HO^{0}\!(\imath_{\mathcal{SSN}}^{!}(\operatorname{Free}_{\boxdot\sAlg}(\mathscr{G}_{\Pi_{Q}})))/\HO^{0}(\imath_{\mathcal{SSN}}^{!}\mathcal{I}_{\Pi_{Q},\mathrm{Alg}})\rightarrow\HO^{0}(\mathscr{A}_{\Pi_{Q}}^{\mathcal{SSN},\psi}). 
 
 
 
 
 
 Proof. 
 
 The functor  ı 𝒮 ​ 𝒮 ​ 𝒩 ! \imath_{\mathcal{SSN}}^{!}  is strictly monoidal (§ 6.1 ), so it commutes with the operator  Free \operatorname{Free} . By Proposition  6.8 , the subobject  𝒢 Π Q ⊆ ℬ ​ 𝒫 ​ 𝒮 Π Q , Alg ψ \mathscr{G}_{\Pi_{Q}}\subseteq\mathcal{BPS}_{\Pi_{Q},\mathrm{Alg}}^{\psi}  admits a direct sum complement  ℱ ⊆ ℬ ​ 𝒫 ​ 𝒮 Π Q , Alg ψ \mathscr{F}\subseteq\mathcal{BPS}_{\Pi_{Q},\mathrm{Alg}}^{\psi}  such that the multiplication map  𝔪 𝐝 , 𝐞 ψ : ℬ ​ 𝒫 ​ 𝒮 Π Q , Alg , 𝐝 ψ ⊠ ℬ ​ 𝒫 ​ 𝒮 Π Q , Alg , 𝐞 ψ → ℬ ​ 𝒫 ​ 𝒮 Π Q , Alg , 𝐝 + 𝐞 ψ \mathfrak{m}_{\mathbf{d},\mathbf{e}}^{\psi}\colon\mathcal{BPS}_{\Pi_{Q},\mathrm{Alg},\mathbf{d}}^{\psi}\boxtimes\mathcal{BPS}_{\Pi_{Q},\mathrm{Alg},\mathbf{e}}^{\psi}\rightarrow\mathcal{BPS}_{\Pi_{Q},\mathrm{Alg},\mathbf{d}+\mathbf{e}}^{\psi}  factors through the inclusion  ℱ 𝐝 + 𝐞 ⊆ ℬ ​ 𝒫 ​ 𝒮 Π Q , Alg , 𝐝 + 𝐞 ψ \mathscr{F}_{\mathbf{d}+\mathbf{e}}\subseteq\mathcal{BPS}_{\Pi_{Q},\mathrm{Alg},\mathbf{d}+\mathbf{e}}^{\psi}  if  𝐝 ≠ 0 ≠ 𝐞 \mathbf{d}\neq 0\neq\mathbf{e} . Consequently, the multiplication map  H 0 ( ı 𝒮 ​ 𝒮 ​ 𝒩 ! 𝔪 𝐝 , 𝐞 ψ ) : H 0 ( 𝒜 Π Q , 𝐝 𝒮 ​ 𝒮 ​ 𝒩 , ψ ) ⊗ H 0 ( 𝒜 Π Q , 𝐞 𝒮 ​ 𝒮 ​ 𝒩 , ψ ) → H 0 ( 𝒜 Π Q , 𝐝 + 𝐞 𝒮 ​ 𝒮 ​ 𝒩 , ψ ) \HO^{0}(\imath_{\mathcal{SSN}}^{!}\mathfrak{m}_{\mathbf{d},\mathbf{e}}^{\psi})\colon\HO^{0}(\mathscr{A}_{\Pi_{Q},\mathbf{d}}^{\mathcal{SSN},\psi})\otimes\HO^{0}(\mathscr{A}_{\Pi_{Q},\mathbf{e}}^{\mathcal{SSN},\psi})\rightarrow\HO^{0}(\mathscr{A}_{\Pi_{Q},\mathbf{d}+\mathbf{e}}^{\mathcal{SSN},\psi})  factors through  H 0 ( ı 𝒮 ​ 𝒮 ​ 𝒩 ! ℱ 𝐝 + 𝐞 ) \HO^{0}(\imath_{\mathcal{SSN}}^{!}\mathscr{F}_{\mathbf{d}+\mathbf{e}}) . 
 
 
 If  𝐝 = n ​ 1 i ′ \mathbf{d}=n1_{i^{\prime}}  for some  i ′ ∈ Q 0 hyp i^{\prime}\in Q_{0}^{\mathrm{hyp}} ,  n ⩾ 1 n\geqslant 1 , then,  H 0 ( ı 𝒮 ​ 𝒮 ​ 𝒩 ! ℐ 𝒞 ( ℳ Π Q , n ​ 1 i ′ ) ) = 𝐐 e i ′ , n \HO^{0}(\imath_{\mathcal{SSN}}^{!}\mathcal{IC}(\mathcal{M}_{\Pi_{Q},n1_{i^{\prime}}}))=\mathbf{Q}e_{i^{\prime},n}  is one-dimensional  [ Dav21 , §6.4.4] . If  i ′ ∈ Q 0 i^{\prime}\in Q_{0} ,  ℳ Π Q , 1 i ′ = pt \mathcal{M}_{\Pi_{Q},1_{i^{\prime}}}=\mathrm{pt}  and therefore,  H 0 ( ı 𝒮 ​ 𝒮 ​ 𝒩 ! ℐ 𝒞 ( ℳ Π Q , 1 i ′ ) ) = 𝐐 e i ′ , 1 \HO^{0}(\imath_{\mathcal{SSN}}^{!}\mathcal{IC}(\mathcal{M}_{\Pi_{Q},1_{i^{\prime}}}))=\mathbf{Q}e_{i^{\prime},1}  is one-dimensional. If  𝐝 = n ​ e i ′ \mathbf{d}=ne_{i^{\prime}}  with  i ′ ∈ Q 0 iso i^{\prime}\in Q_{0}^{\mathrm{iso}} ,  H 0 ( ı 𝒮 ​ 𝒮 ​ 𝒩 ! 𝒢 n ​ 1 i ′ ) ) = 𝐐 e i ′ , n \HO^{0}(\imath_{\mathcal{SSN}}^{!}\mathscr{G}_{n1_{i^{\prime}}}))=\mathbf{Q}e_{i^{\prime},n}  is one-dimensional by the description of the BPS Lie algebra sheaf for the Jordan quiver  [ Dav20 , §7.2]  and given that in this case,  𝒢 n ​ 1 i ′ = ℬ ​ 𝒫 ​ 𝒮 Π Q , Lie , n ​ 1 i ′ \mathscr{G}_{n1_{i^{\prime}}}=\mathcal{BPS}_{\Pi_{Q},\mathrm{Lie},n1_{i^{\prime}}}  (see also § 6.4.2  which applies to  Π Q \Pi_{Q}  for  Q Q  the Jordan quiver). By Theorem  7.9 , if  𝐝 ≠ n ​ 1 i ′ \mathbf{d}\neq n1_{i^{\prime}}  for  ( i ′ , n ) ∈ I ∞ (i^{\prime},n)\in I_{\infty} , then  H 0 ( ı 𝒮 ​ 𝒮 ​ 𝒩 ! ℱ 𝐝 ) = H 0 ( 𝒜 Π Q , 𝐝 𝒮 ​ 𝒮 ​ 𝒩 , ψ ) \HO^{0}(\imath_{\mathcal{SSN}}^{!}\mathscr{F}_{\mathbf{d}})=\HO^{0}(\mathscr{A}_{\Pi_{Q},\mathbf{d}}^{\mathcal{SSN},\psi}) . If moreover  𝐝 ∈ Φ Π Q + \mathbf{d}\in\Phi^{+}_{\Pi_{Q}} ,  H 0 ( ı 𝒮 ​ 𝒮 ​ 𝒩 ! 𝒢 𝐝 ) \HO^{0}(\imath_{\mathcal{SSN}}^{!}\mathscr{G}_{\mathbf{d}})  is a direct summand of  H 0 ⁡ ( 𝒜 Π Q , 𝐝 𝒮 ​ 𝒮 ​ 𝒩 ) \HO^{0}(\mathscr{A}_{\Pi_{Q},\mathbf{d}}^{\mathcal{SSN}})  with complement  H 0 ( ı 𝒮 ​ 𝒮 ​ 𝒩 ! ℱ 𝐝 ) \HO^{0}(\imath_{\mathcal{SSN}}^{!}\mathscr{F}_{\mathbf{d}}) . It therefore vanishes. The domain of ( 7.2 ) is then equal to  H 0 ⁡ ( Free ⁡ ( ⨁ i ∈ I ∞ 𝐐 ​ e i ) ) \HO^{0}\!\left(\operatorname{Free}\left(\bigoplus_{i\in I_{\infty}}\mathbf{Q}e_{i}\right)\right) . By strict monoidality of  ı 𝒮 ​ 𝒮 ​ 𝒩 ! \imath_{\mathcal{SSN}}^{!} ,  ı 𝒮 ​ 𝒮 ​ 𝒩 ! ℐ Π Q , Alg \imath_{\mathcal{SSN}}^{!}\mathcal{I}_{\Pi_{Q},\mathrm{Alg}}  is generated by the Serre relations for  e ( i ′ , n ) e_{(i^{\prime},n)} ,  ( i ′ , n ) ∈ I ∞ (i^{\prime},n)\in I_{\infty} , given in § 7.6.1 . By Theorem  7.9 ,  H 0 ( ı 𝒮 ​ 𝒮 ​ 𝒩 ! Υ Π Q ) \HO^{0}(\imath_{\mathcal{SSN}}^{!}\Upsilon_{\Pi_{Q}})  vanishes on  H 0 ( ı 𝒮 ​ 𝒮 ​ 𝒩 ! ℐ Π Q , Alg ) \HO^{0}(\imath_{\mathcal{SSN}}^{!}\mathcal{I}_{\Pi_{Q},\mathrm{Alg}}) .
∎ 
 
 
 
 
 
 7.7.  The BPS Lie algebra of a 2-Calabi–Yau category 

 
 The proof of Theorem  1.1  is given in this section. 
 
 
 7.7.1.  Factorization through the Serre ideal 

 
 We adopt ideas from the proof of  [ DHS22 ] . In comparison to the case of totally negative  2 2 -Calabi–Yau categories, there is an extra step which is to show that the morphism  Free ⊡ -Alg ⁡ ( 𝒢 ¯ ) → ℬ ​ 𝒫 ​ 𝒮 ¯ 𝒜 , Alg ψ \operatorname{Free}_{\boxdot\sAlg}(\underline{\mathscr{G}})\rightarrow\underline{\mathcal{BPS}}_{\mathcal{A},\mathrm{Alg}}^{\psi}  factors through the quotient by the Serre ideal  Free ⊡ -Alg ⁡ ( 𝒢 ) → Free ⊡ -Alg ⁡ ( 𝒢 ¯ ) / ℐ ¯ 𝒜 , Alg \operatorname{Free}_{\boxdot\sAlg}(\mathscr{G})\rightarrow\operatorname{Free}_{\boxdot\sAlg}(\underline{\mathscr{G}})/\underline{\mathcal{I}}_{\mathcal{A},\mathrm{Alg}} . 
 
 
 Lemma 7.12 . 
 
 Let  ( Q , 𝐝 ) (Q,\mathbf{d})  be such that  Υ Π Q , 𝐝 : Free ⊡ -Alg ⁡ ( 𝒢 ¯ Π Q ) 𝐝 → ℬ ​ 𝒫 ​ 𝒮 ¯ Π Q , Alg , 𝐝 ψ \Upsilon_{\Pi_{Q},\mathbf{d}}\colon\operatorname{Free}_{\boxdot\sAlg}(\underline{\mathscr{G}}_{\Pi_{Q}})_{\mathbf{d}}\rightarrow\underline{\mathcal{BPS}}_{\Pi_{Q},\mathrm{Alg},\mathbf{d}}^{\psi}  does not vanish on  ℐ ¯ Π Q , Alg , 𝐝 \underline{\mathcal{I}}_{\Pi_{Q},\mathrm{Alg},\mathbf{d}} . Then, if  𝒯 ¯ \underline{\mathcal{T}}  is a mixed Hodge module which is a simple summand of  ℐ ¯ Π Q , Alg , 𝐝 \underline{\mathcal{I}}_{\Pi_{Q},\mathrm{Alg},\mathbf{d}}  not contained in the kernel of  Υ Π Q , 𝐝 \Upsilon_{\Pi_{Q},\mathbf{d}} , then 
 

 
 
 supp ⁡ 𝒯 ⊆ { x ∈ ℳ Π Q , 𝐝 ∣ Υ Π Q x , 𝐝 x ​  does not vanish on  ℐ ¯ Π Q x , Alg , 𝐝 x } ¯ , \operatorname{supp}\mathcal{T}\subseteq\overline{\{x\in\mathcal{M}_{\Pi_{Q},\mathbf{d}}\mid\Upsilon_{\Pi_{Q_{x}},\mathbf{d}_{x}}\text{ does not vanish on $\underline{\mathcal{I}}_{\Pi_{Q_{x}},\mathrm{Alg},\mathbf{d}_{x}}$}\}}, 
 
 
 where the bar indicates the Zariski closure. 
 
 
 
 Proof. 
 
 Since the source and the target of  Υ Π Q , 𝐝 \Upsilon_{\Pi_{Q},\mathbf{d}}  are semisimple mixed Hodge modules, we may prove the lemma in terms of perverse sheaves after applying the functor  𝐫𝐚𝐭 \rat . We let  T T  be an open subset of  supp ⁡ ( 𝒯 ) \operatorname{supp}(\mathcal{T})  such that  𝒯 | T \mathcal{T}_{|T}  is given by a local system shifted by  dim supp ⁡ 𝒯 \dim\operatorname{supp}\mathcal{T} . We let  x ∈ T x\in T . Then, by the commutative diagram Proposition  7.7  and Proposition  7.5 ,  ι 𝒩 ! Υ Π Q x , 𝐝 x \iota_{\mathcal{N}}^{!}\Upsilon_{\Pi_{Q_{x}},\mathbf{d}_{x}}  does not vanish on  ι 𝒩 ! ℐ Π Q x , Alg , 𝐝 x \iota_{\mathcal{N}}^{!}\mathcal{I}_{\Pi_{Q_{x}},\mathrm{Alg},\mathbf{d}_{x}}  and therefore,  Υ Π Q x , 𝐝 x \Upsilon_{\Pi_{Q_{x}},\mathbf{d}_{x}}  does not vanish on  ℐ Π Q x , Alg , 𝐝 x \mathcal{I}_{\Pi_{Q_{x}},\mathrm{Alg},\mathbf{d}_{x}} . This completes the proof of the lemma.
∎ 
 
 
 
 Proposition 7.13 . 
 
 The morphism  Υ 𝒜 : Free ⊡ -Alg ⁡ ( 𝒢 ¯ 𝒜 ) → ℬ ​ 𝒫 ​ 𝒮 ¯ 𝒜 , Alg ψ \Upsilon_{\mathcal{A}}\colon\operatorname{Free}_{\boxdot\sAlg}(\underline{\mathscr{G}}_{\mathcal{A}})\rightarrow\underline{\mathcal{BPS}}_{\mathcal{A},\mathrm{Alg}}^{\psi}  factors through  Free ⊡ -Alg ⁡ ( 𝒢 ¯ 𝒜 ) → Free ⊡ -Alg ⁡ ( 𝒢 ¯ 𝒜 ) / ℐ ¯ 𝒜 , Alg \operatorname{Free}_{\boxdot\sAlg}(\underline{\mathscr{G}}_{\mathcal{A}})\rightarrow\operatorname{Free}_{\boxdot\sAlg}(\underline{\mathscr{G}}_{\mathcal{A}})/\underline{\mathcal{I}}_{\mathcal{A},\mathrm{Alg}} . 
 
 
 
 Proof. 
 
 Step 1: Reduction to preprojective algebras of quivers 
 
 
 If there exists a simple mixed Hodge module  ℱ ¯ ⊆ ℐ 𝒜 , Alg \underline{\mathscr{F}}\subseteq\mathcal{I}_{\mathcal{A},\mathrm{Alg}}  such that the restriction of  Υ 𝒜 \Upsilon_{\mathcal{A}}  to  ℱ ¯ \underline{\mathscr{F}}  is non-zero, then we let  x x  be a point such that  ı x ! ℱ ¯ ≠ 0 \imath_{x}^{!}\underline{\mathscr{F}}\neq 0  where  ı x : { x } → ℳ 𝒜 \imath_{x}\colon\{x\}\rightarrow\mathcal{M}_{\mathcal{A}}  is the inclusion  [ DHS22 , Corollary 12.7] . The point  x x  corresponds to a semisimple object  ℱ = ⨁ j = 1 r ℱ ⊕ m j \mathcal{F}=\bigoplus_{j=1}^{r}\mathcal{F}^{\oplus m_{j}} . We let  ℱ ¯ = { ℱ 1 , … ​ ℱ r } \underline{\mathcal{F}}=\{\mathcal{F}_{1},\ldots\mathcal{F}_{r}\}  be the corresponding collection of simple objects of  𝒜 \mathcal{A} . We let  ι ℱ ¯ \iota_{\underline{\mathcal{F}}}  be as in diagram ( 7.1 ). Then,  ι ℱ ¯ ! Υ 𝒜 \iota_{\underline{\mathcal{F}}}^{!}\Upsilon_{\mathcal{A}}  does not vanish on  ι ℱ ¯ ! ℱ ¯ \iota_{\underline{\mathcal{F}}}^{!}\underline{\mathscr{F}} . But, by Proposition  7.7 , if  Q ℱ ¯ Q_{\underline{\mathcal{F}}}  is a quiver such that its double  Q ℱ ¯ ¯ \overline{Q_{\underline{\mathcal{F}}}}  is the Ext-quiver of  ℱ ¯ = { ℱ 1 , … , ℱ r } \underline{\mathcal{F}}=\{\mathcal{F}_{1},\ldots,\mathcal{F}_{r}\} ,  ι 𝒩 ! Υ Π Q ℱ ¯ \iota_{\mathcal{N}}^{!}\Upsilon_{\Pi_{Q_{\underline{\mathcal{F}}}}}  does not vanish on  ι 𝒩 ! ℐ ¯ Π Q ℱ ¯ , Alg \iota_{\mathcal{N}}^{!}\underline{\mathcal{I}}_{\Pi_{Q_{\underline{\mathcal{F}}}},\mathrm{Alg}} . This implies that  Υ Π Q ℱ ¯ \Upsilon_{\Pi_{Q_{\underline{\mathcal{F}}}}}  does not vanish on  ℐ ¯ Π Q ℱ ¯ , Alg \underline{\mathcal{I}}_{\Pi_{Q_{\underline{\mathcal{F}}}},\mathrm{Alg}} . So if we can prove Proposition  7.13  for  𝒜 = Rep ⁡ Π Q \mathcal{A}=\operatorname{Rep}\Pi_{Q}  for any quiver  Q Q , it is true for any Abelian category  𝒜 \mathcal{A}  satisfying the assumptions of § 4 . 
 
 
 Step 2: Proof for preprojective algebras of quivers 
We turn to the proof of Proposition  7.13  for  𝒜 = Rep ⁡ ( Π Q ) \mathcal{A}=\operatorname{Rep}(\Pi_{Q}) . 
 
 
 We prove by induction the following claim. 
 
 
 Claim 1:  For every quiver  Q Q  and dimension vector  𝐝 ∈ 𝐍 Q 0 \mathbf{d}\in\mathbf{N}^{Q_{0}}  supported on the whole of  Q Q , the morphism 
 

 
 
 Υ Π Q , 𝐝 : ( Free ⊡ -Alg ⁡ ( ⨁ 𝐟 ∈ Φ Π Q + 𝒢 ¯ Π Q , 𝐟 ) ) 𝐝 ⟶ ℋ 0 ​ ( 𝙹𝙷 𝐝 , ∗ ⁡ 𝔻 ​ 𝐐 ¯ 𝔐 Π Q , 𝐝 vir ) ψ = ℬ ​ 𝒫 ​ 𝒮 ¯ Π Q , Alg , 𝐝 ψ \Upsilon_{\Pi_{Q},\mathbf{d}}\colon\bigg(\operatorname{Free}_{\boxdot\sAlg}\bigg(\bigoplus_{\mathbf{f}\in\Phi^{+}_{\Pi_{Q}}}\underline{\mathscr{G}}_{\Pi_{Q},\mathbf{f}}\bigg)\bigg)_{\mathbf{d}}\longrightarrow\mathcal{H}^{0}(\JH_{\mathbf{d},\ast}\mathbb{D}\underline{\mathbf{Q}}_{\mathfrak{M}_{\Pi_{Q},\mathbf{d}}}^{\mathrm{vir}})^{\psi}=\underline{\mathcal{BPS}}_{\Pi_{Q},\mathrm{Alg},\mathbf{d}}^{\psi} 
 
 
 factors through the quotient  Free ⊡ -Alg ⁡ ( 𝒢 ¯ Π Q ) 𝐝 → Free ⊡ -Alg ⁡ ( 𝒢 ¯ Π Q ) 𝐝 / ℐ ¯ Π Q , Alg , 𝐝 \operatorname{Free}_{\boxdot\sAlg}(\underline{\mathscr{G}}_{\Pi_{Q}})_{\mathbf{d}}\rightarrow\operatorname{Free}_{\boxdot\sAlg}(\underline{\mathscr{G}}_{\Pi_{Q}})_{\mathbf{d}}/\underline{\mathcal{I}}_{\Pi_{Q},\mathrm{Alg},\mathbf{d}} . 
 
 
 We let  QuivDim \mathrm{QuivDim}  be the set of pairs  ( Q , 𝐝 ) (Q,\mathbf{d})  of a quiver  Q = ( Q 0 , Q 1 ) Q=(Q_{0},Q_{1})  and a dimension vector  𝐝 ∈ 𝐍 Q 0 \mathbf{d}\in\mathbf{N}^{Q_{0}}  supported on the whole of  Q Q . We define 
 

 
 
 μ : QuivDim → 𝐙 > 0 × 𝐙 < 0 ( Q , 𝐝 ) ↦ ( | 𝐝 | , − | Q 0 | ) = ( ∑ i ∈ Q 0 d i , − | Q 0 | ) . \begin{matrix}\mu\colon&\mathrm{QuivDim}&\rightarrow&\mathbf{Z}_{>0}\times\mathbf{Z}_{<0}&\\
&(Q,\mathbf{d})&\mapsto&(\lvert\mathbf{d}\rvert,-\lvert Q_{0}\rvert)&=\left(\sum_{i\in Q_{0}}d_{i},-\lvert Q_{0}\rvert\right).\end{matrix} 
 
 
 We prove the claim by induction on  QuivDim \mathrm{QuivDim}  with respect to the partial order given by the pull-back by  μ \mu  of the lexicographical order on  𝐙 > 0 × 𝐙 < 0 \mathbf{Z}_{>0}\times\mathbf{Z}_{<0} . More precisely, we have 
 

 
 
 ( Q , 𝐝 ) ⩽ ( Q ′ , 𝐝 ′ ) ⇔ { | 𝐝 | ⩽ | 𝐝 ′ | or  ​ | 𝐝 | = | 𝐝 ′ | ​  and  − | Q 0 | ⩽ − | Q 0 ′ | . (Q,\mathbf{d})\leqslant(Q^{\prime},\mathbf{d}^{\prime})\iff\left\{\begin{aligned} &\lvert\mathbf{d}\rvert\leqslant\lvert\mathbf{d^{\prime}}\rvert\\
&\text{or }\lvert\mathbf{d}\rvert=\lvert\mathbf{d^{\prime}}\rvert\text{ and }-\lvert Q_{0}\rvert\leqslant-\lvert Q_{0}^{\prime}\rvert.\end{aligned}\right. 
 
 
 
 
 We do this by proving the following claim. 
 
 
 Claim 2:  If  Υ Π Q x ′ , 𝐦 x \Upsilon_{\Pi_{Q^{\prime}_{x}},\mathbf{m}_{x}}  vanishes on  ℐ ¯ Π Q x ′ , Alg , 𝐝 x \underline{\mathcal{I}}_{\Pi_{Q^{\prime}_{x}},\mathrm{Alg},\mathbf{d}_{x}}  for all half Ext quivers with dimension vectors  ( Q x ′ , 𝐦 x ) ∈ QuivDim (Q^{\prime}_{x},\mathbf{m}_{x})\in\mathrm{QuivDim}  for closed points  x ∈ ℳ Π Q , 𝐝 x\in\mathcal{M}_{\Pi_{Q},\mathbf{d}}  such that  μ ⁡ ( Q x ′ , 𝐦 x ) < μ ⁡ ( Q , 𝐝 ) \mu(Q^{\prime}_{x},\mathbf{m}_{x})<\mu(Q,\mathbf{d}) , then  Υ Π Q , 𝐝 \Upsilon_{\Pi_{Q},\mathbf{d}}  vanishes on  ℐ ¯ Π Q , Alg , 𝐝 \underline{\mathcal{I}}_{\Pi_{Q},\mathrm{Alg},\mathbf{d}} .
Indeed, suppose  Υ Π Q , 𝐝 \Upsilon_{\Pi_{Q},\mathbf{d}}  does not vanish on  ℐ ¯ Π Q , Alg , 𝐝 \underline{\mathcal{I}}_{\Pi_{Q},\mathrm{Alg},\mathbf{d}} . Let  𝒯 ¯ ⊆ ℐ ¯ Π Q , Alg , 𝐝 \underline{\mathcal{T}}\subseteq\underline{\mathcal{I}}_{\Pi_{Q},\mathrm{Alg},\mathbf{d}}  be a simple direct summand such that the restriction of  Υ Π Q , 𝐝 \Upsilon_{\Pi_{Q},\mathbf{d}}  to  𝒯 ¯ \underline{\mathcal{T}}  is nonzero. 
 
 
 We have the following chain of inclusions 
 

 
 
 supp ⁡ ( 𝒯 ¯ ) \displaystyle\operatorname{supp}(\underline{\mathcal{T}}) 
 ⊆ { x ∈ ℳ Π Q , 𝐝 ∣ Υ Π x , 𝐦 x ​  does not vanish on  ℐ ¯ Π Q x , Alg , 𝐝 x } ¯ \displaystyle\subseteq\overline{\{x\in\mathcal{M}_{\Pi_{Q},\mathbf{d}}\mid\Upsilon_{\Pi_{x},\mathbf{m}_{x}}\text{ does not vanish on $\underline{\mathcal{I}}_{\Pi_{Q_{x}},\mathrm{Alg},\mathbf{d}_{x}}$}\}} 
    (by Lemma  7.12 ) 
 
 
 
 
 ⊆ { x ∈ ℳ Π Q , 𝐝 ∣ μ ⁡ ( Q x , 𝐦 x ) = μ ⁡ ( Q , 𝐝 ) } ¯ \displaystyle\subseteq\overline{\{x\in\mathcal{M}_{\Pi_{Q},\mathbf{d}}\mid\mu(Q_{x},\mathbf{m}_{x})=\mu(Q,\mathbf{d})\}} 
    (by hypothesis and  [ DHS22 , Lemma 11.10] ) 
 
 
 
 
 = 𝔾 a L ¯ ⋅ 0 𝐝 \displaystyle=\mathbb{G}_{a}^{\overline{L}}\cdot 0_{\mathbf{d}} 
    (by  [ DHS22 , Lemma 11.10] ). 
 
 
 In words, the hypothesis guarantees that the support of  𝒯 ¯ \underline{\mathcal{T}}  is contained in the  𝔾 a L ¯ \mathbb{G}_{a}^{\overline{L}} -orbit  Z ¯ = 𝔾 a L ¯ ⋅ 0 𝐝 ≅ 𝐂 L ¯ \overline{Z}=\mathbb{G}_{a}^{\overline{L}}\cdot 0_{\mathbf{d}}\cong\mathbf{C}^{\overline{L}}  of  0 𝐝 0_{\mathbf{d}} .
Since  𝒯 ¯ \underline{\mathcal{T}}  is a  𝔾 a L ¯ \mathbb{G}_{a}^{\overline{L}} -equivariant mixed Hodge module (Lemma  7.8 ), its support is  𝔾 a L ¯ \mathbb{G}_{a}^{\overline{L}} -stable and hence equal to the entire orbit  Z ¯ \overline{Z} . 
 
 
 The groups  𝔾 a L ¯ \mathbb{G}_{a}^{\overline{L}}  and  𝔾 a L \mathbb{G}_{a}^{L}  are contractible, and so are any of their orbits. Therefore taking total cohomology induces equivalences
 Perv 𝔾 a L ¯ ⁡ ( Z ¯ ) ≃ Vect ⁡ ( 𝐐 ) ≃ Perv 𝔾 a L ⁡ ( Z ) \operatorname{Perv}_{\mathbb{G}_{a}^{\overline{L}}}(\overline{Z})\simeq\mathrm{Vect}(\mathbf{Q})\simeq\operatorname{Perv}_{\mathbb{G}_{a}^{L}}(Z)  with the category of  𝐐 \mathbf{Q} -vector spaces, where  Z Z  is the  𝔾 a L \mathbb{G}_{a}^{L} -orbit of  0 𝐝 0_{\mathbf{d}} . Let  ı 𝒮 ​ 𝒮 ​ 𝒩 : ℳ Π Q , 𝐝 𝒮 ​ 𝒮 ​ 𝒩 ↪ ℳ Π Q , 𝐝 \imath_{\mathcal{SSN}}\colon\mathcal{M}_{\Pi_{Q},\mathbf{d}}^{\mathcal{SSN}}\hookrightarrow\mathcal{M}_{\Pi_{Q},\mathbf{d}}  be the inclusion.
We have a cartesian square 
 

 
 
 𝐂 L ≅ Z {\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathbf{C}^{L}\cong Z} ℳ Π Q , 𝐝 𝒮 ​ 𝒮 ​ 𝒩 {\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{M}_{\Pi_{Q},\mathbf{d}}^{\mathcal{SSN}}} 𝐂 L ¯ ≅ Z ¯ {\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathbf{C}^{\overline{L}}\cong\overline{Z}} ℳ Π Q , 𝐝 . {\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{M}_{\Pi_{Q},\mathbf{d}}.} 
 
 
 Since  𝒯 ¯ \underline{\mathcal{T}}  is simple, we have  𝒯 ≔ 𝐫𝐚𝐭 ⁡ ( 𝒯 ¯ ) = 𝐐 𝔾 a L ¯ ​ [ 2 ​ | L | ] \mathcal{T}\coloneqq\rat(\underline{\mathcal{T}})=\mathbf{Q}_{\mathbb{G}_{a}^{\overline{L}}}[2\lvert L\rvert]  and  ı 𝒮 ​ 𝒮 ​ 𝒩 , 𝐝 ! 𝐫𝐚𝐭 ( 𝒯 ) = 𝐐 𝔾 a L \imath_{\mathcal{SSN},\mathbf{d}}^{!}\rat(\mathcal{T})=\mathbf{Q}_{\mathbb{G}_{a}^{L}} . 
 
 
 Hence  H 0 ( ı 𝒮 ​ 𝒮 ​ 𝒩 , 𝐝 ! 𝒯 ) ≅ 𝐐 \HO^{0}({\imath_{\mathcal{SSN},\mathbf{d}}^{!}\mathcal{T}})\cong\mathbf{Q}  is a summand of  H 0 ( ı 𝒮 ​ 𝒮 ​ 𝒩 ! ℐ Π Q , 𝐝 ) \HO^{0}(\imath_{\mathcal{SSN}}^{!}\mathcal{I}_{\Pi_{Q},\mathbf{d}}) , which contradicts Corollary  7.11 . This finishes the proof.
∎ 
 
 
 
 We let 
 

 
 
 Υ ¯ 𝒜 : Free ⊡ -Alg ⁡ ( 𝒢 ¯ 𝒜 ) / ℐ ¯ 𝒜 , Alg → ℬ ​ 𝒫 ​ 𝒮 ¯ 𝒜 , Alg \overline{\Upsilon}_{\mathcal{A}}\colon\operatorname{Free}_{\boxdot\sAlg}(\underline{\mathscr{G}}_{\mathcal{A}})/\underline{\mathcal{I}}_{\mathcal{A},\mathrm{Alg}}\rightarrow\underline{\mathcal{BPS}}_{\mathcal{A},\mathrm{Alg}} 
 
 
 be the morphism obtained using Proposition  7.13 . 
 
 
 We now have the generalisation to arbitrary quivers of  [ DHS22 , Corollary 10.3] . 
 
 
 Corollary 7.14 . 
 
 Let  Q Q  be a quiver. The morphism 
 

 
 
 H 0 ( ı 𝒮 ​ 𝒮 ​ 𝒩 ! Υ ¯ Π Q ) : H 0 ( ı 𝒮 ​ 𝒮 ​ 𝒩 ! ( Free ⊡ -Alg ( 𝒢 ¯ Π Q ) / ℐ ¯ Π Q , Alg ) ) → H 0 ( 𝒜 ¯ Π Q 𝒮 ​ 𝒮 ​ 𝒩 , ψ ) \HO^{0}(\imath_{\mathcal{SSN}}^{!}\overline{\Upsilon}_{\Pi_{Q}})\colon\HO^{0}\!(\imath_{\mathcal{SSN}}^{!}(\operatorname{Free}_{\boxdot\sAlg}(\underline{\mathscr{G}}_{\Pi_{Q}})/\underline{\mathcal{I}}_{\Pi_{Q},\mathrm{Alg}}))\rightarrow\HO^{0}(\underline{\mathscr{A}}_{\Pi_{Q}}^{\mathcal{SSN},\psi}) 
 
 
 is an isomorphism. 
 
 
 
 Proof. 
 
 We may prove the corollary after applying the functor  𝐫𝐚𝐭 \rat . By strict monoidality of  ı 𝒮 ​ 𝒮 ​ 𝒩 ! \imath_{\mathcal{SSN}}^{!}  and  H 0 \HO^{0}  applied to complexes with vanishing derived global sections in negative degrees, we have 
 

 
 
 H 0 ( ı 𝒮 ​ 𝒮 ​ 𝒩 ! ( Free ⊡ -Alg ( 𝒢 Π Q ) / ℐ Π Q , Alg ) ) ≅ H 0 ( ı 𝒮 ​ 𝒮 ​ 𝒩 ! ( Free ⊡ -Alg ( 𝒢 Π Q ) ) ) / H 0 ( ı 𝒮 ​ 𝒮 ​ 𝒩 ! ( ℐ Π Q , Alg ) ) . \HO^{0}\!(\imath_{\mathcal{SSN}}^{!}(\operatorname{Free}_{\boxdot\sAlg}(\mathscr{G}_{\Pi_{Q}})/\mathcal{I}_{\Pi_{Q},\mathrm{Alg}}))\cong\HO^{0}\!(\imath_{\mathcal{SSN}}^{!}(\operatorname{Free}_{\boxdot\sAlg}(\mathscr{G}_{\Pi_{Q}})))/\HO^{0}(\imath_{\mathcal{SSN}}^{!}(\mathcal{I}_{\Pi_{Q},\mathrm{Alg}})). 
 
 
 By Corollary  7.11 , this can again be identified with  Free ( ⨁ i ∈ I ∞ 𝐐 e i ) / H 0 ( ı 𝒮 ​ 𝒮 ​ 𝒩 ! ℐ Π Q , Alg ) \operatorname{Free}(\bigoplus_{i\in I_{\infty}}\mathbf{Q}e_{i})/\HO^{0}(\imath_{\mathcal{SSN}}^{!}\mathcal{I}_{\Pi_{Q},\mathrm{Alg}}) . By the proof of Corollary  7.11 ,  H 0 ( ı 𝒮 ​ 𝒮 ​ 𝒩 ! ℐ Π Q , Alg ) \HO^{0}(\imath_{\mathcal{SSN}}^{!}\mathcal{I}_{\Pi_{Q},\mathrm{Alg}})  is the ideal of Serre relations of  e i e_{i}  as defined in § 7.6.1 . The morphism  H 0 ( ı 𝒮 ​ 𝒮 ​ 𝒩 ! Υ ¯ Π Q ) \HO^{0}(\imath_{\mathcal{SSN}}^{!}\overline{\Upsilon}_{\Pi_{Q}})  can be identified with the morphism of Theorem  7.9 : it is an isomorphism.
∎ 
 
 
 
 
 7.7.2.  Proof of Theorem  1.1 

 
 Theorem  1.1  can now be proven using the same inductive argument as the proof of Proposition  7.13  applied to the morphism  Υ ¯ 𝒜 : Free ⊡ -Alg ⁡ ( 𝒢 ¯ 𝒜 ) / ℐ ¯ 𝒜 , Alg → ℬ ​ 𝒫 ​ 𝒮 ¯ 𝒜 , Alg ψ \overline{\Upsilon}_{\mathcal{A}}\colon\operatorname{Free}_{\boxdot\sAlg}(\underline{\mathscr{G}}_{\mathcal{A}})/\underline{\mathcal{I}}_{\mathcal{A},\mathrm{Alg}}\rightarrow\underline{\mathcal{BPS}}_{\mathcal{A},\mathrm{Alg}}^{\psi} . As for the proof of Proposition  7.13 , we first reduce to the case of quivers and then induct on the set  QuivDim \mathrm{QuivDim}  defined in § 7.7.1 . We first reduce to showing the following claim. 
 
 
 Claim 1: 
If  Υ ¯ Π Q \overline{\Upsilon}_{\Pi_{Q}}  is an isomorphism for any quiver  Q Q , then  Υ ¯ 𝒜 \overline{\Upsilon}_{\mathcal{A}}  is an isomorphism for any  2 2 -Calabi–Yau category as in § 4 . 
 
 
 To prove this, we take a direct summand  𝒯 ¯ ⊆ 𝒦 ¯ ⊕ 𝒞 ¯ \underline{\mathcal{T}}\subseteq\underline{\mathcal{K}}\oplus\underline{\mathcal{C}}  where  𝒦 ¯ = ker ⁡ Υ ¯ 𝒜 \underline{\mathcal{K}}=\ker\overline{\Upsilon}_{\mathcal{A}}  and  𝒞 ¯ = coker ⁡ Υ ¯ 𝒜 \underline{\mathcal{C}}=\operatorname{coker}\overline{\Upsilon}_{\mathcal{A}} . If  𝒯 ¯ \underline{\mathcal{T}}  is non-zero, we take  x ∈ supp ⁡ 𝒯 ¯ x\in\operatorname{supp}\underline{\mathcal{T}}  such that for  ı x : { x } → ℳ 𝒜 \imath_{x}\colon\{x\}\rightarrow\mathcal{M}_{\mathcal{A}} ,  ı x ! 𝒯 ¯ ≠ 0 \imath_{x}^{!}\underline{\mathcal{T}}\neq 0 . We then prove as in  [ DHS22 ]  that  Υ Π Q x \Upsilon_{\Pi_{Q_{x}}}  is not an isomorphism, where  Q x Q_{x}  is defined in § 4.6.1  as a half of the Ext-quiver of the collection  ℱ ¯ \underline{\mathcal{F}}  of simple summands of the semisimple object of  𝒜 \mathcal{A}  corresponding to  x x . More specifically, we have the following commutative diagram obtained using Proposition  7.7  and Proposition  7.5  and the strict monoidality of  ı 𝒩 ! \imath_{\mathcal{N}}^{!} ,  ı ℱ ¯ ! \imath_{\underline{\mathcal{F}}}^{!} : 
 

 
 
 ι 𝒩 ! ( Free ⊡ -Alg ( 𝒢 ¯ Π Q ℱ ¯ ) / ℐ ¯ Π Q , Alg ) {\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\iota_{\mathcal{N}}^{!}(\operatorname{Free}_{\boxdot\sAlg}(\underline{\mathscr{G}}_{\Pi_{Q_{\underline{\mathcal{F}}}}})/\underline{\mathcal{I}}_{\Pi_{Q},\mathrm{Alg}})}} ι 𝒩 ! ℬ ​ 𝒫 ​ 𝒮 ¯ Π Q ℱ ¯ , Alg ψ {\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\iota_{\mathcal{N}}^{!}\underline{\mathcal{BPS}}_{\Pi_{Q_{\underline{\mathcal{F}}}},\mathrm{Alg}}^{\psi}}} ι ℱ ¯ ! ( Free ⊡ -Alg ( 𝒢 ¯ 𝒜 ) / ℐ ¯ 𝒜 , Alg ) {\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\iota_{\underline{\mathcal{F}}}^{!}(\operatorname{Free}_{\boxdot\sAlg}(\underline{\mathscr{G}}_{\mathcal{A}})/\underline{\mathcal{I}}_{\mathcal{A},\mathrm{Alg}})}} ι ℱ ¯ ! ℬ ​ 𝒫 ​ 𝒮 ¯ 𝒜 ψ {\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\iota_{\underline{\mathcal{F}}}^{!}\underline{\mathcal{BPS}}_{\mathcal{A}}^{\psi}}} ı ℱ ¯ ! Υ ¯ 𝒜 \scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\imath_{\underline{\mathcal{F}}}^{!}\overline{\Upsilon}_{\mathcal{A}}} ı 𝒩 ! Υ ¯ Π Q x \scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\imath_{\mathcal{N}}^{!}\overline{\Upsilon}_{\Pi_{Q_{x}}}} 
 
 
 By Propositions  7.5  and  7.7 , both vertical arrows are isomorphisms. It follows that the bottom horizontal arrow  ı ℱ ¯ ! Υ ¯ 𝒜 \imath_{\underline{\mathcal{F}}}^{!}\overline{\Upsilon}_{\mathcal{A}}  is an isomorphism if and only if the top horizontal arrow  ı 𝒩 ! Υ ¯ Π Q x \imath_{\mathcal{N}}^{!}\overline{\Upsilon}_{\Pi_{Q_{x}}}  is an isomorphism. Since  ı ℱ ¯ ! 𝒯 ¯ \imath_{\underline{\mathcal{F}}}^{!}\underline{\mathcal{T}}  is in the kernel or cokernel of  ı ℱ ¯ ! Υ ¯ 𝒜 \imath_{\underline{\mathcal{F}}}^{!}\overline{\Upsilon}_{\mathcal{A}} ,  ı 𝒩 ! Υ ¯ Π Q x \imath_{\mathcal{N}}^{!}\overline{\Upsilon}_{\Pi_{Q_{x}}}  is not an isomorphism, and therefore,  Υ ¯ Π Q x \overline{\Upsilon}_{\Pi_{Q_{x}}}  itself is not an isomorphism. This proves Claim 1. 
 
 
 Then, the fact that  Υ ¯ Π Q \overline{\Upsilon}_{\Pi_{Q}}  is an isomorphism for any quiver  Q Q  is proved by induction on  QuivDim \mathrm{QuivDim}  as in Step  2 2  of the proof of Proposition  7.13 , by eventually appealing to Corollary  7.14 . More precisely, we have the following claim whose proof is identical to that of Lemma  7.12 . 
 
 
 Claim 2:  Let  ( Q , 𝐝 ) ∈ QuivDim (Q,\mathbf{d})\in\mathrm{QuivDim}  be such that 
 

 
 
 Υ ¯ Π Q , 𝐝 : ( Free ⊡ -Alg ⁡ ( 𝒢 ¯ Π Q ) / ℐ ¯ Π Q , Alg ) 𝐝 → ℬ ​ 𝒫 ​ 𝒮 ¯ Π Q , Alg , 𝐝 ψ \overline{\Upsilon}_{\Pi_{Q},\mathbf{d}}\colon\left(\operatorname{Free}_{\boxdot\sAlg}(\underline{\mathscr{G}}_{\Pi_{Q}})/\underline{\mathcal{I}}_{\Pi_{Q},\mathrm{Alg}}\right)_{\mathbf{d}}\rightarrow\underline{\mathcal{BPS}}_{\Pi_{Q},\mathrm{Alg},\mathbf{d}}^{\psi} 
 
 
 is not an isomorphism. Then, if  𝒯 ¯ \underline{\mathcal{T}}  is a mixed Hodge module which is a simple summand of  ker ⁡ Υ ¯ Π Q , 𝐝 ⊕ coker ⁡ Υ ¯ Π Q , 𝐝 \ker\overline{\Upsilon}_{\Pi_{Q},\mathbf{d}}\oplus\operatorname{coker}\overline{\Upsilon}_{\Pi_{Q},\mathbf{d}} , then  supp ⁡ 𝒯 ¯ ⊆ { x ∈ ℳ Π Q , 𝐝 ∣ Υ ¯ Π Q x , 𝐝 x ​ is not an isomorphism } ¯ \operatorname{supp}\underline{\mathcal{T}}\subseteq\overline{\{x\in\mathcal{M}_{\Pi_{Q},\mathbf{d}}\mid\overline{\Upsilon}_{\Pi_{Q_{x}},\mathbf{d}_{x}}\text{is not an isomorphism}\}} . Then, we have the claim whose proof is the same as the one for Claim 2 of the Step 2 of the proof of Proposition  7.13 : 
 
 
 Claim 3:  If  Υ ¯ Π Q x ′ , 𝐦 x \overline{\Upsilon}_{\Pi_{Q^{\prime}_{x}},\mathbf{m}_{x}}  is an isomorphism for all half Ext-quivers with dimension vector  ( Q x ′ , 𝐦 x ) ∈ QuivDim (Q^{\prime}_{x},\mathbf{m}_{x})\in\mathrm{QuivDim}  for closed points  x ∈ ℳ Π Q , 𝐝 x\in\mathcal{M}_{\Pi_{Q},\mathbf{d}}  such that  μ ⁡ ( Q x , 𝐦 x ) < μ ⁡ ( Q , 𝐝 ) \mu(Q_{x},\mathbf{m}_{x})<\mu(Q,\mathbf{d}) , then  Υ ¯ Π Q , 𝐝 \overline{\Upsilon}_{\Pi_{Q},\mathbf{d}}  is an isomorphism. 
 
 
 This proof implies by induction that for any  𝐝 ∈ 𝐍 Q 0 \mathbf{d}\in\mathbf{N}^{Q_{0}} ,  Υ ¯ Π Q , 𝐝 \overline{\Upsilon}_{\Pi_{Q},\mathbf{d}}  is an isomorphism, finishing the proof of Theorem  1.1 .
∎ 
 
 
 
 7.7.3.  The BPS Lie algebra 

 
 In § 7.7.2 , we proved Theorem  1.1 , that is that we have an isomorphism in  MHM ⁡ ( ℳ 𝒜 ) \mathrm{MHM}(\mathcal{M}_{\mathcal{A}}) 
 

 
 
 ℬ ​ 𝒫 ​ 𝒮 ¯ 𝒜 , Alg ψ ≅ 𝐔 ⁡ ( 𝔫 ℳ 𝒜 , 𝒢 ¯ 𝒜 + ) \underline{\mathcal{BPS}}_{\mathcal{A},\mathrm{Alg}}^{\psi}\cong\UEA(\mathfrak{n}^{+}_{\mathcal{M}_{\mathcal{A}},\underline{\mathscr{G}}_{\mathcal{A}}}) 
 
 
 where  𝒢 ¯ 𝒜 \underline{\mathscr{G}}_{\mathcal{A}}  is as in § 6.4.3 , motivating the following definition: 
 
 
 Definition 7.15 . 
 
 Let  𝒜 \mathcal{A}  be a 2-Calabi–Yau category satisfying our assumptions  1 – 6  in § 4.3  and § 4.4 . The BPS  Lie  algebra sheaf of  𝒜 \mathcal{A}  is 
 

 
 
 ℬ ​ 𝒫 ​ 𝒮 ¯ 𝒜 , Lie ≔ 𝔫 ℳ 𝒜 , 𝒢 ¯ 𝒜 + . \underline{\mathcal{BPS}}_{\mathcal{A},\mathrm{Lie}}\coloneqq\mathfrak{n}^{+}_{\mathcal{M}_{\mathcal{A}},\underline{\mathscr{G}}_{\mathcal{A}}}. 
 
 
 In words, this is the generalised half Kac–Moody Lie algebra object in the tensor category of mixed Hodge modules on the coarse moduli space (as defined in § 3.5 ), with generating mixed Hodge modules defined in § 6.4.3 .
If  ı △ : ℳ 𝒜 △ ↪ ℳ 𝒜 \imath_{\triangle}\colon\mathcal{M}_{\mathcal{A}}^{\triangle}\hookrightarrow\mathcal{M}_{\mathcal{A}}  is the inclusion of a saturated monoid (in schemes) corresponding to a Serre subcategory, we define 
 

 
 
 𝔫 ¯ 𝒜 △ , + ≔ H ∗ ( ı △ ! 𝔫 ℳ 𝒜 , 𝒢 ¯ 𝒜 + ) \underline{\mathfrak{n}}_{\mathcal{A}}^{\triangle,+}\coloneqq\HO^{*}(\imath_{\triangle}^{!}\mathfrak{n}^{+}_{\mathcal{M}_{\mathcal{A}},\underline{\mathscr{G}}_{\mathcal{A}}}) 
 
 
 and in particular  𝔫 ¯ 𝒜 + ≔ H ∗ ⁡ ( 𝔫 ℳ 𝒜 , 𝒢 ¯ 𝒜 ) \underline{\mathfrak{n}}_{\mathcal{A}}^{+}\coloneqq\HO^{*}(\mathfrak{n}_{\mathcal{M}_{\mathcal{A}},\underline{\mathscr{G}}_{\mathcal{A}}}) . We denote by  𝔤 𝒜 △ ⊃ 𝔫 𝒜 △ , + \mathfrak{g}^{\triangle}_{\mathcal{A}}\supset\mathfrak{n}_{\mathcal{A}}^{\triangle,+}  the extension to the generalised Kac–Moody Lie algebra as defined in Theorem  1.2 . 
 
 
 
 
 
 7.8.  Verdier duality and the compactly supported CoHA 

 
 Corollary 7.16 . 
 
 The BPS algebra sheaf  ℬ ​ 𝒫 ​ 𝒮 ¯ 𝒜 , Alg \underline{\mathcal{BPS}}_{\mathcal{A},\mathrm{Alg}}  and the BPS Lie algebra sheaf  ℬ ​ 𝒫 ​ 𝒮 ¯ 𝒜 , Lie \underline{\mathcal{BPS}}_{\mathcal{A},\mathrm{Lie}}  are Verdier self-dual mixed Hodge modules on  ℳ 𝒜 \mathcal{M}_{\mathcal{A}} . More precisely, each simple direct summand of  ℬ ​ 𝒫 ​ 𝒮 ¯ 𝒜 , Alg \underline{\mathcal{BPS}}_{\mathcal{A},\mathrm{Alg}}  (and hence of  ℬ ​ 𝒫 ​ 𝒮 ¯ 𝒜 , Lie \underline{\mathcal{BPS}}_{\mathcal{A},\mathrm{Lie}} ) is Verdier self-dual. 
 
 
 
 Proof. 
 
 By Theorem  1.1 , the relative BPS algebra is a quotient of  Free ⊡ -Alg ⁡ ( 𝒢 ¯ 𝒜 ) \operatorname{Free}_{\boxdot\sAlg}(\underline{\mathscr{G}}_{\mathcal{A}})  and so (by finiteness of  ⊕ \oplus  which gives the commutation of  ⊡ \boxdot  with Verdier duality), it suffices to prove the Verdier self-duality of each simple direct summand of  Free ⊡ -Alg ⁡ ( 𝒢 ¯ 𝒜 ) \operatorname{Free}_{\boxdot\sAlg}(\underline{\mathscr{G}}_{\mathcal{A}}) . Such a simple direct summand appears in the pushforward of the  ℐ ​ 𝒞 ¯ \underline{\mathcal{IC}}  mixed Hodge module by the finite map  ⊕ ′ : ∏ i = 1 r ℳ 𝒜 , m i × ∏ j = 1 s ℳ 𝒜 , n j → ℳ 𝒜 \oplus^{\prime}\colon\prod_{i=1}^{r}\mathcal{M}_{\mathcal{A},m_{i}}\times\prod_{j=1}^{s}\mathcal{M}_{\mathcal{A},n_{j}}\rightarrow\mathcal{M}_{\mathcal{A}} ,  ( ( x i ) 1 ⩽ i ⩽ r , ( y j ) 1 ⩽ j ⩽ s ) ↦ ⨁ i = 1 r x i l i ⊕ ⨁ j = 1 s y j ((x_{i})_{1\leqslant i\leqslant r},(y_{j})_{1\leqslant j\leqslant s})\mapsto\bigoplus_{i=1}^{r}x_{i}^{l_{i}}\oplus\bigoplus_{j=1}^{s}y_{j} , where  m i ∈ Σ K ⁡ ( 𝒜 ) ∩ Φ K ⁡ ( 𝒜 ) + , iso m_{i}\in\Sigma_{\K(\mathcal{A})}\cap\Phi^{+,\mathrm{iso}}_{\K(\mathcal{A})} ,  n j ∈ Σ K ⁡ ( 𝒜 ) ∖ ( Σ K ⁡ ( 𝒜 ) ∩ Φ K ⁡ ( 𝒜 ) + , iso ) n_{j}\in\Sigma_{\K(\mathcal{A})}\smallsetminus(\Sigma_{\K(\mathcal{A})}\cap\Phi^{+,\mathrm{iso}}_{\K(\mathcal{A})})  and  l i ∈ 𝐙 ⩾ 1 l_{i}\in\mathbf{Z}_{\geqslant 1} . The map  ⊕ ′ \oplus^{\prime}  is small and is a covering over an open subspace of its image with structure group a product of symmetric groups. The corollary then follows from the fact that all representations of symmetric groups are self-dual.
∎ 
 
 
 
 Recall the class map  cl : ℳ 𝒜 → K ⁡ ( 𝒜 ) \mathrm{cl}\colon\mathcal{M}_{\mathcal{A}}\rightarrow\K(\mathcal{A})  (§ 6.3.1 ). The absolute CoHA can be seen as  cl ∗ ​ 𝒜 ¯ 𝒜 ∈ 𝒟 + ​ ( MHM ⁡ ( K ⁡ ( 𝒜 ) ) ) \mathrm{cl}_{*}\underline{\mathscr{A}}_{\mathcal{A}}\in\mathcal{D}^{+}(\mathrm{MHM}(\K(\mathcal{A})))  endowed with the multiplication  cl ∗ ​ 𝔪 \mathrm{cl}_{*}\mathfrak{m} . We also consider the variant using the functor  cl ! \mathrm{cl}_{!} . By the properness of  ⊕ \oplus  (implying  ⊕ ∗ = ⊕ ! \oplus_{*}=\oplus_{!} ),  cl ! \mathrm{cl}_{!}  gives a monoidal functor  cl : 𝒟 + ​ ( MHM ⁡ ( ℳ 𝒜 ) ) → 𝒟 + ​ ( MHM ⁡ ( K ⁡ ( 𝒜 ) ) ) \mathrm{cl}\colon\mathcal{D}^{+}(\mathrm{MHM}(\mathcal{M}_{\mathcal{A}}))\rightarrow\mathcal{D}^{+}(\mathrm{MHM}(\K(\mathcal{A}))) . We can then consider the  compactly supported CoHA  of  𝒜 \mathcal{A} , defined as  cl ! 𝒜 ¯ 𝒜 \mathrm{cl}_{!}\underline{\mathscr{A}}_{\mathcal{A}} . A subalgebra is given by the compactly supported BPS algebra  cl ! ℬ ​ 𝒫 ​ 𝒮 ¯ 𝒜 , Alg \mathrm{cl}_{!}\underline{\mathcal{BPS}}_{\mathcal{A},\mathrm{Alg}} , and we define  cl ! ℬ ​ 𝒫 ​ 𝒮 ¯ 𝒜 , Lie \mathrm{cl}_{!}\underline{\mathcal{BPS}}_{\mathcal{A},\mathrm{Lie}}  to be the compactly supported BPS Lie algebra. A corollary of Theorems  1.1  and  1.7  is the following. 
 
 
 Corollary 7.17 . 
 
 
 (1)  
 
 The compactly supported BPS algebra  cl ! ℬ 𝒫 𝒮 𝒜 , Alg \mathrm{cl}_{!}\mathcal{BPS}_{\mathcal{A},\mathrm{Alg}}  is isomorphic to  𝐔 ⁡ ( 𝔫 K ⁡ ( 𝒜 ) , ( − , − ) 𝒞 , IP c + ) \UEA(\mathfrak{n}^{+}_{\K(\mathcal{A}),(-,-)_{\mathscr{C}},\mathrm{IP}_{\mathrm{c}}})  where 
 

 
 
 IP c : Φ K ⁡ ( 𝒜 ) + → 𝐍 [ t ± 1 / 2 ] m ↦ IP c ​ ( 𝒢 𝒜 , m ) = ∑ j ∈ 𝐙 dim H j ( 𝒢 𝒜 , m ) t − j / 2 . \begin{matrix}\mathrm{IP}_{\mathrm{c}}&\colon&\Phi^{+}_{\K(\mathcal{A})}&\rightarrow&\mathbf{N}[t^{\pm 1/2}]\\
&&m&\mapsto&\mathrm{IP}_{\mathrm{c}}(\mathscr{G}_{\mathcal{A},m})&=\sum_{j\in\mathbf{Z}}\dim\HO^{j}(\mathscr{G}_{\mathcal{A},m})t^{-j/2}.\end{matrix} 
 
 
 
 (2)  
 
 We have a PBW isomorphism 
 

 
 
 Sym ( 𝔫 K ⁡ ( 𝒜 ) , ( − , − ) 𝒞 , IP c + ⊗ H 𝐂 ∗ ∗ ) → cl ! 𝒜 𝒜 . \Sym\left(\mathfrak{n}^{+}_{\K(\mathcal{A}),(-,-)_{\mathscr{C}},\mathrm{IP}_{\mathrm{c}}}\otimes\HO^{*}_{\mathbf{C}^{*}}\right)\rightarrow\mathrm{cl}_{!}\mathscr{A}_{\mathcal{A}}. 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 Proof. 
 
 Both statements are straightforward consequences of Theorem  1.1 , and the description of the weight function uses that  𝒢 𝒜 , m \mathscr{G}_{\mathcal{A},m}  is Verdier self-dual and so  H c ∗ ⁡ ( 𝒢 𝒜 , m ) ≅ H ∗ ⁡ ( 𝒢 𝒜 , m ) ∗ \HO^{*}_{\mathrm{c}}(\mathscr{G}_{\mathcal{A},m})\cong\HO^{*}(\mathscr{G}_{\mathcal{A},m})^{*}  (the second star in the r.h.s. indicates the graded linear dual). 
 
 
 ∎ 
 
 
 
 
 7.9.  Weyl-group invariance 

 
 Let  𝒜 \mathcal{A}  be a 2-Calabi–Yau category as in Definition  7.15 , and let  𝒢 ¯ 𝒜 ∈ MHM ⁡ ( ℳ 𝒜 ) \underline{\mathscr{G}}_{\mathcal{A}}\in\mathrm{MHM}(\mathcal{M}_{\mathcal{A}})  be defined as above (see § 6.4.3 ). Let  ı △ : ℳ 𝒜 △ ↪ ℳ 𝒜 \imath_{\triangle}\colon\mathcal{M}_{\mathcal{A}}^{\triangle}\hookrightarrow\mathcal{M}_{\mathcal{A}}  be the inclusion of monoids corresponding to a Serre subcategory, and let us assume that if  ℳ 𝒜 , m ⊆ ℳ 𝒜 \mathcal{M}_{\mathcal{A},m}\subseteq\mathcal{M}_{\mathcal{A}}  is a connected component corresponding to a real simple root  m m , then  ℳ 𝒜 , m △ = ℳ 𝒜 , m \mathcal{M}_{\mathcal{A},m}^{\triangle}=\mathcal{M}_{\mathcal{A},m} . It follows that the weight function  P : Φ M , ( − , − ) + → 𝐍 [ t ± 1 / 2 ] P\colon\Phi_{M,(-,-)}^{+}\rightarrow\mathbf{N}[t^{\pm 1/2}]  defining the generalised Kac–Moody Lie algebra  𝔤 𝒜 △ \mathfrak{g}^{\triangle}_{\mathcal{A}}  satisfies  P ⁡ ( m ) = 1 P(m)=1 , as required by the definition of GKM algebras in § 3.2 . 
 
 
 We define the graph  Γ \Gamma  as follows: the vertices  V V  of  Γ \Gamma  are labelled by real simple roots  a ∈ Φ 𝒜 + , a\in\Phi_{\mathcal{A}}^{+,\real}  of  𝒜 \mathcal{A} . If  i i  and  j j  are two vertices of  Γ \Gamma , then we have  − ( i , j ) 𝒞 -(i,j)_{\mathscr{C}}  edges between them. Let  W Γ W_{\Gamma}  be the Weyl group associated to the graph  Γ \Gamma : it is the subgroup of the group of linear automorphisms of  𝐂 V \mathbf{C}^{V}  generated by the reflections 
 

 
 
 s i ​ ( λ ) = λ − λ T ​ C i ​ 1 i s_{i}(\lambda)=\lambda-\lambda^{T}C_{i}1_{i} 
 
 
 where  C i C_{i}  is the  i i th column of the Cartan matrix defined by  C i ​ j = 2 ​ δ i ​ j − # ⁡ { edges between  ​ i ​  and  ​ j } C_{ij}=2\delta_{ij}-\#\{\textrm{edges between }i\textrm{ and }j\} . Let  M ^ \widehat{M}  denote the groupification of  K ⁡ ( 𝒜 ) \K(\mathcal{A}) . The group  W Γ W_{\Gamma}  acts similarly on  M ^ ⊗ 𝐙 𝐂 \widehat{M}\otimes_{\mathbf{Z}}\mathbf{C}  by reflections: 
 

 
 
 s i ​ ( m ) = m − ( m , i ) 𝒞 ​ i . s_{i}(m)=m-(m,i)_{\mathscr{C}}i. 
 
 
 
 
 Proposition 7.18 . 
 
 Let  𝒜 \mathcal{A}  be a 2-Calabi–Yau category, and let  ı △ : ℳ 𝒜 △ ↪ ℳ 𝒜 \imath_{\triangle}\colon\mathcal{M}^{\triangle}_{\mathcal{A}}\hookrightarrow\mathcal{M}_{\mathcal{A}}  be as above. Let  m , m ′ ∈ K ⁡ ( 𝒜 ) m,m^{\prime}\in\K(\mathcal{A})  satisfy  w ⁡ ( m ) = m ′ w(m)=m^{\prime} , where  w ∈ W Γ w\in W_{\Gamma}  and  W Γ W_{\Gamma}  is the Weyl group defined above. Then there is an isomorphism of weight spaces 
 

 
 (7.3) 
 
 𝔫 𝒜 , m BPS , △ , + ≅ 𝔫 𝒜 , m ′ BPS , △ , + . \mathfrak{n}_{\mathcal{A},m}^{\mathtt{\aBPS},\triangle,+}\cong\mathfrak{n}_{\mathcal{A},m^{\prime}}^{\mathtt{\aBPS},\triangle,+}. 
 
 
 
 
 
 Proof. 
 
 Let  𝔤 ⊆ 𝔤 𝒜 BPS , △ \mathfrak{g}\subseteq\mathfrak{g}_{\mathcal{A}}^{\mathtt{\aBPS},\triangle}  be the Kac–Moody Lie algebra generated by the subspace 
 

 
 
 ⨁ m ∈ Φ K ⁡ ( 𝒜 ) , ( − , − ) + , ( 𝔤 𝒜 , m BPS , △ + 𝔤 𝒜 , − m BPS , △ ) . \bigoplus_{m\in\Phi_{\K(\mathcal{A}),(-,-)}^{+,\real}}\left(\mathfrak{g}_{\mathcal{A},m}^{\mathtt{\aBPS},\triangle}+\mathfrak{g}_{\mathcal{A},-m}^{\mathtt{\aBPS},\triangle}\right). 
 
 
 In other words, we set  𝔤 \mathfrak{g}  to be the Kac–Moody Lie algebra associated to the (half) Ext quiver of the union of objects in  𝒜 \mathcal{A}  corresponding to real simple roots. Then  𝔤 𝒜 BPS , △ \mathfrak{g}_{\mathcal{A}}^{\mathtt{\aBPS},\triangle}  is an integrable  𝔤 \mathfrak{g} -representation, and the claim follows by general theory of Kac–Moody Lie algebras  [ Kac90 , Proposition 3.7a)] .
∎ 
 
 
 
 
 
 8.  Comparison with the 3D BPS Lie algebra and cohomological integrality for 2CY categories 

 
 8.1.  BPS Lie algebras for Jacobi algebras 

 
 The BPS Lie algebra associated to a quiver with general potential, or Jacobi algebra, was defined in  [ DM20 , §6.1, Corollary 6.11] . We briefly recall the definitions, and refer to  [ KS11 ,  Dav17a ,  DM20 ]  for background and full details. While the theory we present here, as in the rest of the paper, can readily be lifted to categories of (monodromic) mixed Hodge modules/mixed Hodge structures, we describe everything at the level of perverse sheaves/vector spaces. 
 
 
 Let  Q Q  be a quiver, which we assume to be symmetric (i.e. for every pair of vertices  i , j ∈ Q 0 i,j\in Q_{0}  we assume that there are as many arrows from  i i  to  j j  as there are from  j j  to  i i ). We fix a bilinear form  ψ : 𝐙 Q 0 × 𝐙 Q 0 → 𝐙 \psi\colon\mathbf{Z}^{Q_{0}}\times\mathbf{Z}^{Q_{0}}\rightarrow\mathbf{Z}  satisfying the condition 
 

 
 
 ψ ( 𝐝 ′′ , 𝐝 ′ ) + ψ ( 𝐝 ′ , 𝐝 ′′ ) ≡ χ Q ( 𝐝 ′ , 𝐝 ′ ) χ Q ( 𝐝 ′′ , 𝐝 ′′ ) + χ Q ( 𝐝 ′ , 𝐝 ′′ ) ( mod 2 ) , \psi(\mathbf{d}^{\prime\prime},\mathbf{d}^{\prime})+\psi(\mathbf{d}^{\prime},\mathbf{d}^{\prime\prime})\equiv\chi_{Q}(\mathbf{d}^{\prime},\mathbf{d}^{\prime})\chi_{Q}(\mathbf{d}^{\prime\prime},\mathbf{d}^{\prime\prime})+\chi_{Q}(\mathbf{d}^{\prime},\mathbf{d}^{\prime\prime})\quad\pmod{2}, 
 
 
 as in  [ KS11 , §2.6] , or  [ DM20 , §1.6] . We next pick a  potential   W ∈ 𝐂 ​ Q / [ 𝐂 ​ Q , 𝐂 ​ Q ] W\in\mathbf{C}Q/[\mathbf{C}Q,\mathbf{C}Q] . The potential  W W  defines a function  Tr ⁡ ( W ) \Tr(W)  on  𝔐 Q , 𝐝 \mathfrak{M}_{Q,\mathbf{d}} , sending a  𝐂 ​ Q \mathbf{C}Q -module  ρ \rho  to  Tr ( W ¯ ⋅ : ρ → ρ ) \Tr(\overline{W}\cdot\colon\rho\rightarrow\rho)  where  W ¯ \overline{W}  is any lift of  W W  to  𝐂 ​ Q \mathbf{C}Q . This function factors through  𝙹𝙷 : 𝔐 Q , 𝐝 → ℳ Q , 𝐝 \JH\colon\mathfrak{M}_{Q,\mathbf{d}}\rightarrow\mathcal{M}_{Q,\mathbf{d}} . We define 
 

 
 
 𝒜 Q , W , 𝐝 ≔ 𝙹𝙷 ∗ ⁡ ϕ Tr ⁡ ( W ) 𝔭 ​ 𝐐 𝔐 Q , 𝐝 vir \mathscr{A}_{Q,W,\mathbf{d}}\coloneqq\JH_{*}{}^{\mathfrak{p}}\!\phi_{\Tr(W)}\mathbf{Q}^{\mathrm{vir}}_{\mathfrak{M}_{Q,\mathbf{d}}} 
 
 
 where  𝐐 𝔐 Q , 𝐝 vir ≔ 𝐐 𝔐 Q , 𝐝 ​ [ − χ Q ​ ( 𝐝 , 𝐝 ) ] \mathbf{Q}_{\mathfrak{M}_{Q,\mathbf{d}}}^{\mathrm{vir}}\coloneqq\mathbf{Q}_{\mathfrak{M}_{Q,\mathbf{d}}}[-\chi_{Q}(\mathbf{d},\mathbf{d})] , and  𝒜 Q , W ≔ ⨁ 𝐝 ∈ 𝐍 Q 0 𝒜 Q , W , 𝐝 \mathscr{A}_{Q,W}\coloneqq\bigoplus_{\mathbf{d}\in\mathbf{N}^{Q_{0}}}\mathscr{A}_{Q,W,\mathbf{d}} , with  ϕ Tr ⁡ ( W ) 𝔭 {}^{\mathfrak{p}}\!\phi_{\Tr(W)}  the perverse vanishing cycles functor for the function  Tr ⁡ ( W ) \Tr(W) . Via pushforward and pullback in vanishing cycle cohomology, along morphisms from the stack of short exact sequences of  𝐂 ​ Q \mathbf{C}Q -modules, along with the Thom–Sebastiani isomorphism, the complex  𝒜 Q , W \mathscr{A}_{Q,W}  carries the structure of an algebra object in  𝒟 + ​ ( Perv ⁡ ( ℳ Q ) ) ≅ 𝒟 c + ​ ( ℳ Q ) \mathcal{D}^{+}(\operatorname{Perv}(\mathcal{M}_{Q}))\cong\mathcal{D}^{+}_{\mathrm{c}}(\mathcal{M}_{Q}) ,  [ KS11 ,  Dav17a ] . The monoidal structure on this category is defined via the direct image along the direct sum map  ⊕ : ℳ Q × ℳ Q → ℳ Q \oplus\colon\mathcal{M}_{Q}\times\mathcal{M}_{Q}\rightarrow\mathcal{M}_{Q} . As usual, we denote by  𝒜 Q , W ψ \mathscr{A}_{Q,W}^{\psi}  the  ψ \psi -twisted CoHA, in which we twist the multiplication morphism  𝒜 Q , W , 𝐝 ′ ⊡ 𝒜 Q , W , 𝐝 ′′ → 𝒜 Q , W , 𝐝 ′ + 𝐝 ′′ \mathscr{A}_{Q,W,\mathbf{d}^{\prime}}\boxdot\mathscr{A}_{Q,W,\mathbf{d}^{\prime\prime}}\rightarrow\mathscr{A}_{Q,W,\mathbf{d}^{\prime}+\mathbf{d}^{\prime\prime}}  by  ( − 1 ) ψ ⁡ ( 𝐝 ′ , 𝐝 ′′ ) (-1)^{\psi(\mathbf{d}^{\prime},\mathbf{d}^{\prime\prime})} . 
 
 
 For  𝐝 ∈ 𝐍 Q 0 \mathbf{d}\in\mathbf{N}^{Q_{0}}  we define 
 

 
 
 ℬ ​ 𝒫 ​ 𝒮 Q , W , 𝐝 ≔ ( τ ⩽ 1 𝔭 ​ 𝙹𝙷 ∗ ​ ϕ Tr ⁡ ( W ) 𝔭 ​ 𝐐 𝔐 Q , 𝐝 vir ) ​ [ 1 ] . \mathcal{BPS}_{Q,W,\mathbf{d}}\coloneqq({{}^{\mathfrak{p}}\tau}^{\leqslant 1}\!\JH_{*}{}^{\mathfrak{p}}\!\phi_{\Tr(W)}\mathbf{Q}_{\mathfrak{M}_{Q,\mathbf{d}}}^{\mathrm{vir}})[1]. 
 
 
 The following theorem is the basis for the definition of the BPS Lie algebra associated to a quiver with potential  [ DM20 , Theorem C] . 
 
 
 Theorem 8.1   ( [ DM20 , Theorem C, Corollary 6.11] ) . 
 
 The complex  ℬ ​ 𝒫 ​ 𝒮 Q , W , 𝐝 \mathcal{BPS}_{Q,W,\mathbf{d}}  is a perverse sheaf. Setting  𝔤 Q , W , 𝐝 ≔ H ∗ ⁡ ℬ ​ 𝒫 ​ 𝒮 Q , W , 𝐝 ​ [ − 1 ] \mathfrak{g}_{Q,W,\mathbf{d}}\coloneqq\HO^{*}\!\mathcal{BPS}_{Q,W,\mathbf{d}}[-1]  and  𝔤 Q , W ≔ ⨁ 𝐝 ∈ 𝐍 Q 0 𝔤 Q , W , 𝐝 \mathfrak{g}_{Q,W}\coloneqq\bigoplus_{\mathbf{d}\in\mathbf{N}^{Q_{0}}}\mathfrak{g}_{Q,W,\mathbf{d}} , the natural morphism  𝔤 Q , W → H ∗ ⁡ 𝒜 Q , W \mathfrak{g}_{Q,W}\rightarrow\HO^{*}\!\mathscr{A}_{Q,W}  is an injection, and the image is closed under the commutator Lie bracket for the algebra  H ∗ ⁡ 𝒜 Q , W ψ \HO^{*}\!\mathscr{A}_{Q,W}^{\psi} . 
 
 
 
 The resulting  𝐍 Q 0 \mathbf{N}^{Q_{0}} -graded Lie algebra is denoted  𝔤 Q , W ψ \mathfrak{g}_{Q,W}^{\psi} . We include the superscript  ψ \psi  since a priori this algebra depends on the bilinear form  ψ \psi  (in contrast with the Lie algebra  𝔫 𝒜 + \mathfrak{n}_{\mathcal{A}}^{+}  we have associated with a 2CY category  𝒜 \mathcal{A} ). We use the notation  𝔤 Q , W \mathfrak{g}_{Q,W}  as opposed to  𝔫 Q , W + \mathfrak{n}^{+}_{Q,W} , following  [ DM20 ] . In the case of the tripled quiver with potential § 8.2 , the Lie algebra  𝔤 Q , W ψ \mathfrak{g}_{Q,W}^{\psi}  appears a posteriori to be a half of a GKM Lie algebra (by Proposition  8.4  and our main result Theorem  1.2 ), and so one could have expected the letter  𝔫 \mathfrak{n} . 
 
 
 
 8.2.  Tripled quiver with potential 

 
 Let  Q Q  be a quiver, and denote by  Q ~ \widetilde{Q}  the tripled quiver of  Q Q  defined in § 2 . We denote by 
 

 
 (8.1) 
 
 W ~ = ( ∑ i ∈ Q 0 ω i ) ​ ( ∑ a ∈ Q 1 [ a , a ∗ ] ) \widetilde{W}=\left(\sum_{i\in Q_{0}}\omega_{i}\right)\left(\sum_{a\in Q_{1}}[a,a^{*}]\right) 
 
 
 the  canonical cubic potential . We denote by 
 

 
 
 h : ℳ Q ~ → ℳ Q ¯ h\colon\mathcal{M}_{\widetilde{Q}}\rightarrow\mathcal{M}_{\overline{Q}} 
 
 
 the forgetful map, forgetting the additional loops  ω i \omega_{i}  at the vertices. By the dimensional reduction isomorphism  [ Dav17a , Theorem A.1]  there is a natural isomorphism 
 

 
 
 Λ 𝐝 : h ∗ ​ 𝒜 Q ~ , W ~ , 𝐝 ≅ 𝒜 Π Q , 𝐝 \Lambda_{\mathbf{d}}\colon h_{*}\mathscr{A}_{\widetilde{Q},\widetilde{W},\mathbf{d}}\cong\mathscr{A}_{\Pi_{Q},\mathbf{d}} 
 
 
 and  Λ = ⨁ 𝐝 ∈ 𝐍 Q 0 ( − 1 ) ( | 𝐝 | 2 ) ​ Λ 𝐝 \Lambda=\bigoplus_{\mathbf{d}\in\mathbf{N}^{Q_{0}}}(-1)^{\binom{\lvert\mathbf{d}\lvert}{2}}\Lambda_{\mathbf{d}}  is an isomorphism of algebra objects  [ Dav17 ,  YZ18 ] . Note that 
 

 
 
 χ Q ~ ( 𝐝 ′ , 𝐝 ′ ) χ Q ~ ( 𝐝 ′′ , 𝐝 ′′ ) + χ Q ~ ( 𝐝 ′ , 𝐝 ′′ ) ≡ ( 𝐝 ′ , 𝐝 ′′ ) Q ( mod 2 ) \chi_{\widetilde{Q}}(\mathbf{d}^{\prime},\mathbf{d}^{\prime})\chi_{\widetilde{Q}}(\mathbf{d}^{\prime\prime},\mathbf{d}^{\prime\prime})+\chi_{\widetilde{Q}}(\mathbf{d}^{\prime},\mathbf{d}^{\prime\prime})\equiv(\mathbf{d}^{\prime},\mathbf{d}^{\prime\prime})_{Q}\quad\pmod{2} 
 
 
 so in particular we may retain our assumed choice of  ψ \psi : we set  ψ ⁡ ( 𝐝 ′ , 𝐝 ′′ ) = χ Q ​ ( 𝐝 ′ , 𝐝 ′′ ) \psi(\mathbf{d}^{\prime},\mathbf{d}^{\prime\prime})=\chi_{Q}(\mathbf{d}^{\prime},\mathbf{d}^{\prime\prime}) . For this particular class of quivers with potential, the following theorem provides a lift of the BPS Lie algebra  𝔤 Q ~ , W ~ \mathfrak{g}_{\widetilde{Q},\widetilde{W}}  to a category of perverse sheaves: 
 
 
 Theorem 8.2   ( [ Dav20 , Theorem 4.1 and 6.1]  ) . 
 
 The complex  ℬ ​ 𝒫 ​ 𝒮 Π Q , Lie 𝟹 ​ 𝚍 ≔ h ∗ ​ ℬ ​ 𝒫 ​ 𝒮 Q ~ , W ~ ​ [ − 1 ] = h ∗ ​ τ ⩽ 1 𝔭 ​ 𝙹𝙷 ∗ ​ ϕ Tr ⁡ ( W ) 𝔭 ​ 𝐐 𝔐 Q vir \mathcal{BPS}^{\mathtt{3d}}_{\Pi_{Q},\mathrm{Lie}}\coloneqq h_{*}\mathcal{BPS}_{\widetilde{Q},\widetilde{W}}[-1]=h_{*}{{}^{\mathfrak{p}}\tau}^{\leqslant 1}\!\JH_{*}{}^{\mathfrak{p}}\!\phi_{\Tr(W)}\mathbf{Q}_{\mathfrak{M}_{Q}}^{\mathrm{vir}}  is a perverse sheaf. Moreover, the morphism 
 

 
 
 ℬ ​ 𝒫 ​ 𝒮 Π Q , Lie 𝟹 ​ 𝚍 → ℬ ​ 𝒫 ​ 𝒮 Π Q , Alg ψ \mathcal{BPS}^{\mathtt{3d}}_{\Pi_{Q},\mathrm{Lie}}\rightarrow\mathcal{BPS}_{\Pi_{Q},\mathrm{Alg}}^{\psi} 
 
 
 is an inclusion of perverse sheaves, and the image is closed under the commutator Lie bracket on the target. We denote by  ℬ ​ 𝒫 ​ 𝒮 Π Q , Lie 𝟹 ​ 𝚍 , ψ \mathcal{BPS}^{\mathtt{3d},\psi}_{\Pi_{Q},\mathrm{Lie}}  the resulting Lie algebra object. The resulting morphism 
 

 
 
 𝐔 ⁡ ( ℬ ​ 𝒫 ​ 𝒮 Π Q , Lie 𝟹 ​ 𝚍 , ψ ) → ℬ ​ 𝒫 ​ 𝒮 Π Q , Alg ψ \UEA(\mathcal{BPS}^{\mathtt{3d},\psi}_{\Pi_{Q},\mathrm{Lie}})\rightarrow\mathcal{BPS}_{\Pi_{Q},\mathrm{Alg}}^{\psi} 
 
 
 is an isomorphism of algebra objects. There is a natural isomorphism of Lie algebras  H ∗ ⁡ ℬ ​ 𝒫 ​ 𝒮 Π Q , Lie 𝟹 ​ 𝚍 , ψ ≅ 𝔤 Q ~ , W ~ ψ \HO^{*}\!\mathcal{BPS}^{\mathtt{3d},\psi}_{\Pi_{Q},\mathrm{Lie}}\cong\mathfrak{g}_{\widetilde{Q},\widetilde{W}}^{\psi} . 
 
 
 
 In particular, we have a morphism 
 

 
 
 PBW Π Q : Sym ⊡ ⁡ ( ℬ ​ 𝒫 ​ 𝒮 Π Q , Lie 𝟹 ​ 𝚍 ) → ℬ ​ 𝒫 ​ 𝒮 Π Q , Alg ψ \mathrm{PBW}_{\Pi_{Q}}\colon\Sym_{\boxdot}(\mathcal{BPS}_{\Pi_{Q},\mathrm{Lie}}^{\mathtt{3d}})\rightarrow\mathcal{BPS}_{\Pi_{Q},\mathrm{Alg}}^{\psi} 
 
 
 obtained by evaluating symmetric tensors with the aid of the algebra structure on the target. 
 
 
 Proposition 8.3   ( [ Dav20 , Formula 67] ) . 
 
 The map  PBW Π Q \mathrm{PBW}_{\Pi_{Q}}  is an isomorphism of perverse sheaves. 
 
 
 
 Proposition 8.4 . 
 
 There is a natural morphism 
 

 
 
 ℬ ​ 𝒫 ​ 𝒮 Π Q , Lie → ℬ ​ 𝒫 ​ 𝒮 Π Q , Lie 𝟹 ​ 𝚍 , ψ \mathcal{BPS}_{\Pi_{Q},\mathrm{Lie}}\rightarrow\mathcal{BPS}_{\Pi_{Q},\mathrm{Lie}}^{\mathtt{3d},\psi} 
 
 
 in  Perv ⁡ ( ℳ Π Q ) \operatorname{Perv}(\mathcal{M}_{\Pi_{Q}}) . It is an isomorphism of Lie algebra objects. Taking derived global sections, there is an isomorphism of Lie algebras 
 

 
 
 𝔫 Π Q BPS , + ≅ 𝔤 Q ~ , W ~ ψ . \mathfrak{n}^{\mathtt{\aBPS},+}_{\Pi_{Q}}\cong\mathfrak{g}_{\widetilde{Q},\widetilde{W}}^{\psi}. 
 
 
 
 
 
 Proof. 
 
 By Theorem  1.1  there is a monomorphism of Lie algebra objects 
 

 
 (8.2) 
 
 ℬ ​ 𝒫 ​ 𝒮 Π Q , Lie → ℬ ​ 𝒫 ​ 𝒮 Π Q , Alg ψ \mathcal{BPS}_{\Pi_{Q},\mathrm{Lie}}\rightarrow\mathcal{BPS}_{\Pi_{Q},\mathrm{Alg}}^{\psi} 
 
 
 and by  [ Dav20 , Formula 67]  there is a monomorphism 
 

 
 (8.3) 
 
 ℬ ​ 𝒫 ​ 𝒮 Π Q , Lie 𝟹 ​ 𝚍 , ψ → ℬ ​ 𝒫 ​ 𝒮 Π Q , Alg ψ . \mathcal{BPS}_{\Pi_{Q},\mathrm{Lie}}^{\mathtt{3d},\psi}\rightarrow\mathcal{BPS}_{\Pi_{Q},\mathrm{Alg}}^{\psi}. 
 
 
 In both instances we consider the target as a Lie algebra object via the commutator bracket. By Theorem  1.1  again,  ℬ ​ 𝒫 ​ 𝒮 Π Q , Lie \mathcal{BPS}_{\Pi_{Q},\mathrm{Lie}}  can be written as a quotient of  Free ⊡ -Lie ⁡ ( 𝒢 Π Q ) \operatorname{Free}_{\boxdot\sLie}(\mathscr{G}_{\Pi_{Q}}) , and in particular for each  𝐝 ∈ Φ Π Q + \mathbf{d}\in\Phi^{+}_{\Pi_{Q}}  the semisimple perverse sheaf  ℬ ​ 𝒫 ​ 𝒮 Π Q , Lie \mathcal{BPS}_{\Pi_{Q},\mathrm{Lie}}  contains a unique copy of  𝒢 𝐝 \mathscr{G}_{\mathbf{d}} . Since  ℬ ​ 𝒫 ​ 𝒮 Π Q , Lie 𝟹 ​ 𝚍 \mathcal{BPS}_{\Pi_{Q},\mathrm{Lie}}^{\mathtt{3d}}  contains  𝒢 𝐝 \mathscr{G}_{\mathbf{d}}  for each  𝐝 ∈ Φ Π Q + \mathbf{d}\in\Phi^{+}_{\Pi_{Q}}   [ Dav20 , §7] , the morphism ( 8.2 ) factors uniquely through ( 8.3 ). We therefore have monomorphisms of Lie algebra objects. 
 

 
 (8.4) 
 
 ℬ ​ 𝒫 ​ 𝒮 Π Q , Lie → ℬ ​ 𝒫 ​ 𝒮 Π Q , Lie 𝟹 ​ 𝚍 , ψ → ℬ ​ 𝒫 ​ 𝒮 Π Q , Alg ψ . \mathcal{BPS}_{\Pi_{Q},\mathrm{Lie}}\rightarrow\mathcal{BPS}_{\Pi_{Q},\mathrm{Lie}}^{\mathtt{3d},\psi}\rightarrow\mathcal{BPS}_{\Pi_{Q},\mathrm{Alg}}^{\psi}. 
 
 
 By evaluating symmetric products using the algebra structure on  ℬ ​ 𝒫 ​ 𝒮 Π Q , Alg ψ \mathcal{BPS}_{\Pi_{Q},\mathrm{Alg}}^{\psi} , we obtain from ( 8.4 ) the morphisms of perverse sheaves 
 

 
 (8.5) 
 
 Sym ⊡ ⁡ ( ℬ ​ 𝒫 ​ 𝒮 Π Q , Lie ) → Sym ⊡ ⁡ ( ℬ ​ 𝒫 ​ 𝒮 Π Q , Lie 𝟹 ​ 𝚍 ) → ℬ ​ 𝒫 ​ 𝒮 Π Q , Alg ψ . \Sym_{\boxdot}(\mathcal{BPS}_{\Pi_{Q},\mathrm{Lie}})\rightarrow\Sym_{\boxdot}(\mathcal{BPS}_{\Pi_{Q},\mathrm{Lie}}^{\mathtt{3d}})\rightarrow\mathcal{BPS}_{\Pi_{Q},\mathrm{Alg}}^{\psi}. 
 
 
 
 
 The composition  Sym ⊡ ⁡ ( ℬ ​ 𝒫 ​ 𝒮 Π Q , Lie ) → ℬ ​ 𝒫 ​ 𝒮 Π Q , Alg ψ \Sym_{\boxdot}(\mathcal{BPS}_{\Pi_{Q},\mathrm{Lie}})\rightarrow\mathcal{BPS}_{\Pi_{Q},\mathrm{Alg}}^{\psi}  is an isomorphism by Theorem  1.1  and the second arrow in ( 8.5 ) is the morphism  PBW Π Q \mathrm{PBW}_{\Pi_{Q}} , which is an isomorphism by Proposition  8.3 . Therefore the first arrow in ( 8.5 ) is an isomorphism, and consequently the first arrow in ( 8.4 ) is also an isomorphism.
∎ 
 
 
 
 Let  Q Q  be a quiver, and let  Γ \Gamma  be the underlying graph of  Q Q . The Weyl group  W Γ W_{\Gamma}  acts on  𝐂 Q 0 \mathbf{C}^{Q_{0}} , and its image is generated by the reflections 
 

 
 
 s i : 𝐝 ↦ 𝐝 − ( 𝐝 , 1 i ) ​ 1 i s_{i}\colon\mathbf{d}\mapsto\mathbf{d}-(\mathbf{d},1_{i})1_{i} 
 
 
 with  i i  a vertex that supports no 1-cycles, i.e.  i ∈ Q 0 i\in Q_{0}  (see also § 7.9 ). 
 
 
 Corollary 8.5 . 
 
 Let  Q Q  be a quiver, and let  𝐝 , 𝐝 ′ ∈ 𝐍 Q 0 \mathbf{d},\mathbf{d}^{\prime}\in\mathbf{N}^{Q_{0}}  be dimension vectors such that  w ⋅ 𝐝 = 𝐝 ′ w\cdot\mathbf{d}=\mathbf{d}^{\prime}  for some  w ∈ W Γ w\in W_{\Gamma} . Then there is an isomorphism 
 

 
 
 𝔤 Q ~ , W ~ , 𝐝 ≅ 𝔤 Q ~ , W ~ , 𝐝 ′ . \mathfrak{g}_{\widetilde{Q},\widetilde{W},\mathbf{d}}\cong\mathfrak{g}_{\widetilde{Q},\widetilde{W},\mathbf{d}^{\prime}}. 
 
 
 
 
 
 Proof. 
 
 This follows from combining Propositions  7.18  and  8.4 .
∎ 
 
 
 
 Using the characterisation ( 9.4 ) of Kac polynomials in terms of characteristic functions of BPS cohomology, we obtain a new proof of the Weyl-invariance of Kac polynomials 
 

 
 
 A Q , 𝐝 ​ ( q ) = A Q , w ⋅ 𝐝 ​ ( q ) A_{Q,\mathbf{d}}(q)=A_{Q,w\cdot\mathbf{d}}(q) 
 
 
 which is part of Theorem 1 of Kac’s original paper  [ Kac83 ] . 
 
 
 
 8.3.  Cohomological integrality and PBW isomorphism for  2 2 -Calabi–Yau categories 

 
 8.3.1.  Restriction of BPS Lie algebras 

 
 We let  ℱ ¯ = { ℱ 1 , … , ℱ r } \underline{\mathcal{F}}=\{\mathcal{F}_{1},\ldots,\mathcal{F}_{r}\}  be a  Σ \Sigma -collection in an Abelian category  𝒜 \mathcal{A} , satisfying the assumptions of Theorem  4.2 , where  𝒜 \mathcal{A}  also satisfies Assumptions  1 – 6  from § 4.3  and § 4.4 , so that we can define the BPS Lie algebra for  𝒜 \mathcal{A} . We let  Q Q  be a half Ext-quiver of the collection  ℱ ¯ \underline{\mathcal{F}} . 
 
 
 Lemma 8.6 . 
 
 Let  ℬ ​ 𝒫 ​ 𝒮 ¯ Π Q , Lie \underline{\mathcal{BPS}}_{\Pi_{Q},\mathrm{Lie}}  be the BPS Lie algebra sheaf on  ℳ Π Q \mathcal{M}_{\Pi_{Q}}  and let  ℬ ​ 𝒫 ​ 𝒮 ¯ 𝒜 , Lie \underline{\mathcal{BPS}}_{\mathcal{A},\mathrm{Lie}}  be the BPS Lie algebra sheaf on  ℳ 𝒜 \mathcal{M}_{\mathcal{A}} . Then with the notations of diagram ( 7.1 ), we have a canonical isomorphism of Lie algebras  ı 𝒩 ! ℬ ​ 𝒫 ​ 𝒮 ¯ Π Q , Lie ≅ ı ℱ ¯ ! ℬ ​ 𝒫 ​ 𝒮 ¯ 𝒜 , Lie \imath_{\mathcal{N}}^{!}\underline{\mathcal{BPS}}_{\Pi_{Q},\mathrm{Lie}}\cong\imath_{\underline{\mathcal{F}}}^{!}\underline{\mathcal{BPS}}_{\mathcal{A},\mathrm{Lie}} . 
 
 
 
 Proof. 
 
 Once again, we may prove this lemma after applying the functor  𝐫𝐚𝐭 \rat . Then it follows from Lemma  7.1 , Proposition  5.3  and Lemma  7.3 . More precisely,  ı 𝒩 ! ℬ 𝒫 𝒮 Π Q , Lie ψ \imath_{\mathcal{N}}^{!}\mathcal{BPS}_{\Pi_{Q},\mathrm{Lie}}^{\psi}  is the sub-Lie algebra of  ι 𝒩 ! ℬ 𝒫 𝒮 Π Q , Alg ψ \iota_{\mathcal{N}}^{!}\mathcal{BPS}_{\Pi_{Q},\mathrm{Alg}}^{\psi}  generated by  ι 𝒩 ! 𝒢 Π Q , 𝐝 \iota_{\mathcal{N}}^{!}\mathscr{G}_{\Pi_{Q},\mathbf{d}}  for  𝐝 ∈ Φ Π Q + \mathbf{d}\in\Phi^{+}_{\Pi_{Q}}  and  ı ℱ ¯ ! ℬ 𝒫 𝒮 𝒜 , Lie ψ \imath_{\underline{\mathcal{F}}}^{!}\mathcal{BPS}_{\mathcal{A},\mathrm{Lie}}^{\psi}  is the sub-Lie algebra of  ı ℱ ¯ ! ℬ 𝒫 𝒮 𝒜 , Alg ψ \imath_{\underline{\mathcal{F}}}^{!}\mathcal{BPS}_{\mathcal{A},\mathrm{Alg}}^{\psi}  generated by  ι ! ℱ ¯ 𝒢 𝒜 , m \iota^{!}_{\underline{\mathcal{F}}}\mathscr{G}_{\mathcal{A},m} ,  m ∈ Φ 𝒜 + m\in\Phi^{+}_{\mathcal{A}} , and for  𝐝 ∈ Φ Π Q + \mathbf{d}\in\Phi^{+}_{\Pi_{Q}} ,  ι 𝒩 , 𝐝 ! 𝒢 Π Q , 𝐝 ≅ ι ℱ ¯ , λ ℱ ¯ ​ ( 𝐝 ) ! 𝒢 𝒜 , λ ℱ ¯ ​ ( 𝐝 ) \iota_{\mathcal{N},\mathbf{d}}^{!}\mathscr{G}_{\Pi_{Q},\mathbf{d}}\cong\iota_{\underline{\mathcal{F}},\lambda_{\underline{\mathcal{F}}}(\mathbf{d})}^{!}\mathscr{G}_{\mathcal{A},\lambda_{\underline{\mathcal{F}}}(\mathbf{d})}  as in the proof of Proposition  7.4  (where  λ ℱ ¯ \lambda_{\underline{\mathcal{F}}}  is defined in § 4.6.1 ), which moreover satisfy the same Serre relations (using Lemma  5.2 ).
∎ 
 
 
 
 
 8.3.2.  Proof of the cohomological integrality theorem for 2CY categories 

 
 We prove Theorem  1.7 . We first construct the morphism  Ψ 𝒜 \Psi_{\mathcal{A}} . 
 
 
 Composing the adjunction morphism  ℬ ​ 𝒫 ​ 𝒮 ¯ 𝒜 , Alg = τ ⩽ 0 ​ 𝒜 ¯ 𝒜 → 𝒜 ¯ 𝒜 \underline{\mathcal{BPS}}_{\mathcal{A},\mathrm{Alg}}=\tau^{\leqslant 0}\underline{\mathscr{A}}_{\mathcal{A}}\rightarrow\underline{\mathscr{A}}_{\mathcal{A}}  with the inclusion  ℬ ​ 𝒫 ​ 𝒮 ¯ 𝒜 , Lie → ℬ ​ 𝒫 ​ 𝒮 ¯ 𝒜 , Alg ψ \underline{\mathcal{BPS}}_{\mathcal{A},\mathrm{Lie}}\rightarrow\underline{\mathcal{BPS}}_{\mathcal{A},\mathrm{Alg}}^{\psi}  provided by Theorem  1.1 , we have a canonical morphism 
 

 
 
 ℬ ​ 𝒫 ​ 𝒮 ¯ 𝒜 , Lie → 𝒜 ¯ 𝒜 . \underline{\mathcal{BPS}}_{\mathcal{A},\mathrm{Lie}}\rightarrow\underline{\mathscr{A}}_{\mathcal{A}}. 
 
 
 We fix a positive determinant line bundle on  𝔐 𝒜 \mathfrak{M}_{\mathcal{A}} . This means that we fix a morphism  det : 𝔐 𝒜 → B ⁡ 𝐂 ∗ \det\colon\mathfrak{M}_{\mathcal{A}}\rightarrow\B\mathbf{C}^{*}  such that for every point  x ∈ 𝔐 𝒜 x\in\mathfrak{M}_{\mathcal{A}}  representing an object  ℱ \mathcal{F} , if we let  ı : B ⁡ 𝐂 ∗ → 𝔐 𝒜 \imath\colon\B\mathbf{C}^{*}\rightarrow\mathfrak{M}_{\mathcal{A}}  be the morphism corresponding to the inclusion of groups  𝐂 ∗ ⊆ Aut 𝒜 ⁡ ( ℱ ) \mathbf{C}^{*}\subseteq\Aut_{\mathcal{A}}(\mathcal{F}) , and write  H 𝐂 ∗ = 𝐐 ⁡ [ u ] \HO_{\mathbf{C}^{*}}=\mathbf{Q}[u] , then  ı ∗ ​ det ∗ u = m ℱ ​ u \imath^{*}\det^{*}u=m_{\mathcal{F}}u  for some  m ℱ ∈ 𝐙 ⩾ 1 m_{\mathcal{F}}\in\mathbf{Z}_{\geqslant 1} , and  ℱ ↦ m ℱ \mathcal{F}\mapsto m_{\mathcal{F}}  descends to  K ⁡ ( 𝒜 ) \K(\mathcal{A}) . Fixing a determinant line bundle on  𝔐 𝒜 \mathfrak{M}_{\mathcal{A}} , the algebra  H 𝐂 ∗ ∗ \HO^{*}_{\mathbf{C}^{*}}  acts on  𝒜 ¯ 𝒜 \underline{\mathscr{A}}_{\mathcal{A}} , and we obtain a morphism 
 

 
 
 ℬ ​ 𝒫 ​ 𝒮 ¯ 𝒜 , Lie ⊗ H 𝐂 ∗ ∗ → 𝒜 ¯ 𝒜 ψ . \underline{\mathcal{BPS}}_{\mathcal{A},\mathrm{Lie}}\otimes\HO^{*}_{\mathbf{C}^{*}}\rightarrow\underline{\mathscr{A}}_{\mathcal{A}}^{\psi}. 
 
 
 The algebra structure on  𝒜 ¯ 𝒜 ψ \underline{\mathscr{A}}_{\mathcal{A}}^{\psi}  now induces by iterated multiplications a morphism 
 

 
 
 Ψ 𝒜 ψ : Sym ⊡ ⁡ ( ℬ ​ 𝒫 ​ 𝒮 ¯ 𝒜 , Lie ⊗ H 𝐂 ∗ ∗ ) → 𝒜 ¯ 𝒜 ψ \Psi^{\psi}_{\mathcal{A}}\colon\Sym_{\boxdot}(\underline{\mathcal{BPS}}_{\mathcal{A},\mathrm{Lie}}\otimes\HO^{*}_{\mathbf{C}^{*}})\rightarrow\underline{\mathscr{A}}_{\mathcal{A}}^{\psi} 
 
 
 in  𝒟 + ​ ( MHM ⁡ ( ℳ 𝒜 ) ) \mathcal{D}^{+}(\mathrm{MHM}(\mathcal{M}_{\mathcal{A}})) . 
 
 
 Proof of Theorem  1.7 . 
 
 We want to prove that  Ψ 𝒜 ψ \Psi^{\psi}_{\mathcal{A}}  is an isomorphism (or equivalently by conservativity of the functor  𝐫𝐚𝐭 \rat , that  𝐫𝐚𝐭 ⁡ ( Ψ 𝒜 ψ ) \rat(\Psi_{\mathcal{A}}^{\psi})  is an isomorphism). We let  𝒞 ¯ ≔ cone ⁡ ( Ψ 𝒜 ) \underline{\mathcal{C}}\coloneqq\mathrm{cone}(\Psi_{\mathcal{A}}) . If  𝒞 ¯ \underline{\mathcal{C}}  is non-zero, then there exists a closed point  x ∈ ℳ 𝒜 x\in\mathcal{M}_{\mathcal{A}}  such that, with  ı x \imath_{x}  the inclusion of  x x ,  ı x ! 𝒞 ¯ ≠ 0 \imath_{x}^{!}\underline{\mathcal{C}}\neq 0 . We let  ℱ ¯ \underline{\mathcal{F}}  be the collection of simple direct summands of  𝒜 \mathcal{A}  corresponding to  x x . Then,  ι ℱ ¯ ! 𝒞 ¯ \iota_{\underline{\mathcal{F}}}^{!}\underline{\mathcal{C}}  is non-zero, so  ı ℱ ¯ ! Ψ 𝒜 ψ \imath_{\underline{\mathcal{F}}}^{!}\Psi_{\mathcal{A}}^{\psi}  is not an isomorphism. But  ı ℱ ¯ ! Ψ 𝒜 ψ \imath_{\underline{\mathcal{F}}}^{!}\Psi_{\mathcal{A}}^{\psi}  can be identified with the morphism  ı 𝒩 ! Ψ Π Q ℱ ¯ ψ \imath_{\mathcal{N}}^{!}\Psi_{\Pi_{Q_{\underline{\mathcal{F}}}}}^{\psi}  and so the latter cannot be an isomorphism. Then  Ψ Π Q ℱ ¯ ψ \Psi_{\Pi_{Q_{\underline{\mathcal{F}}}}}^{\psi}  is not an isomorphism. This is in contradiction with the PBW theorem 5 5 
                     5 
                    
                    
                    
                  We remark that although the proof of  [ Dav23a , Theorem D] , and the proof of  [ DM20 , Theorem C] , on which it is based, are written for a specific choice of positive determinant bundle, the proof works without modification for an arbitrary positive choice.  for preprojective algebras  [ Dav23a , Theorem D] , using the isomorphism of Proposition  8.4 .
∎ 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 9.  Cuspidal polynomials of quivers 

 
 9.1.  Cuspidal polynomials 

 
 Our main theorem (Theorem  1.2 ) can be applied to proving the positivity of absolutely cuspidal polynomials of quivers  [ BS19 ] , a strengthening of the Kac positivity conjecture  [ Kac83 , Conjecture 2] , in full generality. 
 
 
 Let  Q = ( Q 1 , Q 0 , s , t : Q 1 → Q 0 ) Q=(Q_{1},Q_{0},s,t\colon Q_{1}\rightarrow Q_{0})  be a quiver. In the early ’90s Ringel and Green defined  [ Rin90 ,  Rin92 ,  Gre95 ]  the  Hall bialgebra  of  Q Q  over a finite field  𝐅 q \mathbf{F}_{q} . As a vector space over  𝐂 \mathbf{C} , it has a basis given by the set of isomorphism classes of representations of  Q Q  over  𝐅 q \mathbf{F}_{q} : 
 

 
 
 H Q , 𝐅 q := ⨁ [ M ] ∈ Rep Q ( 𝐅 q ) / ∼ 𝐂 [ M ] H_{Q,\mathbf{F}_{q}}:=\bigoplus_{[M]\in\operatorname{Rep}_{Q}(\mathbf{F}_{q})/\sim}\mathbf{C}[M] 
 
 
 where  Rep Q ⁡ ( 𝐅 q ) \operatorname{Rep}_{Q}(\mathbf{F}_{q})  denotes the category of finite-dimensional modules and the product is the usual Hall-type product: we recommend  [ Sch06 ]  for background. Green  [ Gre95 ]  defined a coproduct  Δ \Delta  on  H Q , 𝐅 q H_{Q,\mathbf{F}_{q}} , so that  H Q , 𝐅 q H_{Q,\mathbf{F}_{q}}  has the structure of a twisted Hopf algebra (see Xiao’s paper  [ Xia97 ]  for an explicit description of the antipode). The twist, which won’t be used in this paper, is explained in [loc. cit.]. 
 
 
 Sevenhant and Van den Bergh proved  [ SV01 ]  that  H Q , 𝐅 q H_{Q,\mathbf{F}_{q}}  has the structure of the positive part of the quantised enveloping algebra of a generalised Kac–Moody algebra with deformation parameter specialised at  q \sqrt{q} . The generators are given by the space of  cuspidal functions . These are by definition the primitive elements of the coproduct: 
 

 
 
 H Q , 𝐅 q cusp = { f ∈ H Q , 𝐅 q ∣ Δ ⁡ ( f ) = f ⊗ 1 + 1 ⊗ f } , H_{Q,\mathbf{F}_{q}}^{\mathrm{cusp}}=\{f\in H_{Q,\mathbf{F}_{q}}\mid\Delta(f)=f\otimes 1+1\otimes f\}, 
 
 
 satisfying Serre relations, see  [ Hen21 , Theorem 3.4]  for a detailed formulation. In  [ SV01 ]  it is assumed that the quiver is loop-free, although this assumption can be removed. 
 
 
 For  𝐝 ∈ 𝐍 Q 0 \mathbf{d}\in\mathbf{N}^{Q_{0}} , let  M Q , 𝐝 ​ ( q ) M_{Q,\mathbf{d}}(q)  be the number of isomorphism classes of  𝐅 q \mathbf{F}_{q} –representations of  Q Q  of dimension vector  𝐝 \mathbf{d} , and let  I Q , 𝐝 ​ ( q ) I_{Q,\mathbf{d}}(q)  be the number of isomorphism classes of  indecomposable   𝐅 q \mathbf{F}_{q} –representations of  Q Q  of dimension vector  𝐝 \mathbf{d} . We denote by  A Q , 𝐝 ​ ( q ) A_{Q,\mathbf{d}}(q)  the  Kac polynomial , counting the number of isomorphism classes of  absolutely indecomposable   𝐅 q \mathbf{F}_{q} –representations of  Q Q  of dimension vector  𝐝 \mathbf{d} . By Kac  [ Kac83 ] , all of these functions are polynomials in  q q . Moreover, by  [ HLR13 ]  (or  [ Dav18 ,  DGT16 ]  for different approaches), the coefficients of  A Q , 𝐝 ​ ( q ) A_{Q,\mathbf{d}}(q)  are nonnegative, as originally conjectured by Kac in  [ Kac83 ] . 
 
 
 Given a  𝐍 Q 0 \mathbf{N}^{Q_{0}} -graded vector space  V V , we define  ch ⁡ ( V ) = ∑ 𝐝 ∈ 𝐍 Q 0 dim ( V 𝐝 ) ​ z 𝐝 \mathrm{ch}(V)=\sum_{\mathbf{d}\in\mathbf{N}^{Q_{0}}}\dim(V_{\mathbf{d}})z^{\mathbf{d}} . If  V V  also carries a cohomological  𝐙 \mathbf{Z} -grading, we define 
 

 
 
 ch 𝐙 ​ ( V ) ≔ ∑ n ∈ 𝐙 ∑ 𝐝 ∈ 𝐙 Q 0 dim ( V 𝐝 n ) ​ q n / 2 ​ z 𝐝 . \mathrm{ch}^{\mathbf{Z}}(V)\coloneqq\sum_{n\in\mathbf{Z}}\sum_{\mathbf{d}\in\mathbf{Z}^{Q_{0}}}\dim(V_{\mathbf{d}}^{n})q^{n/2}z^{\mathbf{d}}. 
 
 
 
 
 The  𝐍 Q 0 \mathbf{N}^{Q_{0}} -graded character of the Hall algebra is given by the formula 
 

 
 (9.1) 
 
 ch ⁡ ( H Q , F q ) = ∑ 𝐝 ∈ 𝐍 Q 0 M Q , 𝐝 ​ ( q ) ​ z 𝐝 = Exp z ⁡ ( ∑ 𝐝 > 0 I Q , 𝐝 ​ ( q ) ​ z 𝐝 ) = Exp q , z ⁡ ( ∑ 𝐝 > 0 A Q , 𝐝 ​ ( q ) ​ z 𝐝 ) , \mathrm{ch}(H_{Q,F_{q}})=\sum_{\mathbf{d}\in\mathbf{N}^{Q_{0}}}M_{Q,\mathbf{d}}(q)z^{\mathbf{d}}=\Exp_{z}\left(\sum_{\mathbf{d}>0}I_{Q,\mathbf{d}}(q)z^{\mathbf{d}}\right)=\Exp_{q,z}\left(\sum_{\mathbf{d}>0}A_{Q,\mathbf{d}}(q)z^{\mathbf{d}}\right), 
 
 
 where  Exp z \Exp_{z}  and  Exp q , z \Exp_{q,z}  denote the plethystic exponentials, see  [ BS19 , §1.5] . The second equality follows from the Krull–Schmidt property of the category of representations of the quiver and the third from Galois descent for quiver representations. 
 
 
 The space  H Q , 𝐅 q cusp H_{Q,\mathbf{F}_{q}}^{\mathrm{cusp}}  is naturally graded by the dimension vector:  H Q , 𝐅 q cusp = ⨁ 𝐝 ∈ 𝐍 Q 0 H Q , 𝐅 q cusp ​ [ 𝐝 ] H_{Q,\mathbf{F}_{q}}^{\mathrm{cusp}}=\bigoplus_{\mathbf{d}\in\mathbf{N}^{Q_{0}}}H_{Q,\mathbf{F}_{q}}^{\mathrm{cusp}}[\mathbf{d}] . There has been a growing interest in understanding this space and to find a parametrisation of cuspidal functions  [ BS19 ,  Hen21 ] . The first step was to compute its dimension. We have the following result. 
 
 
 Theorem 9.1   ( [ BS19 , Theorem 1.1] ) . 
 
 The dimension  dim 𝐂 H Q , 𝐅 q cusp ​ [ 𝐝 ] \dim_{\mathbf{C}}H_{Q,\mathbf{F}_{q}}^{\mathrm{cusp}}[\mathbf{d}]  is given by a polynomial with rational coefficients  C Q , 𝐝 ​ ( q ) ∈ 𝐐 ​ [ q ] C_{Q,\mathbf{d}}(q)\in\mathbf{Q}[q] . 
 
 
 
 The vector space  H Q , 𝐅 q cusp ​ [ 𝐝 ] H_{Q,\mathbf{F}_{q}}^{\mathrm{cusp}}[\mathbf{d}]  is ungraded, and its dimension is determined by  q q , via the evaluation of the polynomial  C Q , 𝐝 ​ ( q ) C_{Q,\mathbf{d}}(q) . We will see that this same polynomial is obtained as the characteristic function of a  fixed  cohomologically graded vector space. 
 
 
 Bozec and Schiffmann combinatorially defined a new family of polynomials  ( C Q , 𝐝 abs ​ ( q ) ) 𝐝 ∈ 𝐍 Q 0 (C_{Q,\mathbf{d}}^{\mathrm{abs}}(q))_{\mathbf{d}\in\mathbf{N}^{Q_{0}}}  from the family  ( C Q , 𝐝 ​ ( q ) ) 𝐝 ∈ 𝐍 Q 0 (C_{Q,\mathbf{d}}(q))_{\mathbf{d}\in\mathbf{N}^{Q_{0}}} , which enjoy more favourable properties. We denote by  𝐍 prim Q 0 \mathbf{N}^{Q_{0}}_{\prim}  the set of indivisible dimension vectors, i.e. of  𝐝 ∈ 𝐍 Q 0 \mathbf{d}\in\mathbf{N}^{Q_{0}}  which cannot be written  l ​ 𝐝 ′ l\mathbf{d}^{\prime}  with  l ∈ 𝐙 ⩾ 2 l\in\mathbf{Z}_{\geqslant 2}  and  𝐝 ′ ∈ 𝐍 Q 0 \mathbf{d}^{\prime}\in\mathbf{N}^{Q_{0}} . Then 
 
 •  
 
 C Q , 𝐝 abs ​ ( q ) = C Q , 𝐝 ​ ( q ) ​  if  χ Q ​ ( 𝐝 , 𝐝 ) < 0 C_{Q,\mathbf{d}}^{\mathrm{abs}}(q)=C_{Q,\mathbf{d}}(q)\text{ if $\chi_{Q}(\mathbf{d},\mathbf{d})<0$} , 
 
 •  
 
 Exp z ⁡ ( ∑ l ∈ 𝐙 > 0 C Q , l ​ 𝐝 ​ ( q ) ​ z l ​ 𝐝 ) = Exp q , z ⁡ ( ∑ l ∈ 𝐙 > 0 C Q , l ​ 𝐝 abs ​ ( q ) ​ z l ​ 𝐝 ) \Exp_{z}\left(\sum_{l\in\mathbf{Z}_{>0}}C_{Q,l\mathbf{d}}(q)z^{l\mathbf{d}}\right)=\Exp_{q,z}\left(\sum_{l\in\mathbf{Z}_{>0}}C_{Q,l\mathbf{d}}^{\mathrm{abs}}(q)z^{l\mathbf{d}}\right)  if  𝐝 ∈ ( 𝐍 Q 0 ) prim \mathbf{d}\in(\mathbf{N}^{Q_{0}})_{\prim}  and  χ Q ​ ( 𝐝 , 𝐝 ) = 0 \chi_{Q}(\mathbf{d},\mathbf{d})=0 , 
 
 •  
 
 C Q , 𝐝 abs ​ ( q ) = 0 C_{Q,\mathbf{d}}^{\mathrm{abs}}(q)=0  otherwise. 
 
 
 The definition is motivated by the fact that if there exists a  𝐍 × 𝐍 Q 0 \mathbf{N}\times\mathbf{N}^{Q_{0}} -graded GKM Lie algebra  𝔤 Q 𝐍 \mathfrak{g}_{Q}^{\mathbf{N}}  associated with the monoid  ( 𝐍 Q 0 , ( − , − ) ) (\mathbf{N}^{Q_{0}},(-,-))  (in the sense of § 3.2 ), whose positive part  𝔫 Q 𝐍 , + \mathfrak{n}^{\mathbf{N},+}_{Q}  has graded character 
 

 
 
 ch 𝐙 ​ ( 𝔫 Q 𝐍 , + ) = ∑ 𝐝 ∈ 𝐍 Q 0 A Q , 𝐝 ​ ( q ) ​ z 𝐝 , \mathrm{ch}^{\mathbf{Z}}(\mathfrak{n}_{Q}^{\mathbf{N},+})=\sum_{\mathbf{d}\in\mathbf{N}^{Q_{0}}}A_{Q,\mathbf{d}}(q)z^{\mathbf{d}}, 
 
 
 then the  𝐍 × 𝐍 Q 0 \mathbf{N}\times\mathbf{N}^{Q_{0}} -graded dimension of the space  ⨁ 𝐝 ∈ 𝐍 Q 0 ∖ { 0 } V 𝐝 \bigoplus_{\mathbf{d}\in\mathbf{N}^{Q_{0}}\smallsetminus\{0\}}V_{\mathbf{d}}  of simple roots of  𝔤 Q 𝐍 \mathfrak{g}_{Q}^{\mathbf{N}}  is given by the generating series 
 

 
 (9.2) 
 
 ch 𝐙 ​ ( V 𝐝 ) = ∑ 𝐝 ∈ 𝐍 Q 0 ∖ { 0 } C Q , 𝐝 abs ​ ( q ) ​ z 𝐝 , \mathrm{ch}^{\mathbf{Z}}(V_{\mathbf{d}})=\sum_{\mathbf{d}\in\mathbf{N}^{Q_{0}}\smallsetminus\{0\}}C^{\mathrm{abs}}_{Q,\mathbf{d}}(q)z^{\mathbf{d}}, 
 
 
 see  [ BS19 ] . 
 
 
 Theorem 9.2   ( [ BS19 , Theorem 1.4, Theorem 1.6] ) . 
 
 The polynomials  C Q , 𝐝 abs ​ ( q ) C_{Q,\mathbf{d}}^{\mathrm{abs}}(q)  have integer coefficients. 
 
 
 
 In  [ BS19 ]  Bozec and Schiffmann conjecture the following, see also  [ Sch18 , Conjecture 3.4] . 
 
 
 Conjecture 9.3 . 
 
 For any  𝐝 ∈ 𝐍 Q 0 \mathbf{d}\in\mathbf{N}^{Q_{0}} ,  C Q , 𝐝 abs ​ ( q ) ∈ 𝐍 ⁡ [ q ] C_{Q,\mathbf{d}}^{\mathrm{abs}}(q)\in\mathbf{N}[q] . 
 
 
 
 This conjecture is known for isotropic dimension vectors  [ DX03 ,  BS19 ,  Hen21 ] , and Theorem  9.5  establishes it in full generality. For isotropic dimension vectors, we have the following, from  [ BS19 , §1.2 ii)] . 
 
 
 Proposition 9.4 . 
 
 Let  𝐝 ∈ 𝐍 Q 0 \mathbf{d}\in\mathbf{N}^{Q_{0}}  be isotropic:  χ Q ​ ( 𝐝 , 𝐝 ) = 0 \chi_{Q}(\mathbf{d},\mathbf{d})=0 . We have: 
 

 
 
 C Q , 𝐝 abs ( q ) = { q ​  if the support of  𝐝  is an affine quiver or the Jordan quiver 0 ​  otherwise. C^{\mathrm{abs}}_{Q,\mathbf{d}}(q)=\left\{\begin{aligned} &q\text{ if the support of $\mathbf{d}$ is an affine quiver or the Jordan quiver}\\
&0\text{ otherwise.}\end{aligned}\right. 
 
 
 
 
 
 Let  Q Q  be a quiver. Assuming that the coefficients of the polynomials  C Q , 𝐝 abs ​ ( q ) C_{Q,\mathbf{d}}^{\mathrm{abs}}(q)  are nonnegative, Bozec and Schiffmann prove that the  𝐍 Q 0 × 𝐍 \mathbf{N}^{Q_{0}}\times\mathbf{N} -graded Lie algebra  𝔫 Q 𝐍 , + ≔ 𝔫 𝐍 Q 0 , ( − , − ) , C Q , 𝐝 abs + \mathfrak{n}_{Q}^{\mathbf{N},+}\coloneqq\mathfrak{n}^{+}_{\mathbf{N}^{Q_{0}},(-,-),C_{Q,\mathbf{d}}^{\mathrm{abs}}}  is such that 
 

 
 (9.3) 
 
 ch 𝐙 ​ ( 𝐔 ⁡ ( 𝔫 Q 𝐍 , + ) ) = Exp q , z ⁡ ( ∑ 𝐝 ∈ 𝐍 Q 0 ∖ { 0 } A Q , 𝐝 ​ ( q ) ​ z 𝐝 ) . \mathrm{ch}^{\mathbf{Z}}(\UEA(\mathfrak{n}^{\mathbf{N},+}_{Q}))=\Exp_{q,z}\left(\sum_{\mathbf{d}\in\mathbf{N}^{Q_{0}}\smallsetminus\{0\}}A_{Q,\mathbf{d}}(q)z^{\mathbf{d}}\right). 
 
 
 
 
 By the graded PBW theorem, this is equivalent to the equality ( 9.2 ). Conversely, if such a Borcherds Lie algebra  𝔤 Q 𝐍 \mathfrak{g}_{Q}^{\mathbf{N}}  exists, then the polynomials  C Q , 𝐝 abs ​ ( q ) C^{\mathrm{abs}}_{Q,\mathbf{d}}(q)  have nonnegative coefficients: they are given by the equality 
 
 
 

 
 (9.4)

 By  [ Dav20 , §1.2] , the 3d BPS Lie algebra ,  𝔤 Q ~ , W ~ ≅ H ∗ ⁡ ℬ ​ 𝒫 ​ 𝒮 Π Q , Lie 𝟹 ​ 𝚍 \mathfrak{g}_{\widetilde{Q},\widetilde{W}}\cong\HO^{*}\mathcal{BPS}_{\Pi_{Q},\mathrm{Lie}}^{\mathtt{3d}} , is a  𝐍 Q 0 × 2 ​ 𝐍 ⩽ 0 \mathbf{N}^{Q_{0}}\times 2\mathbf{N}_{\leqslant 0} -graded Lie algebra with character 
 
 
 

 
 (9.5) 
 
 ch 𝐙 ​ ( 𝔤 Q ~ , W ~ ) = ∑ 𝐝 ∈ 𝐍 Q 0 A Q , 𝐝 ​ ( q − 1 ) ​ z 𝐝 \mathrm{ch}^{\mathbf{Z}}(\mathfrak{g}_{\widetilde{Q},\widetilde{W}})=\sum_{\mathbf{d}\in\mathbf{N}^{Q_{0}}}A_{Q,\mathbf{d}}(q^{-1})z^{\mathbf{d}} 
 
 
 which by Proposition  8.4  is isomorphic to  𝔫 Π Q BPS , + \mathfrak{n}^{\mathtt{\aBPS},+}_{\Pi_{Q}} . Via this isomorphism  𝔤 Q ~ , W ~ \mathfrak{g}_{\widetilde{Q},\widetilde{W}}  is identified with the positive part of a GKM associated to the monoid  𝐍 Q 0 \mathbf{N}^{Q_{0}}  with the bilinear form  ( − , − ) Q (-,-)_{Q} , symmetrisation of the Euler form of  Q Q , and with graded dimension of the spaces of generators given by 
 

 
 (9.6)
