 10.3.  Lowest weight modules 

 
 Recall from § 3.3  the definition of lowest weight modules over generalised Kac–Moody Lie algebras. We will use the following easy algebraic lemma. 
 
 
 Lemma 10.4 . 
 
 Let  M ′ M^{\prime}  be a monoid, satisfying our standing assumptions that the morphism  + +  has finite fibres, and the morphism  M ′ → M ′ ^ M^{\prime}\rightarrow\widehat{M^{\prime}}  is an embedding in a lattice. Let  ( − , − ) (-,-)  be a bilinear form on the monoid  M = M ′ × 𝐍 M=M^{\prime}\times\mathbf{N} . Let 
 

 
 
 P : Φ M , ( − , − ) + → 𝐍 [ t ± 1 / 2 ] m ↦ P m ​ ( t 1 / 2 ) \begin{matrix}P&\colon&\Phi_{M,(-,-)}^{+}&\rightarrow&\mathbf{N}[t^{\pm 1/2}]\\
&&m&\mapsto&P_{m}(t^{1/2})\end{matrix} 
 
 
 be a weight function, with  𝔤 M , ( − , − ) , P \mathfrak{g}_{M,(-,-),P}  the associated generalised Kac–Moody algebra, so that for each  m ∈ Φ M , ( − , − ) + m\in\Phi_{M,(-,-)}^{+}  we may define a cohomologically graded vector space  V m ⊆ 𝔤 M , ( − , − ) , P V_{m}\subseteq\mathfrak{g}_{M,(-,-),P}  of generators of weight  m m , with characteristic polynomial  ch 𝐙 ​ ( V m ) = P m ​ ( t 1 / 2 ) \mathrm{ch}^{\mathbf{Z}}(V_{m})=P_{m}(t^{1/2}) . We assume that  P ⁡ ( m ) = 1 P(m)=1  for  m ∈ Φ M , ( − , − ) + , m\in\Phi^{+,\real}_{M,(-,-)} . Restricting  ( − , − ) (-,-)  to  M ′ = M × { 0 } M^{\prime}=M\times\{0\} , and restricting  P P  to  Φ M ′ , ( − , − ) + \Phi_{M^{\prime},(-,-)}^{+}  we define also  𝔤 ≔ 𝔤 M ′ , ( − , − ) , P \mathfrak{g}\coloneqq\mathfrak{g}_{M^{\prime},(-,-),P} . For  𝐟 ∈ M ′ ^ ∨ = Hom ⁡ ( M ′ ^ , 𝐙 ) \mathbf{f}\in\widehat{M^{\prime}}^{\vee}=\operatorname{Hom}(\widehat{M^{\prime}},\mathbf{Z})  we denote by  L 𝐟 L_{\mathbf{f}}  the associated simple lowest weight  𝔤 \mathfrak{g} -module (§ 3.3 ). Then there is a direct sum decomposition of  𝔤 \mathfrak{g} -modules 
 

 
 (10.1) 
 
 ⨁ m ′ ∈ M ′ 𝔤 M , ( − , − ) , P , ( m ′ , 1 ) ≅ ⨁ m ′ ∈ M ′ V ( m ′ , 1 ) ⊗ L λ m ′ \bigoplus_{m^{\prime}\in M^{\prime}}\mathfrak{g}_{M,(-,-),P,(m^{\prime},1)}\cong\bigoplus_{m^{\prime}\in M^{\prime}}V_{(m^{\prime},1)}\otimes L_{\lambda_{m^{\prime}}} 
 
 
 where we define the linear map 
 

 
 
 λ m ′ : M ′ \displaystyle\lambda_{m^{\prime}}\colon M^{\prime} 
 → 𝐙 \displaystyle\rightarrow\mathbf{Z} 
 
 
 
 n ′ \displaystyle n^{\prime} 
 ↦ ( ( m ′ , 1 ) , ( n ′ , 0 ) ) . \displaystyle\mapsto((m^{\prime},1),(n^{\prime},0)). 
 
 
 
 
 
 Proof. 
 
 We denote the left hand side of ( 10.1 ) by  L L . Then it is easy to see that  L L  is a lowest weight module for the action of  𝔤 \mathfrak{g} , and the spaces  V ( m ′ , 1 ) V_{(m^{\prime},1)}  for  ( m ′ , 1 ) ∈ Φ M ′ , ( − , − ) + (m^{\prime},1)\in\Phi^{+}_{M^{\prime},(-,-)}  are lowest weight spaces of weight  λ m ′ \lambda_{m^{\prime}}  which generate  L L  a a  𝔤 \mathfrak{g} -module. On the other hand  [ Bor88 , Corollary 2.5]   𝔤 M , ( − , − ) , P \mathfrak{g}_{M,(-,-),P}  carries an invariant bilinear form  ⟨ − , − ⟩ \langle-,-\rangle  such that  ( − , − ) 0 ≔ ⟨ − , ω ⁡ ( − ) ⟩ (-,-)_{0}\coloneqq\langle-,\omega(-)\rangle  is positive definite on  ⨁ m ′ ∈ M ′ 𝔤 M , ( − , − ) , P , ( m ′ , 1 ) \bigoplus_{m^{\prime}\in M^{\prime}}\mathfrak{g}_{M,(-,-),P,(m^{\prime},1)} , where  ω \omega  is the Cartan involution from § 3.4 . It follows that  ⨁ m ′ ∈ M ′ 𝔤 M , ( − , − ) , P , ( m ′ , 1 ) \bigoplus_{m^{\prime}\in M^{\prime}}\mathfrak{g}_{M,(-,-),P,(m^{\prime},1)}  is semisimple as a  𝔤 \mathfrak{g} -representation, and the result follows.
∎ 
 
 
 
 For  𝐞 ∈ N = Hom ⁡ ( 𝐙 Q 0 , 𝐙 ) \mathbf{e}\in N=\operatorname{Hom}(\mathbf{Z}^{Q_{0}},\mathbf{Z})  we denote by  L 𝐞 △ L_{\mathbf{e}}^{\triangle}  the lowest weight module for  𝔤 Π Q BPS , △ \mathfrak{g}_{\Pi_{Q}}^{\mathtt{\aBPS},\triangle}  of lowest weight  𝐞 \mathbf{e} . 
 
 
 The following is now our main theorem on the representation theory of  𝔤 Π Q BPS \mathfrak{g}_{\Pi_{Q}}^{\mathtt{\aBPS}} , and more generally  𝔤 Π Q BPS , △ \mathfrak{g}_{\Pi_{Q}}^{\mathtt{\aBPS},\triangle} . 
 
 
 Theorem 10.5 . 
 
 The  𝔫 Π Q BPS , △ , + \mathfrak{n}_{\Pi_{Q}}^{\mathtt{\aBPS},\triangle,+} -action on  𝕄 𝐟 △ , ψ ​ ( Q ) \mathbb{M}_{\mathbf{f}}^{\triangle,\psi}(Q)  extends to a  𝔤 Π Q BPS , △ \mathfrak{g}_{\Pi_{Q}}^{\mathtt{\aBPS},\triangle} -action, for which  𝕄 𝐟 △ , ψ ​ ( Q ) \mathbb{M}^{\triangle,\psi}_{\mathbf{f}}(Q)  decomposes as a direct sum of cohomologically shifted simple lowest weight representations  L 𝐞 △ L^{\triangle}_{\mathbf{e}} . 
 
 
 Precisely we have 
 

 
 (10.2) 
 
 𝕄 𝐟 △ , ψ ( Q ) ≅ ⨁ ( 𝐝 , 1 ) ∈ Φ Π Q 𝐟 + H ∗ ( ı △ ! ℐ 𝒞 ( ℳ Π Q 𝐟 , ( 𝐝 , 1 ) ) ) ⊗ L ( ( 𝐝 , 1 ) , ( − , 0 ) ) Q 𝐟 △ . \mathbb{M}^{\triangle,\psi}_{\mathbf{f}}(Q)\cong\bigoplus_{(\mathbf{d},1)\in\Phi_{\Pi_{Q_{\mathbf{f}}}}^{+}}\HO^{*}\!(\imath_{\triangle}^{!}\mathcal{IC}(\mathcal{M}_{\Pi_{Q_{\mathbf{f}}},(\mathbf{d},1)}))\otimes L_{((\mathbf{d},1),(-,0))_{Q_{\mathbf{f}}}}^{\triangle}. 
 
 
 
 
 
 We can rewrite the weight appearing in the theorem 
 

 
 
 ( ( 𝐝 , 1 ) , ( − , 0 ) ) Q 𝐟 : 𝐝 ′ ↦ ( 𝐝 , 𝐝 ′ ) Q − 𝐟 ⋅ 𝐝 ′ . ((\mathbf{d},1),(-,0))_{Q_{\mathbf{f}}}\colon\mathbf{d}^{\prime}\mapsto(\mathbf{d},\mathbf{d}^{\prime})_{Q}-\mathbf{f}\cdot\mathbf{d}^{\prime}. 
 
 
 
 
 Proof. 
 
 By Proposition  10.3  and the remarks following it there is an isomorphism of  𝔫 Π Q BPS , △ , + = ⨁ 0 ≠ m ′ ∈ 𝐍 Q 0 𝔫 Π Q 𝐟 , ( m ′ , 0 ) BPS , △ , + \mathfrak{n}_{\Pi_{Q}}^{\mathtt{\aBPS},\triangle,+}=\bigoplus_{0\neq m^{\prime}\in\mathbf{N}^{Q_{0}}}\mathfrak{n}_{\Pi_{Q_{\mathbf{f}}},(m^{\prime},0)}^{\mathtt{\aBPS},\triangle,+} -modules 
 

 
 
 𝕄 𝐟 △ , ψ ​ ( Q ) ≅ ⨁ m ′ ∈ 𝐍 Q 0 𝔫 Π Q 𝐟 , ( m ′ , 1 ) BPS , △ , + , \mathbb{M}^{\triangle,\psi}_{\mathbf{f}}(Q)\cong\bigoplus_{m^{\prime}\in\mathbf{N}^{Q_{0}}}\mathfrak{n}_{\Pi_{Q_{\mathbf{f}}},(m^{\prime},1)}^{\mathtt{\aBPS},\triangle,+}, 
 
 
 and so the action of  𝔫 Π Q BPS , △ , + \mathfrak{n}_{\Pi_{Q}}^{\mathtt{\aBPS},\triangle,+}  extends to a  𝔤 Π Q BPS , △ \mathfrak{g}_{\Pi_{Q}}^{\mathtt{\aBPS},\triangle} -action via the identification 
 

 
 
 𝔤 Π Q BPS , △ = ⨁ 𝐝 ∈ 𝐙 Q 0 𝔤 Π Q 𝐟 , ( 𝐝 , 0 ) BPS , △ \mathfrak{g}_{\Pi_{Q}}^{\mathtt{\aBPS},\triangle}=\bigoplus_{\mathbf{d}\in\mathbf{Z}^{Q_{0}}}\mathfrak{g}_{\Pi_{Q_{\mathbf{f}}},(\mathbf{d},0)}^{\mathtt{\aBPS},\triangle} 
 
 
 and the action of  𝔤 Π Q 𝐟 BPS , △ \mathfrak{g}_{\Pi_{Q_{\mathbf{f}}}}^{\mathtt{\aBPS},\triangle}  on itself. The theorem then follows from Lemma  10.4  and the characterisation of simple roots of  𝔤 Π Q 𝐟 BPS , △ \mathfrak{g}_{\Pi_{Q_{\mathbf{f}}}}^{\mathtt{\aBPS},\triangle}  given in Theorem  1.2 .
∎ 
 
 
 
 Corollary 10.6 . 
 
 Let  𝐟 ∈ 𝐍 Q 0 \mathbf{f}\in\mathbf{N}^{Q_{0}}  have full support. The action of  𝔤 Π Q BPS , △ \mathfrak{g}_{\Pi_{Q}}^{\mathtt{\aBPS},\triangle}  on  𝕄 𝐟 △ ​ ( Q ) \mathbb{M}^{\triangle}_{\mathbf{f}}(Q)  is faithful. 
 
 
 
 Proof. 
 
 By Theorem  10.5  there is a summand  L ( ( 0 , 1 ) , ( − , 0 ) ) Q 𝐟 △ ⊆ 𝕄 𝐟 △ , ψ ​ ( Q ) L_{((0,1),(-,0))_{Q_{\mathbf{f}}}}^{\triangle}\subseteq\mathbb{M}^{\triangle,\psi}_{\mathbf{f}}(Q) , and in particular, a lowest weight vector  e ∞ ∈ 𝕄 𝐟 △ , ψ ​ ( Q ) e_{\infty}\in\mathbb{M}^{\triangle,\psi}_{\mathbf{f}}(Q)  of degree  ( 0 , … , 0 , 1 ) ∈ 𝐍 ( Q Π 𝐟 ) 0 (0,\ldots,0,1)\in\mathbf{N}^{(Q_{\Pi_{\mathbf{f}}})_{0}} , which we identify as one of the Chevalley generators of  𝔤 Π Q 𝐟 BPS , △ \mathfrak{g}^{\mathtt{\aBPS},\triangle}_{\Pi_{Q_{\mathbf{f}}}} , of degree  ( 0 , … , 0 , 1 ) (0,\ldots,0,1) , forming part of the  𝔰 ​ 𝔩 2 \mathfrak{sl}_{2} -triple  e ∞ , f ∞ , h ∞ e_{\infty},f_{\infty},h_{\infty} . Now let  v ∈ 𝔫 Π Q , 𝐝 BPS , △ , + v\in\mathfrak{n}_{\Pi_{Q},\mathbf{d}}^{\mathtt{\aBPS},\triangle,+} . Then  [ h ∞ , v ] = ( − 𝐟 ⋅ 𝐝 ) v ≠ 0 [h_{\infty},v]=(-\mathbf{f}\cdot\mathbf{d})v\neq 0  (by our assumption on  𝐟 \mathbf{f} ), so by the representation theory of  𝔰 ​ 𝔩 2 \mathfrak{sl}_{2}  we have  [ v , e ∞ ] ≠ 0 [v,e_{\infty}]\neq 0 . We deduce that the  𝔫 Π Q , 𝐝 BPS , △ , + \mathfrak{n}_{\Pi_{Q},\mathbf{d}}^{\mathtt{\aBPS},\triangle,+} -action and the  𝔥 Π Q , 𝐝 BPS , △ \mathfrak{h}_{\Pi_{Q},\mathbf{d}}^{\mathtt{\aBPS},\triangle} -action are faithful. 
 
 
 With  ( − , − ) 0 (-,-)_{0}  the positive definite form from the proof of Lemma  10.4 , we have  ( e ∞ , [ w ⁡ ( v ) , [ v , e ∞ ] ] ) 0 = ( [ v , e ∞ ] , [ v , e ∞ ] ) 0 ≠ 0 (e_{\infty},[w(v),[v,e_{\infty}]])_{0}=([v,e_{\infty}],[v,e_{\infty}])_{0}\neq 0  and so the  𝔫 Π Q , 𝐝 BPS , △ , − \mathfrak{n}_{\Pi_{Q},\mathbf{d}}^{\mathtt{\aBPS},\triangle,-} -action is also faithful.
∎ 
 
 
 
 
 10.4.  Actions on cohomology of quiver varieties, revisited 

 
 10.4.1.  Hilbert schemes of  𝐀 2 \mathbf{A}^{2}  and Heisenberg algebras 

 
 First we recover the famous description of the cohomology of Hilbert schemes of  𝐀 2 \mathbf{A}^{2}  as an irreducible lowest weight module for an infinite-dimensional Heisenberg algebra, as in  [ Nak99 ,  Nak97 ] . 
 
 
 Let  Q Q  be the Jordan quiver, with one vertex and one loop, and let  𝐟 = 1 \mathbf{f}=1 , so that  Q 𝐟 ¯ \overline{Q_{\mathbf{f}}}  is the ADHM quiver (with the help of which the construction of instantons in  [ Ati+94 ]  can be formulated): 
 

 
 
 ∙ {\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\bullet} ∙ . {\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\bullet.} 
 
 
 It is easy to check that in this case  ( 0 , 1 ) (0,1)  is the only element of  Φ Π Q 𝐟 + \Phi^{+}_{\Pi_{Q_{\mathbf{f}}}}  of the form  ( n , 1 ) (n,1)  (using the characterisation given in Proposition  5.3  and the definition of the set of roots). In addition, there is a natural isomorphism  [ Nak99 ] 
 

 
 
 N ⁡ ( Q , n , 1 ) ≅ Hilb n ⁡ ( 𝐀 2 ) . N(Q,n,1)\cong\Hilb_{n}(\mathbf{A}^{2}). 
 
 
 By definition  𝔫 Π Q , n BPS , + ≅ H ∗ ⁡ ℐ ​ 𝒞 ​ ( 𝐀 2 ) ≅ 𝐐 ⁡ [ 2 ] \mathfrak{n}^{\mathtt{\aBPS},+}_{\Pi_{Q},n}\cong\HO^{*}\mathcal{IC}(\mathbf{A}^{2})\cong\mathbf{Q}[2] , and  𝔫 Π Q BPS , + \mathfrak{n}^{\mathtt{\aBPS},+}_{\Pi_{Q}}  is the cohomologically graded generalised Kac–Moody Lie algebra determined by the weight function 
 

 
 
 P : Φ Π Q + ≅ 𝐙 ⩾ 1 → \displaystyle P\colon\Phi^{+}_{\Pi_{Q}}\cong\mathbf{Z}_{\geqslant 1}\rightarrow 
 𝐍 ⁡ [ t ± 1 ] \displaystyle\mathbf{N}[t^{\pm 1}] 
 
 
 
 n ↦ \displaystyle n\mapsto 
 t − 1 . \displaystyle t^{-1}. 
 
 
 Precisely,  𝔤 Π Q BPS \mathfrak{g}_{\Pi_{Q}}^{\aBPS}  is the generalised Kac–Moody Lie algebra spanned by elements  e n , f n , h n e_{n},f_{n},h_{n}  of respective cohomological degrees  − 2 , 2 , 0 -2,2,0  and  𝐙 Q 0 \mathbf{Z}^{Q_{0}} -degree  n , 0 , − n n,0,-n  respectively, i.e. the infinite-dimensional Heisenberg algebra. Since all but one of the summands on the right hand side of ( 10.2 ) vanish, Theorem  10.5  yields 
 

 
 
 ⨁ n ⩾ 0 H ∗ ⁡ ( Hilb n ⁡ ( 𝐀 2 ) , 𝐐 vir ) ≅ L 1 \bigoplus_{n\geqslant 0}\HO^{*}\!(\Hilb_{n}(\mathbf{A}^{2}),\mathbf{Q}^{\mathrm{vir}})\cong L_{1} 
 
 
 where  L 1 = 𝐐 ⁡ [ e 1 , e 2 , … ] L_{1}=\mathbf{Q}[e_{1},e_{2},\ldots]  is the irreducible lowest weight module for  𝔤 Π Q BPS \mathfrak{g}_{\Pi_{Q}}^{\mathtt{\aBPS}}  of weight  1 1 . 
 
 
 
 10.4.2.  Irreducible lowest weight  𝔤 Π Q BPS , 𝒮 ​ 𝒮 ​ 𝒩 \mathfrak{g}_{\Pi_{Q}}^{\mathtt{\aBPS},\mathcal{SSN}} -modules from Nakajima quiver varieties 

 
 In this section we recover Nakajima’s original construction  [ Nak98 ]  of lowest weight representations of Kac–Moody Lie algebras by restricting to the zeroth cohomological degree in our main theorem (Theorem  10.5 ) on the representation theory of  𝔤 Π Q BPS , △ \mathfrak{g}^{\mathtt{\aBPS},\triangle}_{\Pi_{Q}}  for the choice  △ = 𝒮 ​ 𝒮 ​ 𝒩 \triangle=\mathcal{SSN} . 
 
 
 Let  Q Q  be a quiver. Let  𝐟 ∈ 𝐍 Q 0 \mathbf{f}\in\mathbf{N}^{Q_{0}}  be a dimension vector. Consider the CoHA  H 0 ⁡ ( 𝒜 Π Q 𝒮 ​ 𝒮 ​ 𝒩 , ψ ) \HO^{0}(\mathscr{A}_{\Pi_{Q}}^{\mathcal{SSN},\psi})  obtained by taking the zeroth cohomologically graded piece of the CoHA  H ∗ ⁡ 𝒜 Π Q 𝒮 ​ 𝒮 ​ 𝒩 , ψ \HO^{*}\!\mathscr{A}_{\Pi_{Q}}^{\mathcal{SSN},\psi} . Recall that by Theorem  7.9  this is isomorphic to the enveloping algebra of half of the Borcherds–Bozec algebra  𝔤 Q \mathfrak{g}_{Q} . By Theorem  1.2 , if  𝐝 ∈ Σ Π Q 𝐟 \mathbf{d}\in\Sigma_{\Pi_{Q_{\mathbf{f}}}}  there is an identification between 
 

 
 
 H 0 ( ι 𝒮 ​ 𝒮 ​ 𝒩 ! ℐ 𝒞 ( ℳ Π Q 𝐟 , 𝐝 ) ) \HO^{0}(\iota_{\mathcal{SSN}}^{!}\mathcal{IC}(\mathcal{M}_{\Pi_{Q_{\mathbf{f}}},\mathbf{d}})) 
 
 
 and a basis of Chevalley generators of weight  𝐝 \mathbf{d}  inside  𝔤 Q 𝐟 \mathfrak{g}_{Q_{\mathbf{f}}} : in particular, the above cohomology group vanishes if  𝐝 \mathbf{d}  is supported at more than one vertex. Applying  H 0 ⁡ ( − ) \HO^{0}(-)  to the isomorphism 
 

 
 
 𝕄 𝐟 𝒮 ​ 𝒮 ​ 𝒩 ( Q ) ≅ ⨁ ( 𝐝 , 1 ) ∈ Φ Q 𝐟 + H ∗ ( ι 𝒮 ​ 𝒮 ​ 𝒩 ! ℐ 𝒞 ( ℳ Π Q 𝐟 , ( 𝐝 , 1 ) ) ) ⊗ L ( ( 𝐝 , 1 ) , ( − , 0 ) ) Q 𝐟 \mathbb{M}^{\mathcal{SSN}}_{\mathbf{f}}(Q)\cong\bigoplus_{(\mathbf{d},1)\in\Phi_{Q_{\mathbf{f}}}^{+}}\HO^{*}\!(\iota_{\mathcal{SSN}}^{!}\mathcal{IC}(\mathcal{M}_{\Pi_{Q_{\mathbf{f}}},(\mathbf{d},1)}))\otimes L_{((\mathbf{d},1),(-,0))_{Q_{\mathbf{f}}}} 
 
 
 we thus find that every summand apart from the one for  ( 𝐝 , 1 ) = ( 0 , … , 0 , 1 ) ∈ Φ Π Q 𝐟 + (\mathbf{d},1)=(0,\ldots,0,1)\in\Phi^{+}_{\Pi_{Q_{\mathbf{f}}}}  vanishes, so that we obtain the following theorem 
 
 
 Theorem 10.7 . 
 
 There is an isomorphism of  𝐔 ⁡ ( 𝔤 Q ) = H 0 ⁡ ( 𝒜 Π Q 𝒮 ​ 𝒮 ​ 𝒩 , ψ ) \UEA(\mathfrak{g}_{Q})=\HO^{0}(\mathscr{A}_{\Pi_{Q}}^{\mathcal{SSN},\psi}) -modules 
 

 
 
 ⨁ 𝐝 ∈ 𝐍 Q 0 H 0 ( N 𝒮 ​ 𝒮 ​ 𝒩 ( Q , 𝐝 , 𝐟 ) , ι 𝒮 ​ 𝒮 ​ 𝒩 ! 𝐐 vir ) ≅ L − 𝐟 ⋅ . \bigoplus_{\mathbf{d}\in\mathbf{N}^{Q_{0}}}\HO^{0}(N^{\mathcal{SSN}}(Q,\mathbf{d},\mathbf{f}),\iota_{\mathcal{SSN}}^{!}\mathbf{Q}^{\mathrm{vir}})\cong L_{-\mathbf{f}\cdot}. 
 
 
 
 
 
 Here, we denote by  L − 𝐟 ⋅ L_{-\mathbf{f}\cdot}  the simple lowest weight module of  𝔤 Q \mathfrak{g}_{Q}  of lowest weight  𝐝 ↦ − 𝐟 ⋅ 𝐝 \mathbf{d}\mapsto-\mathbf{f}\cdot\mathbf{d} , the usual dot product as in § 2 . In the special case in which  Q Q  has no loops, this is Nakajima’s original theorem  [ Nak98 , Thm.10.2] . In the case in which there are loops, this is a CoHA analogue of results in  [ Boz16 ]  on highest weight crystals for the Borcherds–Bozec algebra. 
 
 
 
 
 
 11.  Equivariant parameters 

 
 11.1.  Deformed BPS algebra 

 
 In this section, we explain how to adapt the main results of this paper when we turn on equivariant parameters. We let  𝒜 \mathcal{A}  and  𝙹𝙷 : 𝔐 𝒜 → ℳ 𝒜 \JH\colon\mathfrak{M}_{\mathcal{A}}\rightarrow\mathcal{M}_{\mathcal{A}}  be as in § 4 . We let  G G  be an algebraic group and we assume that  G G  acts on  𝙹𝙷 \JH  and that this action is compatible with the direct sum morphism: if  G G  acts diagonally on  𝔐 𝒜 × 𝔐 𝒜 \mathfrak{M}_{\mathcal{A}}\times\mathfrak{M}_{\mathcal{A}} , the direct sum morphism  ⊕ : 𝔐 𝒜 × 𝔐 𝒜 → 𝔐 𝒜 \oplus\colon\mathfrak{M}_{\mathcal{A}}\times\mathfrak{M}_{\mathcal{A}}\rightarrow\mathfrak{M}_{\mathcal{A}}  is  G G -equivariant. To avoid discussing group actions on stacks  [ Rom05 ] , we assume that each connected component of  𝔐 𝒜 \mathfrak{M}_{\mathcal{A}}  is a global quotient stack  X / H X/H  of an algebraic variety  X X  by an algebraic group  H H , and  G G  acts on  X X  giving a  G × H G\times H -action on  X X  (i.e. the  G G  and  H H  actions commute with each other). 
 
 
 We indicate by the  G G -superscript the objects and maps induced when taking the stack-theoretic quotient by  G G . For example, we let  𝔐 𝒜 G ≔ 𝔐 𝒜 / G \mathfrak{M}_{\mathcal{A}}^{G}\coloneqq\mathfrak{M}_{\mathcal{A}}/G  (for a given connected component  X / H X/H  of  𝔐 𝒜 \mathfrak{M}_{\mathcal{A}} , there is a connected component  X / ( H × G ) X/(H\times G)  of  𝔐 𝒜 G \mathfrak{M}_{\mathcal{A}}^{G} ),  ℳ 𝒜 G ≔ ℳ 𝒜 / G \mathcal{M}_{\mathcal{A}}^{G}\coloneqq\mathcal{M}_{\mathcal{A}}/G ,  𝙹𝙷 G : 𝔐 𝒜 G → ℳ 𝒜 G \JH^{G}\colon\mathfrak{M}_{\mathcal{A}}^{G}\rightarrow\mathcal{M}_{\mathcal{A}}^{G} ,  ⊕ G : ℳ 𝒜 G × B ​ G ℳ 𝒜 G → ℳ 𝒜 G \oplus^{G}\colon\mathcal{M}_{\mathcal{A}}^{G}\times_{\mathrm{B}G}\mathcal{M}_{\mathcal{A}}^{G}\rightarrow\mathcal{M}_{\mathcal{A}}^{G} . Note that the source of the map  ⊕ G \oplus^{G}  can be identified with the stack quotient  ( ℳ 𝒜 × ℳ 𝒜 ) / G (\mathcal{M}_{\mathcal{A}}\times\mathcal{M}_{\mathcal{A}})/G  by the diagonal action of  G G . 
 
 
 We have the (smooth) quotient map  π : ℳ 𝒜 → ℳ 𝒜 G \pi\colon\mathcal{M}_{\mathcal{A}}\rightarrow\mathcal{M}_{\mathcal{A}}^{G} , and the commutative square 
 

 
 (11.1) 
 
 ℳ 𝒜 × ℳ 𝒜 {\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathcal{M}_{\mathcal{A}}\times\mathcal{M}_{\mathcal{A}}}} ℳ 𝒜 {\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathcal{M}_{\mathcal{A}}}} ℳ 𝒜 G × B ​ G ℳ 𝒜 G {\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathcal{M}_{\mathcal{A}}^{G}\times_{\mathrm{B}G}\mathcal{M}_{\mathcal{A}}^{G}}} ℳ 𝒜 G {\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathcal{M}_{\mathcal{A}}^{G}}} π × π \scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\pi\times\pi} ⊕ G \scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\oplus^{G}} ⊕ \scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\oplus} π \scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\pi} ⌟ \scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\lrcorner} 
 
 
 is Cartesian. 
 
 
 The categories  Perv ⁡ ( ℳ 𝒜 G ) ​ [ dim G ] \operatorname{Perv}(\mathcal{M}_{\mathcal{A}}^{G})[\dim G] ,  MHM ⁡ ( ℳ 𝒜 G ) ​ [ dim G ] \mathrm{MHM}(\mathcal{M}_{\mathcal{A}}^{G})[\dim G] ,  𝒟 c + ​ ( ℳ 𝒜 G ) \mathcal{D}^{+}_{\mathrm{c}}(\mathcal{M}_{\mathcal{A}}^{G})  and  𝒟 + ​ ( MHM ⁡ ( ℳ 𝒜 G ) ) \mathcal{D}^{+}(\mathrm{MHM}(\mathcal{M}_{\mathcal{A}}^{G}))  all carry a symmetric monoidal tensor product defined by  ℱ ⊡ G 𝒢 ≔ ⊕ ∗ G ( ℱ ⊠ 𝒢 ) \mathscr{F}\boxdot^{G}\mathscr{G}\coloneqq\oplus_{*}^{G}(\mathscr{F}\boxtimes\mathscr{G}) . 
 
 
 By smooth base change in the diagram ( 11.1 ),  π ∗ : 𝒟 + ​ ( MHM ⁡ ( ℳ 𝒜 G ) ) → 𝒟 + ​ ( MHM ⁡ ( ℳ 𝒜 ) ) \pi^{*}\colon\mathcal{D}^{+}(\mathrm{MHM}(\mathcal{M}_{\mathcal{A}}^{G}))\rightarrow\mathcal{D}^{+}(\mathrm{MHM}(\mathcal{M}_{\mathcal{A}}))  is a symmetric monoidal functor, mapping objects of  MHM ⁡ ( ℳ 𝒜 G ) ​ [ dim G ] \mathrm{MHM}(\mathcal{M}_{\mathcal{A}}^{G})[\dim G]  to objects of  MHM ⁡ ( ℳ 𝒜 ) \mathrm{MHM}(\mathcal{M}_{\mathcal{A}}) . We have a similar statement for the constructible derived categories and the categories of perverse sheaves. 
 
 
 We define  𝐐 ¯ 𝔐 𝒜 G vir \underline{\mathbf{Q}}_{\mathfrak{M}^{G}_{\mathcal{A}}}^{\mathrm{vir}}  by setting its restriction to the connected component  𝔐 𝒜 , a G \mathfrak{M}^{G}_{\mathcal{A},a} , to be  𝐐 ¯ 𝔐 𝒜 , a G ⊗ 𝐋 ( a , a ) 𝒞 / 2 \underline{\mathbf{Q}}_{\mathfrak{M}^{G}_{\mathcal{A},a}}\otimes\mathbf{L}^{(a,a)_{\mathscr{C}}/2} , as in the non-equivariant case § 6.1 . We gather in the following proposition the properties of the  G G -equivariant versions of the main objects of this paper. Parts (1) and (3) follow from (4), and the analogous results for  G = { 1 } G=\{1\} . 
 
 
 Proposition 11.1 . 
 
 
 (1)  
 
 The mixed Hodge module complex  𝒜 ¯ 𝒜 G ≔ ( 𝙹𝙷 𝒜 G ) ∗ ​ 𝐐 ¯ 𝔐 𝒜 G vir \underline{\mathscr{A}}_{\mathcal{A}}^{G}\coloneqq(\JH_{\mathcal{A}}^{G})_{*}\underline{\mathbf{Q}}_{\mathfrak{M}^{G}_{\mathcal{A}}}^{\mathrm{vir}}  is pure of weight zero, concentrated in cohomological degrees  ⩾ − dim G \geqslant-\dim G . 
 
 (2)  
 
 The relative CoHA has the structure of an algebra object in  𝒟 + ​ ( MHM ⁡ ( ℳ 𝒜 G ) ) \mathcal{D}^{+}(\mathrm{MHM}(\mathcal{M}_{\mathcal{A}}^{G}))  defined as in  [ DHS22 ]  (working over  B ​ G \mathrm{B}G ). We denote by  𝔪 G \mathfrak{m}^{G}  the multiplication map. 
 
 (3)  
 
 The degree  − dim G -\dim G  cohomology  ℬ ​ 𝒫 ​ 𝒮 ¯ 𝒜 , Alg G ≔ ℋ − dim G ( 𝒜 ¯ 𝒜 G ) [ dim G ] \underline{\mathcal{BPS}}_{\mathcal{A},\mathrm{Alg}}^{G}\coloneqq\mathcal{H}^{-\dim G}(\underline{\mathscr{A}}_{\mathcal{A}}^{G})[\dim G]  has an induced algebra structure. It is an algebra object in  MHM ⁡ ( ℳ 𝒜 G ) ​ [ dim G ] \mathrm{MHM}(\mathcal{M}_{\mathcal{A}}^{G})[\dim G] . 
 
 (4)  
 
 We have canonical identifications of algebra objects  π ∗ ​ 𝒜 ¯ 𝒜 G = 𝒜 ¯ 𝒜 \pi^{*}\underline{\mathscr{A}}_{\mathcal{A}}^{G}=\underline{\mathscr{A}}_{\mathcal{A}} ,  π ∗ ​ ℬ ​ 𝒫 ​ 𝒮 ¯ 𝒜 , Alg G = ℬ ​ 𝒫 ​ 𝒮 ¯ 𝒜 , Alg \pi^{*}\underline{\mathcal{BPS}}^{G}_{\mathcal{A},\mathrm{Alg}}=\underline{\mathcal{BPS}}_{\mathcal{A},\mathrm{Alg}} . 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 11.2.  Deformed relative BPS Lie algebra 

 
 For  l ∈ 𝐍 l\in\mathbf{N}  and  m ′ ∈ K ⁡ ( 𝒜 ) m^{\prime}\in\K(\mathcal{A}) , we let 
 

 
 
 u m G : ℳ 𝒜 , m ′ G → ℳ 𝒜 , l ​ m ′ G u_{m}^{G}\colon\mathcal{M}_{\mathcal{A},m^{\prime}}^{G}\rightarrow\mathcal{M}_{\mathcal{A},lm^{\prime}}^{G} 
 
 
 be the map obtained by composing the diagonal morphism  ℳ 𝒜 , m ′ G → ℳ 𝒜 , m ′ G × B ​ G … × B ​ G ℳ 𝒜 , m ′ G \mathcal{M}_{\mathcal{A},m^{\prime}}^{G}\rightarrow\mathcal{M}_{\mathcal{A},m^{\prime}}^{G}\times_{\mathrm{B}G}\ldots\times_{\mathrm{B}G}\mathcal{M}_{\mathcal{A},m^{\prime}}^{G}  with the (iterated) direct sum morphism  ℳ 𝒜 , m ′ G × B ​ G … × B ​ G ℳ 𝒜 , m ′ G → ℳ 𝒜 , l ​ m ′ G \mathcal{M}_{\mathcal{A},m^{\prime}}^{G}\times_{\mathrm{B}G}\ldots\times_{\mathrm{B}G}\mathcal{M}_{\mathcal{A},m^{\prime}}^{G}\rightarrow\mathcal{M}_{\mathcal{A},lm^{\prime}}^{G} .
We let 
 

 
 
 𝒢 ¯ 𝒜 , m G ≔ { ℐ ​ 𝒞 ¯ ​ ( ℳ 𝒜 , m G ) ​ [ dim G ]  if  m ∈ Σ K ⁡ ( 𝒜 ) ( u m G ) ∗ ​ ℐ ​ 𝒞 ¯ ​ ( ℳ 𝒜 , m ′ G ) ​ [ dim G ]  if  m = l ​ m ′ ∈ Φ + iso  with  m ′ ∈ Σ K ⁡ ( 𝒜 )  and  l ⩾ 2 0  otherwise. \underline{\mathscr{G}}^{G}_{\mathcal{A},m}\coloneqq\left\{\begin{aligned} &\underline{\mathcal{IC}}(\mathcal{M}_{\mathcal{A},m}^{G})[\dim G]\quad&\text{ if $m\in\Sigma_{\K(\mathcal{A})}$}\\
&(u_{m}^{G})_{*}\underline{\mathcal{IC}}(\mathcal{M}_{\mathcal{A},m^{\prime}}^{G})[\dim G]\quad&\text{ if $m=lm^{\prime}\in\Phi^{\mathrm{iso}}_{+}$ with $m^{\prime}\in\Sigma_{\K(\mathcal{A})}$ and $l\geqslant 2$}\\
&0\quad&\text{ otherwise.}\end{aligned}\right. 
 
 
 
 
 The construction of GKM Lie algebras and their enveloping algebras in the symmetric monoidal category of mixed Hodge modules on a monoid in the category of schemes as in § 3.5  adapts in a straightforward way to the situation where  ℳ \mathcal{M}  is replaced by a stack quotient of  ℳ \mathcal{M}  by an algebraic group  G G  and one works relatively over  B ​ G \mathrm{B}G . We let  ℳ G ≔ ℳ / G \mathcal{M}^{G}\coloneqq\mathcal{M}/G  and the direct sum map is a (finite) morphism  ⊕ : ℳ G × B ​ G ℳ G → ℳ G \oplus\colon\mathcal{M}^{G}\times_{\mathrm{B}G}\mathcal{M}^{G}\rightarrow\mathcal{M}^{G} . The free algebra is defined accordingly and one can make sense of Serre relations in the same way as for schemes, and we have the following: 
 
 
 Theorem 11.2 . 
 
 We have a canonical isomorphism of algebra objects in  MHM ⁡ ( ℳ 𝒜 G ) \mathrm{MHM}(\mathcal{M}_{\mathcal{A}}^{G}) 
 

 
 
 𝐔 ⁡ ( 𝔫 K ⁡ ( 𝒜 ) , ( − , − ) 𝒞 , 𝒢 ¯ 𝒜 G + ) ≅ ℬ ​ 𝒫 ​ 𝒮 ¯ 𝒜 , Alg G . \UEA(\mathfrak{n}^{+}_{\K(\mathcal{A}),(-,-)_{\mathscr{C}},\underline{\mathscr{G}}_{\mathcal{A}}^{G}})\cong\underline{\mathcal{BPS}}^{G}_{\mathcal{A},\mathrm{Alg}}. 
 
 
 
 
 
 Proof. 
 
 The forgetful functor  π ∗ : MHM ⁡ ( ℳ 𝒜 G ) ​ [ dim G ] → MHM ⁡ ( ℳ 𝒜 ) \pi^{*}\colon\mathrm{MHM}(\mathcal{M}_{\mathcal{A}}^{G})[\dim G]\rightarrow\mathrm{MHM}(\mathcal{M}_{\mathcal{A}})  is monoidal and conservative. Therefore, Theorem  11.2  follows from Theorem  1.1 .
∎ 
 
 
 
 We can therefore define (as in the case  G = { 1 } G=\{1\} ) the relative BPS Lie algebra as 
 

 
 
 ℬ ​ 𝒫 ​ 𝒮 ¯ 𝒜 , Lie G ≔ 𝔫 K ⁡ ( 𝒜 ) , ( − , − ) 𝒞 , 𝒢 ¯ 𝒜 G + . \underline{\mathcal{BPS}}_{\mathcal{A},\mathrm{Lie}}^{G}\coloneqq\mathfrak{n}^{+}_{\K(\mathcal{A}),(-,-)_{\mathscr{C}},\underline{\mathscr{G}}_{\mathcal{A}}^{G}}. 
 
 
 As in § 8.3.2 , we define a PBW map 
 

 
 (11.2) 
 
 Ψ 𝒜 ψ , G : Sym ⊡ G ( ℬ ​ 𝒫 ​ 𝒮 ¯ 𝒜 , Lie G ⊗ H 𝐂 ∗ ∗ ) → 𝒜 ¯ 𝒜 G . \Psi_{\mathcal{A}}^{\psi,G}\colon\Sym_{\boxdot^{G}}\left(\underline{\mathcal{BPS}}_{\mathcal{A},\mathrm{Lie}}^{G}\otimes\HO^{*}_{\mathbf{C}^{*}}\right)\rightarrow\underline{\mathscr{A}}_{\mathcal{A}}^{G}. 
 
 
 
 
 Theorem 11.3   (PBW isomorphism) . 
 
 The map  Ψ 𝒜 ψ , G \Psi_{\mathcal{A}}^{\psi,G}  is an isomorphism of mixed Hodge module complexes. 
 
 
 
 Proof. 
 
 The forgetful functor  π ∗ : 𝒟 + ​ ( MHM ⁡ ( ℳ 𝒜 G ) ) → 𝒟 + ​ ( MHM ⁡ ( ℳ 𝒜 ) ) \pi^{*}\colon\mathcal{D}^{+}(\mathrm{MHM}(\mathcal{M}_{\mathcal{A}}^{G}))\rightarrow\mathcal{D}^{+}(\mathrm{MHM}(\mathcal{M}_{\mathcal{A}}))  is conservative. Therefore, Theorem  11.3  follows from Theorem  1.7 .
∎ 
 
 
 
 
 11.3.  Deformed absolute BPS Lie algebra 

 
 One of the key facts used to deduce results for the absolute CoHA from results for the relative CoHA (for example the GKM description and PBW isomorphism) is the compatibility of the derived global sections functor 
 

 
 
 𝒟 + ​ ( MHM ⁡ ( ℳ 𝒜 ) ) → 𝒟 + ​ ( MHM ⁡ ( K ⁡ ( 𝒜 ) ) ) \mathcal{D}^{+}(\mathrm{MHM}(\mathcal{M}_{\mathcal{A}}))\rightarrow\mathcal{D}^{+}(\mathrm{MHM}(\K(\mathcal{A}))) 
 
 
 with the respective monoidal tensor products. In the equivariant setting, this compatibility no longer holds in general. The derived global section functor 
 

 
 
 𝒟 + ​ ( MHM ⁡ ( ℳ 𝒜 G ) ) → 𝒟 + ​ ( MHM ⁡ ( K ⁡ ( 𝒜 ) / G ) ) \mathcal{D}^{+}(\mathrm{MHM}(\mathcal{M}_{\mathcal{A}}^{G}))\rightarrow\mathcal{D}^{+}(\mathrm{MHM}(\K(\mathcal{A})/G)) 
 
 
 (where  G G  acts trivially on  K ⁡ ( 𝒜 ) \K(\mathcal{A})  and  K ⁡ ( 𝒜 ) / G \K(\mathcal{A})/G  is a quotient stack) is not monoidal for the tensor structures induced by the direct sum (as in § 11.1 ), that is for a given complex variety  X X  and  G G -equivariant mixed Hodge modules  ℱ ¯ , 𝒢 ¯ \underline{\mathscr{F}},\underline{\mathscr{G}}  on  X X , the natural map 
 

 
 (11.3) 
 
 H ∗ ( X / G , ℱ ¯ ) ⊗ H ∗ ⁡ ( B ​ G , 𝐐 ) H ∗ ( X / G , 𝒢 ¯ ) → H ∗ ( X / G × B ​ G X / G , ℱ ¯ ⊠ 𝒢 ¯ ) \HO^{*}(X/G,\underline{\mathscr{F}})\otimes_{\HO^{*}(\mathrm{B}G,\mathbf{Q})}\HO^{*}(X/G,\underline{\mathscr{G}})\rightarrow\HO^{*}(X/G\times_{\mathrm{B}G}X/G,\underline{\mathscr{F}}\boxtimes\underline{\mathscr{G}}) 
 
 
 may not be an isomorphism. Nevertheless, it is an isomorphism under certain hypotheses on  ℱ ¯ , 𝒢 ¯ \underline{\mathscr{F}},\underline{\mathscr{G}}  and  G G . 
 
 
 Lemma 11.4 . 
 
 Let  X X  be an algebraic variety acted on by an algebraic group  G G . We assume that  H ∗ ⁡ ( B ⁡ G , 𝐐 ) \HO^{*}(\B G,\mathbf{Q})  is pure. We let  r : X / G → B ​ G r\colon X/G\rightarrow\mathrm{B}G  be the natural map and  π : X → X / G \pi\colon X\rightarrow X/G  be the quotient map. We let  ℱ ¯ , 𝒢 ¯ \underline{\mathscr{F}},\underline{\mathscr{G}}  be mixed Hodge modules on  X / G X/G  such that  r ∗ ​ ℱ ¯ r_{*}\underline{\mathscr{F}}  and  r ∗ ​ 𝒢 ¯ r_{*}\underline{\mathscr{G}}  are pure mixed Hodge modules on  B ​ G \mathrm{B}G . Then, the Künneth map ( 11.3 ) is an isomorphism. More precisely, we have 
 

 
 
 H ∗ ⁡ ( X / G , ℱ ¯ ) = H ∗ ⁡ ( X , π ∗ ​ ℱ ¯ ) ⊗ H ∗ ⁡ ( B ​ G , 𝐐 ) \HO^{*}(X/G,\underline{\mathscr{F}})=\HO^{*}(X,\pi^{*}\underline{\mathscr{F}})\otimes\HO^{*}(\mathrm{B}G,\mathbf{Q}) 
 
 
 and similarly for  𝒢 ¯ \underline{\mathscr{G}} . 
 
 
 
 Proof. 
 
 The Leray–Serre spectral sequence for the fibration  r r  computing  H ∗ ⁡ ( X / G , ℱ ¯ ) \HO^{*}(X/G,\underline{\mathscr{F}})  degenerates at the second page  E p , q 2 = H p ⁡ ( B ⁡ G , ℋ q ​ ( r ∗ ​ ℱ ¯ ) ) = H p + dim ( G ) ⁡ ( B ⁡ G , 𝐐 ) ⊗ H q − dim ( G ) ⁡ ( X , π ∗ ​ ℱ ¯ ) E^{2}_{p,q}=\HO^{p}(\B G,\mathcal{H}^{q}(r_{*}\underline{\mathscr{F}}))=\HO^{p+\dim(G)}(\B G,\mathbf{Q})\otimes\HO^{q-\dim(G)}(X,\pi^{*}\underline{\mathscr{F}})  for purity reasons.
∎ 
 
 
 
 This proof shows more generally that the Künneth map is an isomorphism when both  H ∗ ⁡ ( X / G , ℱ ) \HO^{*}(X/G,\mathscr{F})  and  H ∗ ⁡ ( X / G , 𝒢 ) \HO^{*}(X/G,\mathscr{G})  are free over  H ∗ ⁡ ( B ​ G , 𝐐 ) \HO^{*}(\mathrm{B}G,\mathbf{Q}) . In the sequel, we assume that the mixed Hodge modules  𝒢 ¯ 𝒜 , m G \underline{\mathscr{G}}_{\mathcal{A},m}^{G} ,  m ∈ Φ K ⁡ ( 𝒜 ) + m\in\Phi^{+}_{\K(\mathcal{A})}  defined in § 11.2  are such that the Künneth map is an isomorphism for any pair  𝒢 ¯ 𝒜 , m G , 𝒢 ¯ 𝒜 , n G \underline{\mathscr{G}}_{\mathcal{A},m}^{G},\underline{\mathscr{G}}_{\mathcal{A},n}^{G} . We say that the mixed Hodge modules  𝒢 ¯ 𝒜 , m G \underline{\mathscr{G}}^{G}_{\mathcal{A},m} ,  m ∈ Φ K ⁡ ( 𝒜 ) + m\in\Phi^{+}_{\K(\mathcal{A})}  satisfy the  Künneth property . Then it follows formally that there is an isomorphism of Lie algebras 
 

 
 (11.4) 
 
 H ∗ ⁡ ( 𝔫 ℳ 𝒜 G , ( − , − ) 𝒞 , 𝒢 ¯ 𝒜 G + ) ≅ 𝔫 K ⁡ ( 𝒜 ) , ( − , − ) 𝒞 , 𝒢 ¯ 𝒜 + ⊗ H ∗ ⁡ ( B ⁡ G , 𝐐 ) . \HO^{*}(\mathfrak{n}^{+}_{\mathcal{M}_{\mathcal{A}}^{G},(-,-)_{\mathscr{C}},\underline{\mathscr{G}}_{\mathcal{A}}^{G}})\cong\mathfrak{n}^{+}_{\K(\mathcal{A}),(-,-)_{\mathscr{C}},\underline{\mathscr{G}}_{\mathcal{A}}}\otimes\HO^{*}(\B G,\mathbf{Q}). 
 
 
 
 
 We immediately deduce the following. 
 
 
 Theorem 11.5 . 
 
 Assume that the mixed Hodge modules  𝒢 ¯ 𝒜 , m G \underline{\mathscr{G}}^{G}_{\mathcal{A},m} ,  m ∈ Φ K ⁡ ( 𝒜 ) + m\in\Phi^{+}_{\K(\mathcal{A})}  satisfy the Künneth property. The deformed BPS algebra  BPS 𝒜 , Alg G \mathrm{BPS}_{\mathcal{A},\mathrm{Alg}}^{G}  is isomorphic to the enveloping algebra of the generalised Kac–Moody algebra defined over  H ∗ ⁡ ( B ​ G , 𝐐 ) \HO^{*}(\mathrm{B}G,\mathbf{Q}) , associated to the monoid with bilinear form  ( K ⁡ ( 𝒜 ) , ( − , − ) 𝒞 ) (\K(\mathcal{A}),(-,-)_{\mathscr{C}})  and the weight function described in Theorem  1.2  (with  ι △ \iota_{\triangle}  the identity morphism here – nilpotent versions are considered in Proposition  11.12 ). In particular, there is an isomorphism of algebras 
 

 
 
 BPS 𝒜 , Alg G ≅ BPS 𝒜 , Alg ⊗ H ∗ ⁡ ( B ⁡ G , 𝐐 ) . \mathrm{BPS}_{\mathcal{A},\mathrm{Alg}}^{G}\cong\mathrm{BPS}_{\mathcal{A},\mathrm{Alg}}\otimes\HO^{*}(\B G,\mathbf{Q}). 
 
 
 
 
 
 Proof. 
 
 The fact that the mixed Hodge modules  𝒢 ¯ 𝒜 , m G \underline{\mathscr{G}}^{G}_{\mathcal{A},m}  satisfy the Künneth property implies that the  H ∗ ⁡ ( B ⁡ G , 𝐐 ) \HO^{*}(\B G,\mathbf{Q}) -algebra obtained by taking derived global sections of  ℬ ​ 𝒫 ​ 𝒮 𝒜 , Alg G \mathcal{BPS}_{\mathcal{A},\mathrm{Alg}}^{G}  is exactly the  H ∗ ⁡ ( B ⁡ G , 𝐐 ) \HO^{*}(\B G,\mathbf{Q}) -linear enveloping algebra of the GKM as described in the statement of the theorem. Theorem  11.5  is then a consequence of Theorem  11.2 .
∎ 
 
 
 
 Given a choice of positive determinant bundle on  𝔐 𝒜 G \mathfrak{M}_{\mathcal{A}}^{G}  we define a map 
 

 
 (11.5) 
 
 Sym H ∗ ⁡ ( B ⁡ G , 𝐐 ) ⁡ ( BPS ¯ 𝒜 , Lie G ⊗ H 𝐂 ∗ ∗ ) → H ∗ ⁡ ( 𝒜 ¯ 𝒜 ψ , G ) \Sym_{\HO^{*}(\B G,\mathbf{Q})}\left(\underline{\mathrm{BPS}}_{\mathcal{A},\mathrm{Lie}}^{G}\otimes\HO^{*}_{\mathbf{C}^{*}}\right)\rightarrow\HO^{*}(\underline{\mathscr{A}}_{\mathcal{A}}^{\psi,G}) 
 
 
 as in § 8.3.2  by using the algebra structure on  H ∗ ⁡ ( 𝒜 ¯ 𝒜 ψ , G ) \HO^{*}(\underline{\mathscr{A}}_{\mathcal{A}}^{\psi,G}) , the  H 𝐂 ∗ ∗ \HO^{*}_{\mathbf{C}^{*}} -action on the CoHA and the iterated CoHA multiplication. 
 
 
 Theorem 11.6   (Deformed PBW theorem) . 
 
 The map ( 11.5 ) is an isomorphism of mixed Hodge module complexes. 
 
 
 
 Proof. 
 
 The Künneth property implies that the map ( 11.5 ) coincides with the map  H ∗ ⁡ ( Ψ 𝒜 ψ , G ) \HO^{*}(\Psi_{\mathcal{A}}^{\psi,G}) . Therefore, Theorem  11.6  follows from Theorem  11.3 .
∎ 
 
 
 
 
 11.4.  Examples 

 
 When  𝒜 \mathcal{A}  is the category of representations of the preprojective algebra  Π Q \Pi_{Q}  of a quiver, there is a natural choice for the group  G G . Namely, we let 
 

 
 
 G ar ≔ ∏ i ∈ Q 0 Sp 2 ​ g i × ∏ i ≠ j ∈ Q 0 GL q i , j , G_{\mathrm{ar}}\coloneqq\prod_{i\in Q_{0}}\mathrm{Sp}_{2g_{i}}\times\prod_{i\neq j\in Q_{0}}\mathrm{GL}_{q_{i,j}}, 
 
 
 be the group of arrow symmetries of  Q ¯ \overline{Q}  preserving the moment map, where  g i g_{i}  is the number of loops at the vertex  i i  in  Q 1 Q_{1}  and  q i , j q_{i,j}  the number of arrows between  i i  and  j j  in  Q ¯ \overline{Q}   [ MO19 , §2.1.3] . The group  G ar G_{\mathrm{ar}}  acts naturally on the representation spaces  𝐀 Q ¯ , 𝐝 \mathbf{A}_{\overline{Q},\mathbf{d}}  defined in § 2 . Indeed, we can write 
 

 
 
 𝐀 Q ¯ , 𝐝 = ∏ i ∈ Q 0 End ⁡ ( 𝐂 d i ) ⊗ 𝐂 2 ​ g i × ∏ i ≠ j ∈ Q 0 T ∗ ​ ( Hom ⁡ ( 𝐂 d i , 𝐂 d j ) ⊗ 𝐂 q i , j ) , \mathbf{A}_{\overline{Q},\mathbf{d}}=\prod_{i\in Q_{0}}\operatorname{End}(\mathbf{C}^{d_{i}})\otimes\mathbf{C}^{2g_{i}}\times\prod_{i\neq j\in Q_{0}}\mathrm{T}^{*}(\operatorname{Hom}(\mathbf{C}^{d_{i}},\mathbf{C}^{d_{j}})\otimes\mathbf{C}^{q_{i,j}}), 
 
 
 where  q i , j q_{i,j}  is the number of arrows  i → j i\rightarrow j  in  Q Q ,  Sp 2 ​ g i \mathrm{Sp}_{2g_{i}}  acts on  𝐂 2 ​ g i \mathbf{C}^{2g_{i}}  and  GL q i , j \mathrm{GL}_{q_{i,j}}  acts on  𝐂 q i , j \mathbf{C}^{q_{i,j}} . 
 
 
 We can take for  G G  any (connected) subgroup of  G ar × 𝐂 ∗ G_{\mathrm{ar}}\times\mathbf{C}^{*} . The additional  𝐂 ∗ \mathbf{C}^{*}  action rescales cotangent fibers (i.e. if  ( x , x ∗ ) ∈ 𝐀 Q ¯ , 𝐝 (x,x^{*})\in\mathbf{A}_{\overline{Q},\mathbf{d}}  and  u ∈ 𝐂 ∗ u\in\mathbf{C}^{*} ,  u ⋅ ( x , x ∗ ) = ( x , u ​ x ∗ ) u\cdot(x,x^{*})=(x,ux^{*}) . For example,  G G  can be a maximal torus of  G ar G_{\mathrm{ar}} . 
 
 
 As shown in the following lemma, equivariant formality holds for the mixed Hodge modules involved in the description of the BPS algebra of  Π Q \Pi_{Q} . 
 
 
 Lemma 11.7 . 
 
 Let  Q Q  be a quiver,  Π Q \Pi_{Q}  its preprojective algebra and  𝐝 ∈ 𝐍 Q 0 \mathbf{d}\in\mathbf{N}^{Q_{0}}  a dimension vector. Then,  IH n ​ ( ℳ Π Q , 𝐝 ) \mathrm{IH}^{n}(\mathcal{M}_{\Pi_{Q},\mathbf{d}})  vanishes for  n n  odd. Therefore, for any connected linear algebraic group  G G  acting on  ℳ Π Q , 𝐝 \mathcal{M}_{\Pi_{Q},\mathbf{d}} , the intersection cohomology complex is naturally equivariant and  IH^* ⁡ ( ℳ Π Q , 𝐝 / G ) = IH G ∗ ​ ( ℳ Π Q , 𝐝 ) ≅ IH^* ⁡ ( ℳ Π Q , 𝐝 ) ⊗ 𝐐 H ∗ ⁡ ( B ​ G , 𝐐 ) \ICA(\mathcal{M}_{\Pi_{Q},\mathbf{d}}/G)=\mathrm{IH}^{*}_{G}(\mathcal{M}_{\Pi_{Q},\mathbf{d}})\cong\ICA(\mathcal{M}_{\Pi_{Q},\mathbf{d}})\otimes_{\mathbf{Q}}\HO^{*}(\mathrm{B}G,\mathbf{Q}) . 
 
 
 
 Proof. 
 
 The last statement is known as “equivariant formality” and follows from the purity of the intersection cohomology. Purity, as well as vanishing of odd intersection cohomology, follow from Theorem  1.1  by taking derived global sections, and the analogous purity and vanishing statements for  H BM ⁡ ( 𝔐 Π Q , 𝐐 vir ) \HO^{\BoMo}(\mathfrak{M}_{\Pi_{Q}},\mathbf{Q}^{\mathrm{vir}}) : this is  [ Dav23a , Theorem A] .
∎ 
 
 
 
 
 11.5.  3d deformed BPS Lie algebra 

 
 One can define the deformed 3d BPS Lie algebra using the tripled quiver with potential and dimensional reduction. It is defined as in § 8.1  after upgrading objects to mixed Hodge modules as in  [ DM20 ] . The group  G ar × 𝐂 ∗ G_{\mathrm{ar}}\times\mathbf{C}^{*}  acts on the representation space  𝐀 Q ~ , 𝐝 ≅ 𝐀 Q ¯ , 𝐝 × ∏ i ∈ Q 0 End ⁡ ( 𝐂 d i ) \mathbf{A}_{\widetilde{Q},\mathbf{d}}\cong\mathbf{A}_{\overline{Q},\mathbf{d}}\times\prod_{i\in Q_{0}}\operatorname{End}(\mathbf{C}^{d_{i}})  of the tripled quiver as follows. The action on the first factor is as defined in § 11.4 . The subgroup  G ar G_{\mathrm{ar}}  acts trivially on the second factor and the additional copy of  𝐂 ∗ \mathbf{C}^{*}  acts by rescaling the factor  ∏ i ∈ Q 0 End ⁡ ( 𝐂 d i ) \prod_{i\in Q_{0}}\operatorname{End}(\mathbf{C}^{d_{i}})  by  u − 1 u^{-1} . For this action, the canonical potential  W ~ \widetilde{W}  gives a  G ar × 𝐂 ∗ G_{\mathrm{ar}}\times\mathbf{C}^{*} -invariant function on  𝐀 Q ~ , 𝐝 \mathbf{A}_{\widetilde{Q},\mathbf{d}} . The vanishing cycle cohomology gives the cohomological Hall algebra  𝒜 ¯ Q ~ , W ~ ψ , G \underline{\mathscr{A}}_{\widetilde{Q},\widetilde{W}}^{\psi,G} , and the perverse filtration together with dimensional reduction provides us with the 3d BPS Lie algebra  ℬ ​ 𝒫 ​ 𝒮 ¯ Π Q , Lie 𝟹 ​ 𝚍 , ψ , G \underline{\mathcal{BPS}}_{\Pi_{Q},\mathrm{Lie}}^{\mathtt{3d},\psi,G} . It is an object of the shifted category of mixed Hodge modules  MHM ⁡ ( ℳ Π Q G ) ​ [ dim G ] \mathrm{MHM}(\mathcal{M}_{\Pi_{Q}}^{G})[\dim G] . All results presented in § 8  hold for the  G G -equivariant deformations of the relative and absolute versions of the BPS algebra and Lie algebra: 
 
 
 Proposition 11.8 . 
 
 We have a canonical isomorphism of Lie algebra objects in mixed Hodge modules 
 

 
 
 ℬ ​ 𝒫 ​ 𝒮 ¯ Π Q , Lie 𝟹 ​ 𝚍 , ψ , G ≅ ℬ ​ 𝒫 ​ 𝒮 ¯ Π Q , Lie G . \underline{\mathcal{BPS}}_{\Pi_{Q},\mathrm{Lie}}^{\mathtt{3d},\psi,G}\cong\underline{\mathcal{BPS}}_{\Pi_{Q},\mathrm{Lie}}^{G}. 
 
 
 
 
 
 Proof. 
 
 Again, by conservativity of the forgetful functor, it suffices to check this statement when  G G  is the trivial group and in this case, this is Theorem  1.16 .
∎ 
 
 
 
 As in the non-equivariant setting, the 3d BPS Lie algebra  𝔤 Q ~ , W ~ ψ , G \mathfrak{g}_{\widetilde{Q},\widetilde{W}}^{\psi,G}  is obtained by passing to derived global sections of  ℬ ​ 𝒫 ​ 𝒮 Π Q , Lie 𝟹 ​ 𝚍 , G \mathcal{BPS}_{\Pi_{Q},\mathrm{Lie}}^{\mathtt{3d},G} . The following is our main result on deformations of BPS Lie algebras for tripled quivers with potential: it says that all deformations obtained by switching on equivariant parameters associated to arrows of the quiver are  trivial . 
 
 
 Corollary 11.9 . 
 
 There is an isomorphism of Lie algebras 
 

 
 
 𝔤 Q ~ , W ~ ψ , G ≅ 𝔤 Q ~ , W ~ ψ ⊗ H ∗ ⁡ ( B ⁡ G , 𝐐 ) . \mathfrak{g}_{\widetilde{Q},\widetilde{W}}^{\psi,G}\cong\mathfrak{g}_{\widetilde{Q},\widetilde{W}}^{\psi}\otimes\HO^{*}(\B G,\mathbf{Q}). 
 
 
 
 
 
 Proof. 
 
 We have  𝔤 Q ~ , W ~ ψ ≅ 𝔫 Π Q ψ \mathfrak{g}_{\widetilde{Q},\widetilde{W}}^{\psi}\cong\mathfrak{n}_{\Pi_{Q}}^{\psi}  by Theorem  1.16  and  𝔤 Q ~ , W ψ , G ≅ 𝔫 Π Q ψ , G \mathfrak{g}_{\widetilde{Q},W}^{\psi,G}\cong\mathfrak{n}_{\Pi_{Q}}^{\psi,G}  by Proposition  11.8 . The isomorphism then follows from ( 11.4 ). 
 
 
 ∎ 
 
 
 
 
 11.6.  Equivariant cohomology of Nakajima quiver varieties 

 
 Our description of the whole cohomology of Nakajima quiver varieties (§ 10 ) adapts to the equivariant setting. As in  [ MO19 , §2.1.2] , the group  G ar × 𝐂 ∗ G_{\mathrm{ar}}\times\mathbf{C}^{*}  acts on quiver varieties and since they have pure cohomology, equivariant formality holds for them for any action of a connected linear algebraic group  G ⊆ G ar × 𝐂 ∗ G\subseteq G_{\mathrm{ar}}\times\mathbf{C}^{*}  (as in Lemma  11.7 ). The description of the  G G -equivariant cohomology of quiver varieties as a direct sum of lowest modules (Theorem  10.5 ) adapts to the  G G -equivariant cohomology in a straightforward way: this eventually amounts to extending the scalars from  𝐐 \mathbf{Q}  to  H ∗ ⁡ ( B ​ G , 𝐐 ) \HO^{*}(\mathrm{B}G,\mathbf{Q}) . 
 
 
 
 11.7.  Nilpotent versions 

 
 If  ℳ 𝒜 △ ⊆ ℳ 𝒜 \mathcal{M}_{\mathcal{A}}^{\triangle}\subseteq\mathcal{M}_{\mathcal{A}}  is a saturated submonoid stable under the  G G -action on  ℳ 𝒜 \mathcal{M}_{\mathcal{A}} , then one can define the corresponding relative and absolute CoHAs  𝒜 ¯ 𝒜 △ , ψ , G \underline{\mathscr{A}}_{\mathcal{A}}^{\triangle,\psi,G}  and  H ∗ ⁡ ( 𝒜 ¯ 𝒜 △ , ψ , G ) \HO^{*}(\underline{\mathscr{A}}_{\mathcal{A}}^{\triangle,\psi,G}) , and the  △ \triangle  BPS algebra and  △ \triangle  Lie algebra  BPS ¯ 𝒜 , Alg △ , ψ , G \underline{\mathrm{BPS}}_{\mathcal{A},\mathrm{Alg}}^{\triangle,\psi,G}  and  BPS ¯ 𝒜 , Lie △ , G \underline{\mathrm{BPS}}_{\mathcal{A},\mathrm{Lie}}^{\triangle,G}  via  ι △ ! \iota_{\triangle}^{!} , as before. In particular, this applies to the case of the preprojective algebras of quivers and  △ ∈ { 𝒩 , 𝒮 ​ 𝒩 , 𝒮 ​ 𝒮 ​ 𝒩 } \triangle\in\{\mathcal{N},\mathcal{SN},\mathcal{SSN}\} . The results of § 10  also translate to the equivariant BPS algebras with various nilpotency conditions. Crucial in the step from relative to absolute CoHAs is again the equivariant formality of the sheaf cohomologies involved. For the nilpotent versions, this equivariant formality follows again from the odd cohomology vanishing. 
 
 
 Proposition 11.10 . 
 
 The stacks  𝔐 Π Q △ \mathfrak{M}_{\Pi_{Q}}^{\triangle}  for  △ ∈ { 𝒩 , 𝒮 ​ 𝒩 , 𝒮 ​ 𝒮 ​ 𝒩 } \triangle\in\{\mathcal{N},\mathcal{SN},\mathcal{SSN}\}  are pure and have no odd cohomology.  △ \triangle  Nakajima quiver varieties are pure and have no odd cohomology for  △ ∈ { 𝒩 , 𝒮 ​ 𝒩 , 𝒮 ​ 𝒮 ​ 𝒩 } \triangle\in\{\mathcal{N},\mathcal{SN},\mathcal{SSN}\} . 
 
 
 
 Proof. 
 
 The first statement is  [ SV20 , Theorem A] , for  △ ∈ { 𝒮 ​ 𝒩 , 𝒮 ​ 𝒮 ​ 𝒩 } \triangle\in\{\mathcal{SN},\mathcal{SSN}\} . For  △ = 𝒩 \triangle=\mathcal{N}  it holds for the BPS Lie algebra since it is Verdier dual to the BPS Lie algebra  𝔤 Π Q \mathfrak{g}_{\Pi_{Q}} , and then cohomological integrality implies it for the cohomology of the whole stack. 
 
 
 The second statement comes from the fact that all nilpotent versions of Nakajima quiver varieties are very  ℓ \ell -pure and polynomial count (when working over a finite field  𝐅 q \mathbf{F}_{q}  and  ℓ \ell -adic cohomology, for  ℓ \ell  and  q q  coprime),  [ Hau10 , Theorem 1] . Alternatively, they follow from the first statement and cohomological wall crossing, i.e.  [ Dav23a , Theorem C] .
∎ 
 
 
 
 Corollary 11.11 . 
 
 For  △ ∈ { 𝒩 , 𝒮 ​ 𝒩 , 𝒮 ​ 𝒮 ​ 𝒩 } \triangle\in\{\mathcal{N},\mathcal{SN},\mathcal{SSN}\} , the complexes of mixed Hodge modules  ι △ ! ℐ ​ 𝒞 ¯ ( ℳ Π Q , 𝐝 ) \iota_{\triangle}^{!}\underline{\mathcal{IC}}(\mathcal{M}_{\Pi_{Q},\mathbf{d}})  have pure cohomology, and vanishing odd cohomology. 
 
 
 
 In summary, we can generalise the results of this section for our chosen three Serre subcategories: 
 
 
 Proposition 11.12 . 
 
 For  △ ∈ { 𝒩 , 𝒮 ​ 𝒩 , 𝒮 ​ 𝒮 ​ 𝒩 } \triangle\in\{\mathcal{N},\mathcal{SN},\mathcal{SSN}\}  there are isomorphisms of Lie algebras 
 

 
 
 𝔤 Q ~ , W ~ △ , ψ , G ≅ 𝔤 Q ~ , W ~ △ , ψ ⊗ H ∗ ⁡ ( B ⁡ G , 𝐐 ) ≅ 𝔫 Π Q △ , + ⊗ H ∗ ⁡ ( B ⁡ G , 𝐐 ) , \mathfrak{g}_{\widetilde{Q},\widetilde{W}}^{\triangle,\psi,G}\cong\mathfrak{g}_{\widetilde{Q},\widetilde{W}}^{\triangle,\psi}\otimes\HO^{*}(\B G,\mathbf{Q})\cong\mathfrak{n}^{\triangle,+}_{\Pi_{Q}}\otimes\HO^{*}(\B G,\mathbf{Q}), 
 
 
 an isomorphism of algebras 
 

 
 
 BPS ¯ 𝒜 , Alg △ , ψ , G ≅ BPS ¯ 𝒜 , Alg △ , ψ ⊗ H ∗ ⁡ ( B ⁡ G , 𝐐 ) . \underline{\mathrm{BPS}}_{\mathcal{A},\mathrm{Alg}}^{\triangle,\psi,G}\cong\underline{\mathrm{BPS}}_{\mathcal{A},\mathrm{Alg}}^{\triangle,\psi}\otimes\HO^{*}(\B G,\mathbf{Q}). 
 
 
 as well as a PBW isomorphism (not respecting the algebra structures on the domain and the target) 
 

 
 
 Sym H ∗ ⁡ ( B ⁡ G , 𝐐 ) ⁡ ( BPS ¯ 𝒜 , Lie △ , G ⊗ H 𝐂 ∗ ∗ ) → H ∗ ⁡ ( 𝒜 ¯ 𝒜 △ , ψ , G ) . \Sym_{\HO^{*}(\B G,\mathbf{Q})}\left(\underline{\mathrm{BPS}}_{\mathcal{A},\mathrm{Lie}}^{\triangle,G}\otimes\HO^{*}_{\mathbf{C}^{*}}\right)\rightarrow\HO^{*}(\underline{\mathscr{A}}_{\mathcal{A}}^{\triangle,\psi,G}). 
 
 
 
 
 
 Here, the algebra  𝔤 Q ~ , W ~ △ , ψ , G \mathfrak{g}_{\widetilde{Q},\widetilde{W}}^{\triangle,\psi,G}  is defined as the derived global sections of  ( ı △ G ) ! ℬ 𝒫 𝒮 Q ~ , W ~ ψ , G (\imath_{\triangle}^{G})^{!}\mathcal{BPS}_{\widetilde{Q},\widetilde{W}}^{\psi,G}  where  ı △ G : ℳ Q ~ △ / G → ℳ Q ~ / G \imath^{G}_{\triangle}\colon\mathcal{M}_{\widetilde{Q}}^{\triangle}/G\rightarrow\mathcal{M}_{\widetilde{Q}}/G  is the inclusion of the saturated submonoid of  ℳ Q ~ \mathcal{M}_{\widetilde{Q}}  of representations of  Q ~ \widetilde{Q}  such that the underlying representation of  Q ¯ \overline{Q}  has the property  △ \triangle . 
 
 
 
 
 Appendix A  Table of notations and conventions 

 
 A.1.  Generalised Kac–Moody algebras and their representations 

 
 
 (1)  
 
 𝔤 Γ \mathfrak{g}_{\Gamma}  is the Kac–Moody Lie algebra associated to a graph  Γ \Gamma  (without loops)  [ Kac90 ] . 
 
 (2)  
 
 𝔤 Q = 𝔫 Q + ⊕ 𝔥 ⊕ 𝔫 Q + \mathfrak{g}_{Q}=\mathfrak{n}_{Q}^{+}\oplus\mathfrak{h}\oplus\mathfrak{n}_{Q}^{+} ,  𝔥 = 𝐂 Q 0 \mathfrak{h}=\mathbf{C}^{Q_{0}} , the Borcherds–Bozec Lie algebra of a quiver  Q Q , with its triangular decomposition (page  7.6.1 ). 
 
 
 
 
 
 A.2.  CoHAs and BPS (Lie) algebras 

 
 
 (1)  
 
 𝒜 ¯ 𝒜 ∈ 𝒟 + ​ ( MHM ⁡ ( ℳ 𝒜 ) ) \underline{\mathscr{A}}_{\mathcal{A}}\in\mathcal{D}^{+}(\mathrm{MHM}(\mathcal{M}_{\mathcal{A}}))  the relative cohomological Hall algebra of  𝒜 \mathcal{A} . This is by definition the complex of mixed Hodge modules  𝙹𝙷 ∗ ⁡ 𝔻 ​ 𝐐 𝔐 𝒜 vir \JH_{*}\mathbb{D}\mathbf{Q}_{\mathfrak{M}_{\mathcal{A}}}^{\mathrm{vir}}  together with the CoHA multiplication;  𝒜 𝒜 = 𝐫𝐚𝐭 ⁡ ( 𝒜 ¯ 𝒜 ) ∈ 𝒟 + ​ ( Perv ⁡ ( ℳ 𝒜 ) ) ≅ 𝒟 c + ​ ( ℳ 𝒜 ) \mathscr{A}_{\mathcal{A}}=\rat(\underline{\mathscr{A}}_{\mathcal{A}})\in\mathcal{D}^{+}(\operatorname{Perv}(\mathcal{M}_{\mathcal{A}}))\cong\mathcal{D}^{+}_{\mathrm{c}}(\mathcal{M}_{\mathcal{A}})  (page  6.1 ).  𝒜 ¯ 𝒜 ψ \underline{\mathscr{A}}_{\mathcal{A}}^{\psi} ,  𝒜 𝒜 ψ \mathscr{A}_{\mathcal{A}}^{\psi}  the relative CoHAs with the  ψ \psi -twisted multiplication (page  6.3.1 ). 
 
 (2)  
 
 𝒜 ¯ Q , W ∈ MMHM ⁡ ( ℳ Q ) \underline{\mathscr{A}}_{Q,W}\in\mathrm{MMHM}(\mathcal{M}_{Q})  the relative CoHA of the quiver with potential  ( Q , W ) (Q,W) , defined as the complex of monodromic mixed Hodge modules  𝙹𝙷 ∗ ⁡ ϕ Tr ⁡ ( W ) 𝔭 ​ 𝐐 𝔐 Q vir \JH_{*}{}^{\mathfrak{p}}\!\phi_{\Tr(W)}\mathbf{Q}_{\mathfrak{M}_{Q}}^{\mathrm{vir}} ;  𝒜 Q , W ∈ Perv ⁡ ( ℳ Q ) \mathscr{A}_{Q,W}\in\operatorname{Perv}(\mathcal{M}_{Q})  the relative CoHA of the quiver with potential  ( Q , W ) (Q,W) , defined as the complex of constructible sheaves  𝙹𝙷 ∗ ⁡ ϕ Tr ⁡ ( W ) 𝔭 ​ 𝐐 𝔐 Q vir \JH_{*}{}^{\mathfrak{p}}\!\phi_{\Tr(W)}\mathbf{Q}_{\mathfrak{M}_{Q}}^{\mathrm{vir}} . Alternatively,  𝒜 Q , W = 𝐫𝐚𝐭 ⁡ ( 𝒜 ¯ Q , W ) \mathscr{A}_{Q,W}=\rat(\underline{\mathscr{A}}_{Q,W}) .  𝒜 ¯ Q , W ψ \underline{\mathscr{A}}_{Q,W}^{\psi} ,  𝒜 Q , W ψ \mathscr{A}_{Q,W}^{\psi}  the  ψ \psi -twisted versions (page  8.1 ). 
 
 (3)  
 
 ℬ ​ 𝒫 ​ 𝒮 ¯ 𝒜 , Alg ∈ MHM ⁡ ( ℳ 𝒜 ) \underline{\mathcal{BPS}}_{\mathcal{A},\mathrm{Alg}}\in\mathrm{MHM}(\mathcal{M}_{\mathcal{A}})  the relative BPS algebra of the category  𝒜 \mathcal{A} , defined as the degree zero cohomology of  𝒜 ¯ 𝒜 \underline{\mathscr{A}}_{\mathcal{A}} ,  ℬ ​ 𝒫 ​ 𝒮 𝒜 , Alg ∈ Perv ⁡ ( ℳ 𝒜 ) \mathcal{BPS}_{\mathcal{A},\mathrm{Alg}}\in\operatorname{Perv}(\mathcal{M}_{\mathcal{A}})  the relative BPS algebra, defined as the degree zero perverse cohomology of  𝒜 𝒜 \mathscr{A}_{\mathcal{A}}  or equivalently as  𝐫𝐚𝐭 ⁡ ( ℬ ​ 𝒫 ​ 𝒮 ¯ 𝒜 , Alg ) \rat(\underline{\mathcal{BPS}}_{\mathcal{A},\mathrm{Alg}})  (page  6.2 ). 
 
 (4)  
 
 ℬ ​ 𝒫 ​ 𝒮 ¯ 𝒜 , Alg ψ \underline{\mathcal{BPS}}_{\mathcal{A},\mathrm{Alg}}^{\psi} ,  ℬ ​ 𝒫 ​ 𝒮 𝒜 , Alg ψ \mathcal{BPS}_{\mathcal{A},\mathrm{Alg}}^{\psi} , the relative BPS algebras with the  ψ \psi -twisted multiplications (page  6.3.1 ). 
 
 (5)  
 
 BPS ¯ 𝒜 = H ∗ ⁡ ( ℬ ​ 𝒫 ​ 𝒮 ¯ 𝒜 , Alg ) ∈ MHM ⁡ ( K ⁡ ( 𝒜 ) ) \underline{\mathrm{BPS}}_{\mathcal{A}}=\HO^{*}(\underline{\mathcal{BPS}}_{\mathcal{A},\mathrm{Alg}})\in\mathrm{MHM}(\K(\mathcal{A}))  the absolute BPS algebra, a  K ⁡ ( 𝒜 ) \K(\mathcal{A}) -graded complex of mixed Hodge structures.  BPS 𝒜 , Alg = H ∗ ⁡ ( ℬ ​ 𝒫 ​ 𝒮 𝒜 , Alg ) \mathrm{BPS}_{\mathcal{A},\mathrm{Alg}}=\HO^{*}(\mathcal{BPS}_{\mathcal{A},\mathrm{Alg}}) , the absolute BPS algebra, a  K ⁡ ( 𝒜 ) \K(\mathcal{A}) -graded complex of vector spaces (i.e. a  K ⁡ ( 𝒜 ) × 𝐙 \K(\mathcal{A})\times\mathbf{Z} -graded vector space) (page  6.2 ). 
 
 (6)  
 
 BPS ¯ 𝒜 , Alg ψ \underline{\mathrm{BPS}}_{\mathcal{A},\mathrm{Alg}}^{\psi} ,  BPS 𝒜 , Alg ψ \mathrm{BPS}_{\mathcal{A},\mathrm{Alg}}^{\psi}  the algebras with the  ψ \psi -twisted multiplications (page  6.3.1 ). 
 
 (7)  
 
 𝔫 ℳ 𝒜 , ( − , − ) 𝒞 , 𝒢 ¯ 𝒜 + = ℬ ​ 𝒫 ​ 𝒮 ¯ 𝒜 , Lie ∈ MHM ⁡ ( ℳ 𝒜 ) \mathfrak{n}_{\mathcal{M}_{\mathcal{A}},(-,-)_{\mathscr{C}},\underline{\mathscr{G}}_{\mathcal{A}}}^{+}=\underline{\mathcal{BPS}}_{\mathcal{A},\mathrm{Lie}}\in\mathrm{MHM}(\mathcal{M}_{\mathcal{A}})  the relative BPS Lie algebra, defined as a positive part of a generalised Kac–Moody Lie algebra in the category of mixed Hodge modules, by generators and relations.  𝔫 ℳ 𝒜 , ( − , − ) 𝒞 , 𝒢 𝒜 + = ℬ ​ 𝒫 ​ 𝒮 𝒜 , Lie ∈ Perv ⁡ ( ℳ 𝒜 ) \mathfrak{n}_{\mathcal{M}_{\mathcal{A}},(-,-)_{\mathscr{C}},\mathscr{G}_{\mathcal{A}}}^{+}=\mathcal{BPS}_{\mathcal{A},\mathrm{Lie}}\in\operatorname{Perv}(\mathcal{M}_{\mathcal{A}})  the relative BPS Lie algebra, defined as a positive part of a generalised Kac–Moody Lie algebra in the perverse sheaves, by generators and relations. Equivalently,  ℬ ​ 𝒫 ​ 𝒮 𝒜 , Lie = 𝐫𝐚𝐭 ⁡ ( ℬ ​ 𝒫 ​ 𝒮 ¯ 𝒜 , Alg ) \mathcal{BPS}_{\mathcal{A},\mathrm{Lie}}=\rat(\underline{\mathcal{BPS}}_{\mathcal{A},\mathrm{Alg}})  (page  7.15 ). 
 
 (8)  
 
 BPS ¯ 𝒜 , Lie = H ∗ ⁡ ( ℬ ​ 𝒫 ​ 𝒮 ¯ 𝒜 , Lie ) \underline{\mathrm{BPS}}_{\mathcal{A},\mathrm{Lie}}=\HO^{*}(\underline{\mathcal{BPS}}_{\mathcal{A},\mathrm{Lie}})  the absolute BPS Lie algebra, a complex of mixed Hodge modules on  K ⁡ ( 𝒜 ) \K(\mathcal{A}) . By strict monoidality of  H ∗ \HO^{*} , it is isomorphic to  𝔫 K ⁡ ( 𝒜 ) , ( − , − ) 𝒞 , H ∗ ⁡ ( 𝒢 ¯ 𝒜 ) + \mathfrak{n}_{\K(\mathcal{A}),(-,-)_{\mathscr{C}},\HO^{*}(\underline{\mathscr{G}}_{\mathcal{A}})}^{+} . We also denote it  𝔫 ¯ 𝒜 BPS , + \underline{\mathfrak{n}}_{\mathcal{A}}^{\mathtt{\aBPS},+} .  BPS 𝒜 , Lie = H ∗ ⁡ ( ℬ ​ 𝒫 ​ 𝒮 𝒜 , Lie ) \mathrm{BPS}_{\mathcal{A},\mathrm{Lie}}=\HO^{*}(\mathcal{BPS}_{\mathcal{A},\mathrm{Lie}})  the absolute BPS Lie algebra, a complex of vector spaces  K ⁡ ( 𝒜 ) \K(\mathcal{A}) . By strict monoidality of  H ∗ \HO^{*} , it is isomorphic to  𝔫 K ⁡ ( 𝒜 ) , ( − , − ) 𝒞 , H ∗ ⁡ ( 𝒢 𝒜 ) + \mathfrak{n}_{\K(\mathcal{A}),(-,-)_{\mathscr{C}},\HO^{*}(\mathscr{G}_{\mathcal{A}})}^{+} . Alternatively, it is obtained from  BPS ¯ 𝒜 , Lie \underline{\mathrm{BPS}}_{\mathcal{A},\mathrm{Lie}}  by forgetting the mixed Hodge structure. We also denote it  𝔫 𝒜 BPS , + \mathfrak{n}_{\mathcal{A}}^{\mathtt{\aBPS},+}  (page  7.15 ). 
 
 (9)  
 
 𝔤 𝒜 BPS = 𝔫 𝒜 BPS , − ⊕ 𝔥 𝒜 ⊕ 𝔫 𝒜 BPS , + \mathfrak{g}_{\mathcal{A}}^{\mathtt{\aBPS}}=\mathfrak{n}_{\mathcal{A}}^{\mathtt{\aBPS},-}\oplus\mathfrak{h}_{\mathcal{A}}\oplus\mathfrak{n}_{\mathcal{A}}^{\mathtt{\aBPS},+} ,  𝔥 𝒜 = K ⁡ ( 𝒜 ) ^ ⊗ 𝐙 𝐂 \mathfrak{h}_{\mathcal{A}}=\widehat{\K(\mathcal{A})}\otimes_{\mathbf{Z}}\mathbf{C} , the full BPS Lie algebra of  𝒜 \mathcal{A}  (page  7.15 ). 
 
 (10)  
 
 ℬ ​ 𝒫 ​ 𝒮 ¯ Q , W ∈ MMHM ⁡ ( ℳ Q ) \underline{\mathcal{BPS}}_{Q,W}\in\mathrm{MMHM}(\mathcal{M}_{Q})  the BPS sheaf of the quiver with potential  ( Q , W ) (Q,W) , defined as  τ ⩽ 0 ​ ( 𝒜 ¯ Q , W ​ [ 1 ] ) \tau^{\leqslant 0}(\underline{\mathscr{A}}_{Q,W}[1]) .  ℬ ​ 𝒫 ​ 𝒮 Q , W ∈ Perv ⁡ ( ℳ Q ) \mathcal{BPS}_{Q,W}\in\operatorname{Perv}(\mathcal{M}_{Q})  the BPS sheaf of the quiver with potential  ( Q , W ) (Q,W) , defined as  τ ⩽ 0 𝔭 ​ ( 𝒜 Q , W ​ [ 1 ] ) {{}^{\mathfrak{p}}\tau}^{\leqslant 0}(\mathscr{A}_{Q,W}[1])  (page  8.1 ). 
 
 (11)  
 
 𝔤 ¯ Q , W = H ∗ ⁡ ( ℬ ​ 𝒫 ​ 𝒮 ¯ Q , W ) \underline{\mathfrak{g}}_{Q,W}=\HO^{*}(\underline{\mathcal{BPS}}_{Q,W}) , the absolute BPS Lie algebra. It is a complex of  𝐍 Q 0 \mathbf{N}^{Q_{0}} -graded mixed Hodge structures. Its Lie bracket is induced by the injective map  𝔤 ¯ Q , W → H ∗ ⁡ 𝒜 ¯ Q , W ψ \underline{\mathfrak{g}}_{Q,W}\rightarrow\HO^{*}\underline{\mathscr{A}}_{Q,W}^{\psi} .  𝔤 Q , W = H ∗ ⁡ ( ℬ ​ 𝒫 ​ 𝒮 ¯ Q , W ) \mathfrak{g}_{Q,W}=\HO^{*}(\underline{\mathcal{BPS}}_{Q,W}) , the absolute BPS Lie algebra. It is a complex of  𝐍 Q 0 \mathbf{N}^{Q_{0}} -graded vector spaces. Its Lie bracket is induced by the injective map  𝔤 Q , W → H ∗ ⁡ 𝒜 Q , W ψ \mathfrak{g}_{Q,W}\rightarrow\HO^{*}\mathscr{A}_{Q,W}^{\psi}  (page  8.1 ). 
 
 (12)  
 
 ℬ ​ 𝒫 ​ 𝒮 ¯ Π Q , Lie 𝟹 ​ 𝚍 ∈ MHM ⁡ ( ℳ Π Q ) \underline{\mathcal{BPS}}_{\Pi_{Q},\mathrm{Lie}}^{\mathtt{3d}}\in\mathrm{MHM}(\mathcal{M}_{\Pi_{Q}})  the BPS sheaf (without its Lie algebra structure) defined by dimensional reduction applied to the BPS mixed Hodge module of the tripled quiver with its canonical potential  ( Q ~ , W ~ ) (\widetilde{Q},\widetilde{W}) .  ℬ ​ 𝒫 ​ 𝒮 Π Q , Lie 𝟹 ​ 𝚍 ∈ Perv ⁡ ( ℳ Π Q ) \mathcal{BPS}_{\Pi_{Q},\mathrm{Lie}}^{\mathtt{3d}}\in\operatorname{Perv}(\mathcal{M}_{\Pi_{Q}})  the BPS sheaf defined by dimensional reduction applied to the BPS perverse sheaf of the tripled quiver with its canonical potential  ( Q ~ , W ~ ) (\widetilde{Q},\widetilde{W}) , or equivalently,  ℬ ​ 𝒫 ​ 𝒮 Π Q , Lie 𝟹 ​ 𝚍 = 𝐫𝐚𝐭 ⁡ ( ℬ ​ 𝒫 ​ 𝒮 ¯ Π Q , Lie 𝟹 ​ 𝚍 ) \mathcal{BPS}_{\Pi_{Q},\mathrm{Lie}}^{\mathtt{3d}}=\rat(\underline{\mathcal{BPS}}_{\Pi_{Q},\mathrm{Lie}}^{\mathtt{3d}})  (page  8.2 ). 
 
 (13)  
 
 ℬ ​ 𝒫 ​ 𝒮 ¯ Π Q , Lie 𝟹 ​ 𝚍 , ψ ∈ MHM ⁡ ( ℳ Π Q ) \underline{\mathcal{BPS}}_{\Pi_{Q},\mathrm{Lie}}^{\mathtt{3d},\psi}\in\mathrm{MHM}(\mathcal{M}_{\Pi_{Q}})  the relative BPS Lie algebra. This is the mixed Hodge module  ℬ ​ 𝒫 ​ 𝒮 ¯ Π Q , Lie 𝟹 ​ 𝚍 \underline{\mathcal{BPS}}_{\Pi_{Q},\mathrm{Lie}}^{\mathtt{3d}}  on which the Lie algebra structure is induced by the monomorphism  ℬ ​ 𝒫 ​ 𝒮 ¯ Π Q , Lie 𝟹 ​ 𝚍 → ℬ ​ 𝒫 ​ 𝒮 ¯ Π Q , Alg ψ \underline{\mathcal{BPS}}_{\Pi_{Q},\mathrm{Lie}}^{\mathtt{3d}}\rightarrow\underline{\mathcal{BPS}}_{\Pi_{Q},\mathrm{Alg}}^{\psi} .  ℬ ​ 𝒫 ​ 𝒮 Π Q , Lie 𝟹 ​ 𝚍 , ψ ∈ Perv ⁡ ( ℳ Π Q ) \mathcal{BPS}_{\Pi_{Q},\mathrm{Lie}}^{\mathtt{3d},\psi}\in\operatorname{Perv}(\mathcal{M}_{\Pi_{Q}})  the relative BPS Lie algebra. This is the perverse sheaf  ℬ ​ 𝒫 ​ 𝒮 ¯ Π Q , Lie 𝟹 ​ 𝚍 \underline{\mathcal{BPS}}_{\Pi_{Q},\mathrm{Lie}}^{\mathtt{3d}}  on which the Lie algebra structure is induced by the monomorphism  ℬ ​ 𝒫 ​ 𝒮 Π Q , Lie 𝟹 ​ 𝚍 → ℬ ​ 𝒫 ​ 𝒮 Π Q , Alg ψ \mathcal{BPS}_{\Pi_{Q},\mathrm{Lie}}^{\mathtt{3d}}\rightarrow\mathcal{BPS}_{\Pi_{Q},\mathrm{Alg}}^{\psi}  (page  8.2 ). 
 
 
 
 
 
 A.3.  Nilpotent versions 

 
 Let  ı △ : ℳ 𝒜 △ → ℳ 𝒜 \imath_{\triangle}\colon\mathcal{M}_{\mathcal{A}}^{\triangle}\rightarrow\mathcal{M}_{\mathcal{A}}  be the inclusion of a saturated locally closed submonoid. For example,  𝒜 ¯ 𝒜 △ = ı △ ! 𝒜 ¯ 𝒜 \underline{\mathscr{A}}_{\mathcal{A}}^{\triangle}=\imath_{\triangle}^{!}\underline{\mathscr{A}}_{\mathcal{A}} ,  𝒜 𝒜 △ = ı △ ! 𝒜 𝒜 \mathscr{A}_{\mathcal{A}}^{\triangle}=\imath_{\triangle}^{!}\mathscr{A}_{\mathcal{A}} ,  ℬ ​ 𝒫 ​ 𝒮 ¯ 𝒜 , Alg △ = ı △ ! ℬ ​ 𝒫 ​ 𝒮 ¯ 𝒜 , Alg \underline{\mathcal{BPS}}_{\mathcal{A},\mathrm{Alg}}^{\triangle}=\imath_{\triangle}^{!}\underline{\mathcal{BPS}}_{\mathcal{A},\mathrm{Alg}} ,  ℬ 𝒫 𝒮 𝒜 , Alg △ = ı △ ! ℬ 𝒫 𝒮 𝒜 , Alg \mathcal{BPS}_{\mathcal{A},\mathrm{Alg}}^{\triangle}=\imath_{\triangle}^{!}\mathcal{BPS}_{\mathcal{A},\mathrm{Alg}} , and we have the  ψ \psi -twisted versions of these four algebra objects,  𝒜 ¯ 𝒜 △ , ψ \underline{\mathscr{A}}_{\mathcal{A}}^{\triangle,\psi} ,  𝒜 𝒜 △ , ψ \mathscr{A}_{\mathcal{A}}^{\triangle,\psi} ,  ℬ ​ 𝒫 ​ 𝒮 ¯ 𝒜 , Alg △ , ψ \underline{\mathcal{BPS}}_{\mathcal{A},\mathrm{Alg}}^{\triangle,\psi}  and  ℬ ​ 𝒫 ​ 𝒮 𝒜 , Alg △ , ψ \mathcal{BPS}_{\mathcal{A},\mathrm{Alg}}^{\triangle,\psi}  (page  6.1 ). 
 
 
 
 A.4.  Equivariant versions 

 
 We add a superscript  G G  to indicate the  G G -equivariant versions of the objects considered (page  11 ). 
 
 
 
 A.5.  Nakajima quiver varieties and their (BPS) cohomology 

 
 
 (1)  
 
 N ⁡ ( Q , 𝐝 , 𝐟 ) N(Q,\mathbf{d},\mathbf{f})  the smooth Nakajima quiver variety constructed from the doubled quiver  Q ¯ \overline{Q} , with dimension vector  𝐝 \mathbf{d}  and framing dimension vector  𝐟 \mathbf{f}  (page  10.2 ). 
 
 (2)  
 
 N ′ ​ ( Q , 𝐝 , 𝐟 ) N^{\prime}(Q,\mathbf{d},\mathbf{f})  the singular Nakajima quiver variety, constructed by GIT quotient, from the doubled quiver  Q ¯ \overline{Q}  with dimension vector  𝐝 \mathbf{d}  and framing dimension vector  𝐟 \mathbf{f}  (page  10.2 ).
 N △ ​ ( Q , 𝐝 , 𝐟 ) ⊆ N ⁡ ( Q , 𝐝 , 𝐟 ) N^{\triangle}(Q,\mathbf{d},\mathbf{f})\subseteq N(Q,\mathbf{d},\mathbf{f})  is the subscheme of stable representations of  Π Q 𝐟 \Pi_{Q_{\mathbf{f}}}  satisfying the condition  △ \triangle . Similarly, we have  N ′ △ ​ ( Q , 𝐝 , 𝐟 ) ⊆ N ′ ​ ( Q , 𝐝 , 𝐟 ) N^{\prime\triangle}(Q,\mathbf{d},\mathbf{f})\subseteq N^{\prime}(Q,\mathbf{d},\mathbf{f})  (page  10.2 ). 
 
 (3)  
 
 𝕄 𝐟 △ ( Q ) = ⨁ 𝐝 ∈ 𝐍 Q 0 H ∗ ( N △ ( Q , 𝐝 , 𝐟 ) , ı △ ! 𝐐 N ⁡ ( Q , 𝐝 , 𝐟 ) vir ) \mathbb{M}_{\mathbf{f}}^{\triangle}(Q)=\bigoplus_{\mathbf{d}\in\mathbf{N}^{Q_{0}}}\HO^{*}(N^{\triangle}(Q,\mathbf{d},\mathbf{f}),\imath_{\triangle}^{!}\mathbf{Q}^{\mathrm{vir}}_{N(Q,\mathbf{d},\mathbf{f})})  the direct sum of the Borel–Moore homologies of all Nakajima quiver varieties with fixed framing dimension vector (page  10.2 ). 
 
 (4)  
 
 𝕄 𝐟 △ , ψ ​ ( Q ) \mathbb{M}_{\mathbf{f}}^{\triangle,\psi}(Q) , the vector space  𝕄 𝐟 △ ​ ( Q ) \mathbb{M}_{\mathbf{f}}^{\triangle}(Q)  with its structure of  𝔤 Π Q BPS , △ \mathfrak{g}_{\Pi_{Q}}^{\mathtt{\aBPS},\triangle} -module or  𝔫 Π Q BPS , △ , + \mathfrak{n}_{\Pi_{Q}}^{\mathtt{\aBPS},\triangle,+} -module (page  10.2 ).

     Instructions for reporting errors 
     We are continuing to improve HTML versions of papers, and your feedback helps enhance accessibility and mobile
      support. To report errors in the HTML that will help us improve conversion and rendering, choose any of the
      methods listed below: 
     
       Click the "Report Issue"  ( 
             
           )  button, located in the page header. 
     
     Tip:  You can select the relevant text first, to include it in your report. 
     Our team has already identified  the following issues . We appreciate your time reviewing and reporting rendering errors we
      may not have found yet. Your efforts will help us improve the HTML versions for all readers, because disability
      should not be a barrier to accessing research. Thank you for your continued support in championing open access for
      all. 
     Have a free development cycle? Help support accessibility at arXiv! Our collaborators at LaTeXML maintain a  list of packages that need conversion , and welcome  developer contributions .
